New tests, all genuinely executed this session (no Docker/Postgres dependency — a real OS subprocess needs neither): `tests/unit/kernel/workflow_engine/test_sandboxed_tool.py` (7 tests — tier declaration, `SandboxBackedTool` conformance, a real command's result correctly mapped, a non-zero exit code, a real timeout, `inputs` genuinely ignored, output genuinely validating against the tool's own declared schema) and 3 new cases in `test_tool_step_executor.py`, including **the real end-to-end proof this step exists for**: a `WorkflowStep` of type `tool`, resolved through `ToolStepExecutor`, genuinely executes a real OS command through a real `SandboxExecutor` — no mocking anywhere in that chain — plus confirmation that a genuinely sandbox-backed tool's output is still schema-validated, not trusted blindly. Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's approved scope: no `DockerSandbox` backend, no Capability Pack, no Architecture/Build/Test/Documentation Agent, no HTTP route, no `kernel/bootstrap.py` wiring (no real running-Kernel consumer needed to prove the dispatch path — the unit tests are the proof) — and this codebase still has no `tool_invoker` package at all, confirmed again by inspection, a gap recorded rather than filled.

**With a real Tool proven, the third increment builds the pipeline's first named role for real: the Architecture Agent, as the first real, thin Capability Pack.** New `capability_packs/software-engineering/` — a schema-valid `manifest.yaml` (validated for real via the existing `ManifestLoader`, not merely eyeballed), one agent (`architect`). **A real, load-bearing architectural discovery, reported rather than silently built around: the manifest schema's own `allOf` rule requires a top-level `entryPoint` (a real `CapabilityPack` implementation) the moment a manifest declares any agent — and no `CapabilityPack`/`PackContext`/`PackRegistration`/`HealthReport` type exists anywhere in this codebase, only a text diagram in platform_sdk.md §6/§7.** Building platform_sdk.md's *full* documented `PackContext` (fourteen optional service attributes — a Tool Invoker, Event Bus, Workspace Service, Traceability Engine, Quality Gate Registry, Speech Gateway, and more, none of which exist as real services yet) would itself be "invented architecture," not implementation of a real one — so new `ai_os_kernel.capability_manager.pack_contract` implements a **deliberately reduced slice**: `PackContext` (identity only), `PackRegistration` (`agents` only), `HealthReport` (status only), `CapabilityPack` Protocol — the identical "reduced slice now, additive later" precedent already used for `TraceContext`/`ContextRequest`/`ProviderCapabilities`. `SoftwareEngineeringPack` (`pack.py`) is a real, correct implementation of this reduced contract.
**A second real, load-bearing discovery: nothing in this codebase automatically turns a registered pack's manifest into `catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools` rows, and nothing calls a pack's own `CapabilityPack.activate()`.** `SqlPackLifecycleRepository.register()` only ever writes `catalog.packs`/`catalog.pack_state_transitions` — it does not parse `manifest.agents[]` at all. This is a real, distinct, larger future Capability Manager increment (a real pack installer) — not attempted here; this step's own tests seed `catalog.agents`/`catalog.prompts` directly, mirroring the identical precedent `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_registry.py` already established for every other catalog-backed agent/tool row in this codebase's history.
**The step's own named tension — decided and recorded, not silently avoided.** `EntrypointLoader` always constructs zero-argument (`cls()`); a real `PromptedAgent` needs a real, already-built `PromptedCompletionService` (database engine, resolved secret, model/pricing config) with nowhere honest to come from inside a synchronous zero-arg `__init__` — `workflow_engine.prompted_agent`'s own docstring already documents this exact incompatibility and its prior resolution ("used through `InMemoryAgentRegistry`"). Resolution: new `ArchitectureAgentEntrypoint` (`agents/architecture.py`) is genuinely zero-argument-constructible (satisfying `EntrypointLoader`/`SqlAgentRegistry` without modifying either), and **lazily** builds its own real `PromptedAgent` on first `execute()` call (async composition deferred to the one method that can `await`, guarded by a lock so concurrent first calls build exactly one shared instance) — reusing `build_anthropic_prompted_completion_service()` unchanged, the identical real composition `kernel/bootstrap.py` itself already uses for its own demo agent, not a second, divergent way to assemble the same pieces. An optional, defaulted-`None` `service_factory` constructor parameter is the one test seam added — production `EntrypointLoader.load()` never supplies it, so `cls()` still succeeds exactly as required; a test supplies a deterministic (`EchoLLMGateway`-backed) one instead.
**A third discovery, recorded rather than worked around: this pack imports Kernel internals directly.** capability_pack_contract.md's own "Platform Interaction Rules" name the Platform SDK as a pack's only sanctioned interaction surface ("Direct Kernel access is prohibited"), but no `ai-os-sdk` package exists yet (kernel's own `pyproject.toml` lists it "Planned, not yet scaffolded") — there is nothing else honest to depend on for a real LLM Gateway/database connection today. Both `agents/architecture.py`'s own docstring and the pack's own `pyproject.toml` record this as a known, temporary compromise, not a template for future packs to copy uncritically.
**Real workspace scaffolding, not a documentation-only pack.** `capability_packs/software-engineering` is a genuine second `uv` workspace member (own `pyproject.toml`, `ai-os-pack-software-engineering`, depending on `ai-os-kernel` directly per the compromise above) — added to the root `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.uv.workspace]`/`[tool.uv.sources]`/`ruff`/`mypy` configuration, `uv lock` regenerated and `uv sync --frozen --all-packages` verified against both the latest `uv` and the CI-pinned `uv==0.5.11` (mirroring every prior dependency-adding step's own verification discipline). The pack module is genuinely `importlib`-importable in this same environment — verified directly, not assumed: `EntrypointLoader.load(...)` was run by hand against both the agent's and the pack's own entrypoint strings and both constructed real objects satisfying `Agent`/the reduced `CapabilityPack` shape respectively.
**Tests, in three tiers matching what can and cannot be verified this session.** `capability_packs/software-engineering/tests/test_pack.py` (6 tests, genuinely executed, no database, no live LLM call): `activate()` registers the real agent; `deactivate()`/`health()` are real and honest; `ArchitectureAgentEntrypoint()` constructs with zero arguments and no eager I/O; concurrent first `execute()` calls build exactly one shared `PromptedAgent` (a real concurrency proof, not asserted by inspection); **the deterministic Echo-backed end-to-end proof this step's own requirement names** — a real `WorkflowStep` of type `agent`, dispatched through the real `AgentStepExecutor`, genuinely renders a real prompt template and completes it via `EchoLLMGateway`, with no live network call. `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_architecture_agent_pack.py` (new, Kernel-side): a deterministic test registers+activates the pack via the real `SqlPackLifecycleRepository`, seeds a real `catalog.agents` row, and proves `SqlAgentRegistry.resolve_agent()` genuinely resolves this pack's entrypoint — the specific tension this step exists to resolve, provable without a live LLM call; an opt-in live test (skipped without `AIOS_SECRET_LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, mirroring `test_prompted_agent_live.py`) goes one step further, genuinely completing against the live Anthropic API through `AgentStepExecutor`/`SqlAgentRegistry`. **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session, so this new integration test file could not be executed live — verified instead by confirming it collects and imports cleanly under pytest, plus the identical `EntrypointLoader`/`ManifestLoader` manual verification already described above.
**Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's approved scope**: no Build/Test/Documentation Agent, no human-approval gating for a proposed architecture (output capture only), no Tool declared by this pack, no workflow definition, no HTTP route, no `kernel/bootstrap.py` wiring, no automated manifest installer, no LICENSE file, no SDK pack contract test suite (it does not exist anywhere in this codebase to run).

**With a real Architecture Agent and a real sandboxed Tool both proven separately, the fourth increment connects them for the first time: a Build Agent that turns an LLM's own instruction into a real, sandboxed file write.** New `ai_os_pack_software_engineering.agents.build.BuildAgentEntrypoint` — the identical zero-argument-constructible, lazily-self-composing pattern `ArchitectureAgentEntrypoint` already established, reused rather than reinvented: on first `execute()`, it builds a real `PromptedAgent` (the same `build_anthropic_prompted_completion_service()` composition) to get a completion, then parses that completion and genuinely writes the result through a `SandboxedCommandTool`. **A small, additive Sandbox Executor extension was required and made this step, not avoided.** Getting LLM-produced file *content* into a sandboxed write command safely needed a delivery mechanism `SandboxExecutor.execute()` did not have: passing content as a command-line argument risks shell-quoting/argv-length issues, and an environment variable has real OS-level size limits. New optional `stdin: bytes | None = None` parameter on both `SandboxExecutor.execute()` and `SandboxedCommandTool` — defaulted `None` (no behaviour change for any existing caller), delivered concurrently with output draining (not before, avoiding a pipe deadlock for larger content), and, when absent, the child's stdin is `DEVNULL` rather than inherited — the identical "no ambient inheritance" principle already applied to environment variables. **The LLM's own file path is independently verified before any sandbox call — the sandbox itself still cannot be trusted to contain a write, and this steps's own approved framing required the boundary to be real, not bypassed for convenience.** A genuine, non-obvious cross-platform subtlety was found and handled: checking only `Path.is_absolute()` is insufficient, since joining an anchored-but-driveless path (`/etc/passwd` on Windows, which has a root but no drive) onto an existing path silently discards the existing path's own tail rather than raising; the fix resolves the *final* joined path and verifies containment via `is_relative_to()`, which catches every escape form uniformly regardless of its syntactic shape. The file-write command itself is a small, portable Python script (no shell) that reads the content from stdin and writes it — never Kernel/pack Python code touching the filesystem directly, per this step's own explicit constraint. New tests, all genuinely executed this session (no Docker/Postgres — a real OS subprocess needs neither): `tests/unit/kernel/sandbox/test_executor.py` (+3, real stdin delivery, real DEVNULL-default non-inheritance, real best-effort delivery when the command never reads it) and `tests/unit/kernel/workflow_engine/test_sandboxed_tool.py` (+1, stdin passthrough), plus `capability_packs/software-engineering/tests/test_build_agent.py` (12 tests — 18 was this pack's *cumulative* total after this step, 6 pre-existing Architecture Agent tests plus these 12 new ones, not this file's own count; corrected here after this file's own count was verified directly against source during a later audit step) including **the real end-to-end proof this step exists for**: a real `WorkflowStep` of type `agent`, dispatched through the real `AgentStepExecutor`, results in a real file genuinely existing on disk afterward with content traceable to the (Echo-backed, deterministic) completion — plus nested-path handling, lazy working-directory creation, malformed-completion rejection, and every path-escape case rejected. A new Kernel-side integration test (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_build_agent_pack.py`) mirrors `test_architecture_agent_pack.py` exactly: a deterministic tier proving `SqlAgentRegistry` genuinely resolves this agent, and an opt-in live tier (skipped without a real Anthropic key) proving the full chain against a live completion. Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's own approved scope: no Test/Documentation Agent, no automatic Architecture -> Build pipeline (each agent is proven independently, not chained), no `DockerSandbox` backend, no automated manifest installer, no `kernel/bootstrap.py` wiring.

**With Architecture and Build both real, the fifth increment closes the pipeline's third named role: a Test Agent that genuinely runs what Build wrote and reports a real, structured pass/fail — never an LLM's opinion.** New `ai_os_pack_software_engineering.agents.verification.TestAgentEntrypoint` — deliberately **not** `PromptedAgent`-backed, the cleanest possible reading of this step's own constraint ("pass/fail must come from real sandboxed execution outcome, never from LLM judgment"): verifying whether an already-written file's own run command exits zero needs no model call whatsoever, so this agent makes none, simpler even than `ArchitectureAgentEntrypoint`/`BuildAgentEntrypoint` (no `service_factory`, no lazily-built completion service, no lock — zero-argument construction is trivial here, not merely satisfied). **A genuine, discovered gap in how any real `WorkflowStep` could hand this agent its own structured input at all was found and resolved, not silently avoided.** `AgentStepExecutor._invocation_inputs()` only ever populates `promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias`/`context` — there is no per-step mechanism for arbitrary named fields like `workingDirectory`. Architecture and Build both sidestep this because their real data flows through prompt rendering; this agent has no prompt to render. Resolution: this agent's own `_extract_payload()` accepts its three required fields (`workingDirectory`, `filePath`, `runCommand`) either directly in `inputs` (the primary, minimal, structured contract this step's own framing names) **or**, when absent, as a JSON object inside the Context Manager's own assembled `context` — the identical "context is the one real channel for external per-step data" principle Architecture/Build already established, reused with a JSON payload rather than free text, since this agent's input is structured. Not a second, invented input-mapping mechanism — the one that already exists, used the only way it can carry more than one named value.
**A second, real, discovered gap: the manifest schema itself assumes every agent uses a model.** `platform_sdk/schemas/manifest.schema.json`'s `agents[]` entry lists `modelAlias` as unconditionally required, with no escape hatch for an agent that calls no model at all. This module has no authority to change that schema — the manifest still declares a syntactically-valid `modelAlias` purely to satisfy it, with an explicit inline comment (and this module's own docstring) recording that the Test Agent's real implementation never reads or uses it. `permissions`, by contrast, genuinely reflects reality: `sandbox:execute` only, no `llm:invoke`.
**Field names deliberately match `BuildAgentOutput`'s own `workingDirectory`/`filePath`** — this agent is designed to consume a Build Agent result directly, even though no automatic hand-off wires that yet (explicitly out of scope, per this step's own framing). `filePath` is independently checked (resolved, containment-verified, existence-verified) before any sandbox call — the identical defensive discipline `build.py`'s own path validation already established, duplicated rather than shared (the same ADR-0004 "no shared module for a single real caller each" reasoning this pack's own `_build_real_service` duplication already used).
**New tests, all genuinely executed this session (no Docker/Postgres — no LLM call, no network at all):** `capability_packs/software-engineering/tests/test_verification_agent.py` (12 tests) — zero-arg construction; a genuine passing case and a genuine failing case via direct `execute()` (both real subprocess outcomes, not asserted by inspection); a missing file, an escaping path, missing required fields, and a nonexistent working directory each rejected clearly before any sandbox call; **the real end-to-end proof this step exists for, for *both* outcomes** — a real `WorkflowStep` of type `agent`, dispatched through the real `AgentStepExecutor` with a real (fake-resolver-backed) Context Manager, genuinely runs a real file inside the sandbox and reports a genuinely correct pass outcome in one test and a genuinely correct fail outcome in another, proving `passed` tracks the real exit code both ways; a malformed JSON context payload rejected with a clear error; the Pydantic input model constructs validly. A new Kernel-side integration test (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_verification_agent_pack.py`) mirrors the pack's own precedent: a deterministic tier proving `SqlAgentRegistry` genuinely resolves this agent, and — **a genuinely discovered, positive difference from its two pack-mates** — a second tier needing no live provider credentials at all (this agent makes no LLM call), proving a real passing and a real failing case through the `SqlAgentRegistry`-resolved instance directly. Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's own approved scope: no Documentation Agent, no automatic Build -> Test pipeline, no `DockerSandbox` backend, no automated manifest installer, no test-framework auto-discovery, no `kernel/bootstrap.py` wiring.
- `mypy --strict` (266 source files, kernel + this pack together), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **571 passed** (Kernel), **30 passed** (this pack, including the 18 from the previous step), zero regressions. **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session, so `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_verification_agent_pack.py` could not be executed live; verified instead via `pytest --collect-only` plus direct execution of every unit-testable real code path (both dispatch outcomes via `AgentStepExecutor`, the payload extractor, the path validator). A cosmetic, harmless `PytestCollectionWarning` is emitted whenever this agent's classes (`TestAgentEntrypoint`, `TestAgentInput`, `TestAgentOutput`, `TestInstructionError`) are imported into a `test_*.py` file — pytest inspects any `Test`-prefixed name reachable from a test module it already decided to collect, regardless of where that name is actually defined; each is skipped automatically (all declare `__init__`), so nothing is ever mis-collected as a test — a known, accepted naming collision, not renamed, to preserve the `Architecture`/`Build`/`Test` agent-name symmetry this pack's own manifest and code deliberately share.

**With Architecture, Build, and Test all real, the sixth increment completes the pipeline's fourth and, for this phase, final named role: a Documentation Agent that genuinely calls an LLM to record a Build result and its Test outcome, writing a real Markdown file through the same sandbox.** New `ai_os_pack_software_engineering.agents.documentation.DocumentationAgentEntrypoint` — `PromptedAgent`-backed like Architecture/Build (unlike Test, this role genuinely needs a model call: describing what was built and why is exactly the synthesis an LLM is the right tool for), using the identical lazy-build/zero-arg pattern. **Simpler than Build in one respect, deliberately**: this agent's completion needs no parsing at all. Build's own completion must be split into a model-chosen path and content; here, the target path is never the model's to choose — it is derived deterministically as `"<filePath>.md"` from the Build Agent's own `filePath`, written into the same `workingDirectory` Build already wrote into (reused directly, like the Test Agent — this agent creates no private working directory of its own). The model's entire completion is therefore used verbatim as the documentation content; its own prompt (`prompts/documentation_record_artifact.md`) instructs exactly that.
**A second, real use of the Test Agent's own `context`-as-JSON dual-path resolution — extended, not reinvented, to also serve a `PromptedAgent`-backed agent.** This agent's real input is six structured fields (a Build result's `workingDirectory`/`filePath`/`instruction` plus a Test outcome's `passed`/`exitCode`/`output`) — the identical "`AgentStepExecutor` has no per-step mechanism for arbitrary structured fields" gap the Test Agent's own `_extract_payload()` already resolved, reused here verbatim for a six-field payload instead of three. The new piece: once recovered, those fields are placed into a `variables` dict this module builds itself and forwards, alongside the caller's own `promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias`, into the internal `PromptedAgent.execute()` call — `PromptedAgent._build_variables`'s own already-documented `inputs.get("variables")` direct passthrough ("No new input-mapping system") is the seam used, not extended; the original `context` key is popped first, since this module has already consumed it, avoiding a redundant, unused re-flattening.
**`filePath` is independently checked — twice, for two different reasons — before any sandbox call *or* LLM call**, duplicating (not importing) both `verification.py`'s own `_resolve_existing_file` (does the Build Agent's file genuinely exist?) and `build.py`'s own `_resolve_safe_relative_path` (does the derived `.md` target stay contained?) — the identical ADR-0004 "no shared module for a single real caller each" reasoning this pack's every agent module already applies. Checking before the LLM call specifically means a real model call is never spent on an input this agent cannot safely act on regardless of what the model returns — a real ordering bug (validation was initially placed after the lazy service build) was caught by the test suite itself and corrected during this step.
**New tests, all genuinely executed this session (no Docker/Postgres for the deterministic tier):** `capability_packs/software-engineering/tests/test_documentation_agent.py` (12 tests) — zero-arg construction; **the real end-to-end proof this step exists for, for *both* Test outcomes** — a real `WorkflowStep` of type `agent`, dispatched through the real `AgentStepExecutor` with a real (fake-resolver-backed) Context Manager, genuinely writes a real Markdown file recording a genuinely passing case in one test and a genuinely failing case in another, with the written file's content asserted equal to the agent's own returned `content`; a direct-`inputs` call (no Context Manager) proving the dual-path extraction's other branch; a missing file, a nonexistent working directory, missing required fields, and a malformed JSON context payload each rejected clearly, before any LLM call; the path-safety duplicates' own unit tests; the Pydantic input model constructs validly. A new Kernel-side integration test (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_documentation_agent_pack.py`) mirrors the pack's own precedent: a deterministic tier proving `SqlAgentRegistry` genuinely resolves this agent, and — **a genuinely discovered, positive difference from Architecture's/Build's own live tests** — the opt-in live tier uses this pack's own real shipped prompt directly (not a self-contained test-only substitute), since this agent's structured input reaches it via direct `inputs` keys, never requiring a Context Manager round trip the way Build's own `{{context}}`-based prompt does.
Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's own approved scope: no automatic Architecture -> Build -> Test -> Documentation pipeline, no Docker backend, no catalog installer, no new persistence/templating system.
- `mypy --strict` (269 source files, kernel + this pack together), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **553 passed** (Kernel), **42 passed** (this pack, including the 30 from the previous step), **595 passed** combined, zero regressions from this step's own changes. **Note on this run:** one unrelated, pre-existing test (`tests/unit/kernel/llm_gateway/adapters/test_local_adapter.py::test_complete_honours_a_real_retry_after_header`, a timing-sensitive real-clock test untouched by this step) failed once under full-suite load and passed cleanly in isolation immediately after — a pre-existing flake, not a regression introduced here; every test this step actually added passed on every run. **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session, so `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_documentation_agent_pack.py` could not be executed live; verified instead via `pytest --collect-only` (2 tests collect cleanly) plus direct execution of every unit-testable real code path (both dispatch outcomes via `AgentStepExecutor`, the payload extractor, both path validators).

**With all four agents real, the seventh increment closes the one remaining gap named by the reprioritization itself: chaining Architecture → Build → Test → Documentation into one real, declared Workflow Engine pipeline — this project's first real, complete, demonstrable product loop.** The genuinely missing piece, exactly as this step's own approved framing named it, was a step-output-to-next-step-input hand-off: nothing in this codebase had ever taken one step's real, persisted output (`workflow_steps.outputs`, data_model.md §4.3) and turned it into the next step's real input. New `ai_os_kernel.context_manager.resolvers.WorkflowStepOutputResolver` is that piece — a second, Kernel-level, generically reusable resolver for the same "Workflow State" source `context_manager.md` §3 already documents (sibling to the existing `WorkflowStateResolver`, not a new source category; §5's own `required_context_types` example list already names `previous_outputs` explicitly). Configured with a flat, statically-declared `{consuming_step_id: source_step_id(s)}` mapping — "this step's input includes the named output of step X," the smallest data structure that says exactly that — plus two small, optional, per-step knobs: `field_selectors` (extract one named field verbatim, for a consumer wanting free text) and `output_transforms` (a real, narrow, reviewed Python callable applied before encoding — the one deliberate exception this pipeline needs, not a workflow-author-facing expression language). This deliberately keeps every cross-step reference entirely inside the Context Manager/composition layer, never as a new field on `WorkflowStep` itself — `workflow_architecture.md`'s own Step Contract section is explicit that its five invocation fields are "never ... a cross-step reference," and its own Context Management section already names "Previous workflow state" as a sanctioned context source without further specifying how — this resolver is that "how," honouring the letter and the spirit of both.
**A real pack-owned workflow file, not a Python-constructed `WorkflowDefinition` — the first genuine consumer of the already-real, previously-unused `WorkflowDefinitionLoader` in this codebase's own history.** `capability_packs/software-engineering/workflows/delivery_pipeline.yaml` declares all four steps in order, each naming a real, pack-qualified `agentId`; `ai_os_pack_software_engineering.pipeline` owns the pipeline-specific configuration of the generic resolver above (which step reads which prior step's output, and the one narrow transform: deriving `runCommand = [sys.executable, filePath]` for the Test step, since the Build Agent's own already-shipped contract has no reason to decide how its own file is run) and a `build_pipeline_trigger()` function mirroring `kernel/bootstrap.py`'s own `_build_workflow_trigger` shape exactly (create → start → run-to-completion), living in the pack rather than the Kernel specifically because every step here needs the pack's own agents, not the Kernel's unrelated demo one.
**A real, previously-undetected bug was found and fixed by this step's own manual end-to-end verification, not by inspection.** `WorkflowStateResolver` has no per-step concept of its own — it unconditionally contributes the workflow instance's own top-level `inputs` to *every* agent step's assembled context, correct and unchanged behaviour for every prior, single-agent-step caller. Combined naively with the new resolver in one `DefaultContextManager`, this corrupted the clean single-JSON/single-text payload build/test/documentation each expect (two JSON objects, or two texts, concatenated by `DefaultContextManager`'s own `"\n\n".join`) — a `json.JSONDecodeError`/malformed-Python-source failure only surfaced by actually running the four-step chain end to end. Fixed with `_StepScopedResolver`, a small, pipeline-owned wrapper restricting `WorkflowStateResolver`'s own contribution to the `architecture` step alone — `WorkflowStateResolver` itself needed no change at all.
**Docker Desktop was unavailable yet again this session — the real end-to-end proof therefore could not run through `pytest`/testcontainers.** Both tiers of `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_delivery_pipeline.py` need a real Postgres container, a genuinely discovered fact about *this specific test* (every prior single-agent test in this pack could stay deterministic without one — this one's job is to prove a step's real, *persisted* output reaches the next step, which only exists once the real `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository` has actually written it). In its place, a manual, ad-hoc end-to-end trace was run this session against a fake, in-memory `WorkflowInstanceRepository` stand-in, driving the real `AgentStepExecutor` + the real `WorkflowStepOutputResolver` + all four real, unmodified pack agents through the exact same code paths production would use — this is what caught the bug above, and confirmed, after the fix, a real design proposal genuinely reaching a real generated file (readable back from the Build step's own persisted `instruction` field), a real Test Agent genuinely running that file and reporting a real `passed: true, exitCode: 0`, and a real Documentation Agent genuinely recording that real outcome to a real Markdown file on disk. This is not a substitute for the real Postgres-backed integration test (which remains unexecuted this session, and is the single highest-priority re-verification item once Docker Desktop is available), but it is a materially stronger confidence check than `--collect-only` alone.
**New tests:** `tests/unit/kernel/context_manager/test_resolvers.py` (+8 — `WorkflowStepOutputResolver`'s own whole-object JSON encoding, single-field extraction, multi-source merge with declared collision order, transform application, absent-from-mapping/not-yet-completed/partially-ready no-op cases, and highest-attempt selection) and `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_delivery_pipeline.py` (2 — a deterministic full-chain proof and an opt-in live one, neither executed this session, see above). **A genuinely discovered, positive difference for the opt-in live tier**: unlike every prior single-agent live test in this pack (each needed a self-contained, test-only substitute prompt, specifically because their own `{{context}}` placeholder needed a real Context Manager/workflow-instance round trip no single-agent test stood up), this test's own live tier uses all three real, shipped prompts directly — this pipeline finally *is* that round trip, for real, end to end.
Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's own approved scope: no human-approval gating between steps, no `DockerSandbox` backend, no automated manifest → catalog installer, no new HTTP route (reuses the existing `build_pipeline_trigger`-style closure pattern, unrouted, exactly like `kernel/bootstrap.py`'s own demo trigger before it), no second pipeline or general-purpose chaining beyond this one, fixed, four-step definition.
- `mypy --strict` (273 source files, kernel + this pack together), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **603 passed** (553 Kernel + 8 new resolver tests + 42 pre-existing pack tests), zero regressions. **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session — see above for how this step's own real end-to-end proof was verified in its place.

**With a real, complete product loop now genuinely running LLM-generated code, the eighth increment closes the single most-repeated, most-explicitly-flagged risk in this entire document: a real ADR-0016 Tier 1 sandbox backend.** New `ai_os_kernel.sandbox.docker_executor.DockerSandbox` implements the existing `SandboxExecutor` Protocol unchanged — no caller (`SandboxedCommandTool`, any of the four pack agents) needs to change to use it. Every control ADR-0016/security_architecture.md §5.1 names for Tier 1 is genuinely requested from the Docker Engine: `network_mode="none"` (no network interface beyond loopback); `read_only=True` root filesystem with exactly one bind-mounted, read-write working directory and one `tmpfs` scratch mount (no other host path is ever mounted, and there is no parameter to add one); a non-root `user` (default `65534:65534`); `cap_drop=["ALL"]` and `security_opt=["no-new-privileges"]`; and real `mem_limit`/`nano_cpus`/`pids_limit` resource ceilings — genuine cgroup-level enforcement, unlike `LocalSubprocessSandbox`'s own documented absence of these. `SandboxGuarantees` for this backend reports all five fields `True` — a materially fuller matrix than `LocalSubprocessSandbox`'s honest 3/5, exactly this step's own stated point. Every container is created fresh and explicitly removed in a `finally` block — never reused across calls.
**"Docker not available" is a clear, typed error, verified for real this session** (the daemon genuinely was unavailable): `DockerSandbox()` itself does zero I/O (the identical synchronous, no-I/O constructor discipline every lazily-built component in this codebase already follows); the underlying `docker.DockerClient` is built and pinged only on the first `execute()` call, and any failure — no daemon, no socket/named pipe, a malformed `DOCKER_HOST` — is caught and re-raised as `DockerSandboxUnavailableError`, never a raw `docker`/`requests` exception. Confirmed directly this session against this environment's own genuinely-unavailable Docker daemon.
**A genuine, discovered, load-bearing limitation this step's own approved scope explicitly does not extend to fixing — recorded, not silently patched.** This pack's own agents construct `command` sequences using `sys.executable` — the *host's own* absolute interpreter path. That path does not exist inside `DockerSandbox`'s own container filesystem (a minimal, different image), so running those exact commands unmodified against this new backend today would fail with "no such file or directory" — not because the backend is broken, but because a host-specific interpreter path is not a portable command. This step's own approved framing explicitly forbids changing any agent/pack code to address this; wiring `DockerSandbox` in as those agents' own default, and resolving how a portable interpreter invocation reaches a container image, is the necessary, explicitly out-of-scope follow-up (§6). This class's own tests instead use commands portable across host and container by construction (`python3`/`sh`/`cat`/`id` resolved via the container image's own `PATH`).
**Docker (docker-py) is now a real, declared dependency of `ai-os-kernel`**, not merely an effective transitive one via `testcontainers` (a dev-only dependency) — `kernel/pyproject.toml` gained `docker>=7.1,<8.0`; `uv.lock` regenerated and re-synced. `docker` ships no inline types and no `py.typed` marker and no maintained stub package exists — root `pyproject.toml` gained one new, narrowly-scoped `[[tool.mypy.overrides]]` (`module = "docker.*"`, `ignore_missing_imports = true`), the identical "declare the gap, do not silently suppress project-wide" treatment every other per-module mypy carve-out in this file already uses.
**A genuine, deliberate first departure from this project's own "real fakes, never mocks" testing convention — recorded, not silently introduced.** Every prior test in this codebase prefers a real, Protocol-conformant fake over a mock (ADR-0004/ADR-0015). That does not transfer to `docker`'s own third-party client/container classes, which this project does not own and cannot meaningfully re-implement as an in-process fake. `tests/unit/kernel/sandbox/test_docker_executor.py` (13 tests) therefore uses `unittest.mock` — the stdlib, no new dependency — scoped *only* to proving this backend's own Python code constructs the right Docker API calls with the right arguments (every Tier 1 control listed above, individually asserted against the real `containers.create()` call's own kwargs), never that Docker itself enforces them. That second, more important proof is `tests/integration/sandbox/test_docker_sandbox_live.py` (7 tests, real, unmocked, genuinely exercising a real daemon when available) — network isolation (a real socket-connect attempt from inside a real container, expected to fail), filesystem containment (a real write inside the mounted directory succeeds and is visible on the host; a real write anywhere else fails with "Read-only file system"), non-root identity, timeout-triggered kill, guaranteed container removal, and real stdin delivery (the single most platform/version-sensitive mechanism in this backend, using `docker`'s own raw `attach_socket` API).
**Docker Desktop was unavailable yet again this session — the real, unmocked proof of network isolation and filesystem containment therefore could not run.** All 7 tests in `tests/integration/sandbox/test_docker_sandbox_live.py` collect cleanly and skip cleanly, with a clear reason, via a `docker_available` fixture that pings the daemon and calls `pytest.skip()` on failure — mirroring this project's own established opt-in-live pattern (ADR-0015) exactly, just gated on daemon reachability instead of an API key. **A genuine, positive fact worth recording**: GitHub Actions' own standard runners ship Docker pre-installed and running by default, and `ci.yml`'s own `integration` job already runs `pytest tests/unit tests/integration -v` — this suite should therefore run for real, unskipped, the next time CI executes, even though it could not run locally this session.
**Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's own approved scope**: no agent/pack/Workflow Engine code changed; `LocalSubprocessSandbox` untouched and still the default everywhere it already was; no wiring of `DockerSandbox` in as any agent's own default (a small, separate decision, recorded here as explicitly deferred — see the `sys.executable`-portability limitation above, which must be resolved first); no network-isolation escape hatch or per-tool override of any kind; no digest-pinning of the default image (`python:3.12-slim`, tag-pinned only) — recorded as a real, later hardening step, the identical "documented hardening path, not a redesign" treatment ADR-0016 itself already gives gVisor.
- `mypy --strict` (276 source files) clean, `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean. Unit suite: **616 passed** (603 previous + 13 new `DockerSandbox` unit tests), zero regressions. **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session — the 7-test live integration suite collects and skips cleanly (see above); re-running it for real is the single highest-priority re-verification item once Docker Desktop is available, alongside the still-unexecuted `test_delivery_pipeline.py` from the previous step.

**This step closed the health audit's own documentation-drift findings — a reconciliation step, not a new increment.** The agent/workflow naming fork was resolved by conforming code to the Approved, authoritative docs: `architect` → `architecture` and `test` → `qa-test`, renamed everywhere (manifest, `pack.py`'s own `PackRegistration`, both pipeline YAML `agents[]`/`agentId` fields, every pack-level and Kernel-level test's own `_AGENT_ID`/`_AGENT_IDS`) — a mechanical, complete rename verified to introduce zero behaviour change both by the full existing test suite passing unmodified in substance and by re-running the manual end-to-end pipeline trace with the new ids, producing byte-for-byte identical output. Workflow-local step ids (`steps[].id: "test"` inside `delivery_pipeline.yaml`) were deliberately left unchanged — they are this workflow's own naming, independent of the agent catalog. `build` had no genuine fit among `agents.md`'s own 15 pre-existing documented ids (`backend-developer`'s own "Backend services and APIs" scope is narrower and stack-specific; this agent is a generic, stack-agnostic single-file writer) — rather than force-fit or silently leave it undocumented, `agents.md`'s own Agent Catalog gained a new, honestly-scoped `build` entry. The workflow was checked directly against `workflows.md` §4's own `se.implement_task` (the document's own named "intended thin slice") and found to genuinely diverge — different shape (no tools, decisions, loops, or quality gates), different purpose (a standalone proof vs. a `foreach`-invoked sub-workflow), different agents (no `code-reviewer`, but includes `architecture`/`documentation` steps `se.implement_task` deliberately does not) — so `se.delivery_pipeline` was kept as its own id and given its own real, honest section in `workflows.md`, cross-referenced from a new "Currently Implemented Subset" section in both `agents.md` and `workflows.md` naming exactly which agents/workflows are real today (4 of 16 agents; 1 of 7 workflows).
**Every other doc-drift gap the audit named was closed the same session**: `context_manager.md` §4 and `workflow_architecture.md`'s own Context Management section now both record `WorkflowStepOutputResolver` as a second real "Workflow State" resolver; `capability_pack_contract.md` carries a dated (2026-07-28), explicit exception note for the `software-engineering` pack's real, live direct-Kernel-import compromise (why, and what removes it — a real `ai-os-sdk`); `manifest_schema.md` gained a new numbered validation rule (renumbering the rest) and `platform_sdk/schemas/manifest.schema.json` itself gained a real, tested `if`/`then` conditional (`modelAlias` required only when `permissions` contains `llm:invoke`) — verified directly against the schema (a `permissions: [llm:invoke]` agent without `modelAlias` now correctly fails; one without `llm:invoke` and without `modelAlias` now correctly passes) — and the QA/Test Agent's own manifest entry no longer declares the now-unnecessary decoy `modelAlias` value, with `agents/verification.py`'s own docstring updated to describe the gap as resolved rather than open; `llm_gateway.md` §15's dead reference to a non-existent `platform_sdk/contracts/llm.py` now correctly points at the real files that actually govern the Gateway's concrete signatures; ADR-0016 gained an "Implementation naming note" (an additive callout, not a rewrite of the Accepted decision text — this codebase's ADR process has no "amend in place" path) and `security_architecture.md` §5.1 cross-references it, both recording that the real Kernel implementation is named `SandboxExecutor`, not `SandboxRuntime`.
**The Postgres/testcontainers fixture fix**: a new, shared `tests/integration/_postgres_fixture.py` (`postgres_container()`, a context manager wrapping `PostgresContainer` construction) replaces the identical, independently-repeated `with PostgresContainer("pgvector/pgvector:pg16", driver="asyncpg") as postgres:` line in all ~30 of this tree's own `database_url` fixtures — the one real, current, thirty-times-repeated case ADR-0004 itself says justifies a shared helper, unlike the "one real caller each" reasoning this codebase applies everywhere else. A genuinely unreachable daemon now raises a clean `pytest.skip()` with a clear reason instead of a raw `docker.errors.DockerException` propagating out of `testcontainers` — verified directly, twice: once by forcing `DOCKER_HOST` to a nonexistent pipe and confirming a real test file skips cleanly (16 skipped, ~2 seconds, clear reason shown via `-rs`), and once by running the entire real suite successfully with Docker genuinely available (see below).
**Docker Desktop became available mid-step — the single highest-priority re-verification item in this document for the last several increments — and taking advantage of it immediately paid for itself twice over.** The first real run of `tests/integration/sandbox/test_docker_sandbox_live.py` found a real, previously-undetected bug: the Docker Engine API's own `containers.create()` does not implicitly pull a missing image the way `docker run` does, so a fresh host without `python:3.12-slim` already pulled failed outright — fixed by pulling explicitly and retrying once on `ImageNotFound`. The second run then found a **second, deeper, genuinely interesting bug**: every stdin-delivery test hung until the wall-clock timeout. Direct, live debugging (not guesswork) traced this to a real Windows/npipe-specific behaviour: `docker.transport.npipesocket.NpipeSocket.shutdown()` is implemented as a full `close()`, not a half-close, and — confirmed by inspecting `docker.types.containers.ContainerConfig` — this backend's own `detach=True` (used because this class always manages containers asynchronously itself regardless of this flag) meant the container's own `StdinOnce`/`AttachStdin` config fields were never set `True`, so the daemon had no configured way to treat the attach socket's closure as stdin EOF. **`detach=False` fixes it** (verified directly: `cat`, previously hanging indefinitely, now exits cleanly the instant its stdin closes) and has no other effect on this class's own control flow, which never relies on docker-py's own blocking/attached-run behaviour either way. A regression-locking assertion (`kwargs["detach"] is False`) was added to the existing mocked unit test so this cannot silently regress.
**With both bugs fixed, all 7 real, unmocked `DockerSandbox` guarantee tests pass against a genuine daemon**: network isolation (a real in-container socket-connect attempt genuinely fails), filesystem containment both directions (a real write inside the mounted directory succeeds and is visible on the host; a real write anywhere else on the read-only root genuinely fails with "Read-only file system"), non-root identity, a real timeout-triggered kill, genuine container removal (checked against the real Docker Engine's own container list, not merely trusted), and — now genuinely — real stdin delivery. **A full real run of the entire integration suite** (`pytest tests/integration`, not `--collect-only`, not a manual trace) reports **218 passed, 10 skipped (all correctly gated on a missing live Anthropic API key), 0 failed** — every Postgres-backed test this project has accumulated across its entire history, including `test_delivery_pipeline.py`'s own deterministic full-chain test, genuinely executed and genuinely green for the first time. Every container this session's own test runs created was confirmed cleanly removed afterward (checked directly against `docker ps -a`); only this development machine's own pre-existing, unrelated containers remain.
- `mypy --strict` (277 source files) clean, `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean. Unit suite: **616 passed** (unchanged — this step's own code changes were renames, a doc-driven schema fix, two real bug fixes in `docker_executor.py`, and one new shared test fixture module, not new unit-testable surface beyond the one regression assertion added). **Full integration suite, genuinely executed for the first time this project's history: 218 passed, 10 skipped, 0 failed.** One unrelated, pre-existing, timing-sensitive test (`test_multi_provider_routing.py::test_one_router_and_dispatcher_genuinely_reach_both_real_provider_adapters`) flaked once under full-suite load and passed cleanly in isolation — the same class of flake already recorded for `test_local_adapter.py` in an earlier step, not caused by this one.

- FastAPI app skeleton with `/api/v1/health/live`, `/api/v1/health/ready`, `/api/v1/version`
- 17 empty component subpackages under `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/` (docstring-only stubs awaiting their stage)

### Stage A – Step 2: Configuration, Observability, Manifest Loader, Health, Template Pack
- **Configuration Manager** (`configuration_manager/`): layered precedence — built-in defaults → `config/platform.yaml` → `infra/environments/<env>.yaml`; `env`/`role` always bootstrap-supplied, never file-driven; `PlatformConfig` frozen/immutable. Only precedence layers 1, 3, 4 implemented (no pack defaults, runtime/experiment overrides, or secrets layer yet — those are later-stage work).
- **Observability skeleton** (`observability/`): `structlog` JSON logging, `TraceIdMiddleware` (generates or honors `X-Trace-Id`, binds via `contextvars`). This is a logging/trace-id skeleton only — **no actual OpenTelemetry SDK wiring or OTLP export yet.**
- **Manifest Loader** (`manifest_loader/`): discovery + JSON Schema validation against `platform_sdk/schemas/manifest.schema.json`; `load_one()` (strict) vs `scan()` (resilient, discovered/failed split).
- **Health & Lifecycle** (`health/`): `HealthService` aggregates pluggable component checks into a real `ReadinessReport` (ready/degraded); wired to configuration and manifest-loader checks.
- **`capability_packs/_template/`**: minimal schema-valid manifest, proven discoverable end-to-end through the live readiness endpoint.
- 29 unit tests added (`tests/unit/kernel/{configuration_manager,manifest_loader,health,entrypoints}/`); `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean; verified against a real running `uvicorn` process.

### Stage B – Persistence Foundation (schema + connectivity only)
- **`infra/docker-compose.yml`**: Postgres 16 (`pgvector/pgvector:pg16`) + Redis 7 (`redis:7-alpine`) for local development, env-driven credentials with local-dev-only defaults, no api/worker services yet (no image exists).
- **`kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/persistence/`**: `DatabaseSettings` (`AIOS_DATABASE_URL`, env-only per configuration_management.md §3.3 — never a YAML value); `build_engine()` — SQLAlchemy 2.0 async Core engine (asyncpg), no ORM (ADR-0011); `schema.py` — Core `Table` definitions for `workflow_instances`, `workflow_events`, `workflow_leases`, column-for-column against `docs/08_database/data_model.md` §4.1/4.2/4.4.
- **Alembic**: root `alembic.ini` (`script_location = kernel/alembic`, no URL committed), async `env.py` reading `AIOS_DATABASE_URL` at run time, one baseline migration (`0001_workflow_state_baseline`) creating the `workflow` schema and its three tables, indexes, `CHECK` constraint on `status`, and the `UNIQUE (workflow_id, seq)` constraint.
- Two documented details deliberately deferred, not silently dropped (see `persistence/schema.py` docstring): the `workflow_events` UPDATE/DELETE revocation for "the application role" (no such DB role exists yet — no auth/role work has landed) and foreign keys to `catalog.workflow_definitions` / `evaluation.experiments` / `evaluation.run_manifests` (those schemas don't exist yet). Columns themselves are present exactly as documented.
- 4 new integration tests (`tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`), real Postgres via `testcontainers` per `test_strategy.md` §2/§3.3 (ADR-0015 — no mocking the database): migration up → down → up round trip, documented-column/constraint verification, and two behavioral proofs (`UNIQUE (workflow_id, seq)` and the `workflow_leases → workflow_instances` foreign key both actually reject bad inserts).
- Verified twice: once via `pytest` against an ephemeral `testcontainers` Postgres, and once by hand against the committed `infra/docker-compose.yml` Postgres (`alembic upgrade head` / `downgrade -1` / `upgrade head`, `\dt workflow.*` confirming all three tables).
- `mypy --strict` (now also covering `kernel/alembic`), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Total suite: 33 tests passing (29 unit + 4 integration).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: Definition Loading and Validation
- **`kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/`**: `WorkflowDefinitionLoader.load(path)` reads one workflow definition YAML file and returns a validated, in-memory `WorkflowDefinition` — field-for-field against the Workflow Contract (`docs/03_architecture/workflow/workflow_architecture.md`), the Human Approval Point Contract (`human_approval_points.md` §4), and the concurrency rule in `workflow_engine.md` §7.1. Fails clearly (`WorkflowDefinitionError`) on a missing file, invalid YAML, a non-mapping document, or any structural violation — never a bare stack trace.
- Validates: required fields (`id`, `name`, `description`, `version`, `inputs`, `outputs`, `steps`, `failureHandling`); `id`/`version` patterns mirroring `platform_sdk/schemas/manifest.schema.json`'s `workflows[]` entry; `inputs`/`outputs` are well-formed JSON Schema documents (via the already-used `jsonschema` library); `steps` non-empty with unique `id`s; a `parallel` step must declare `joinPolicy` (`all`/`any`/`collect`) — explicitly mandatory, "fails validation rather than defaulting silently"; `qualityGates` entries are well-formed gate-id references; `humanApprovalPoints` entries satisfy the full Human Approval Point Contract (`context`, non-empty `options`, etc.); `retryPolicy`, when declared, must bound both attempts and duration ("Retries must be bounded (maximum attempts + maximum duration)" — `error_handling_retry.md` §4); unrecognised fields are rejected (`extra="forbid"`), matching the Manifest Loader's strictness.
- **Deliberately deferred, not invented:** per-step-type fields beyond `id`/`type` (agent/tool references, decision branching, sub-workflow linkage, quality-gate-step/human-approval-step payloads) — no document defines a field-level contract for these yet. Recorded in the module docstring so a future step doesn't mistake the gap for an oversight.
- 17 unit tests (`tests/unit/kernel/workflow_engine/test_loader.py`): one valid minimal definition, one with a full `humanApprovalPoints` entry and `retryPolicy`, and 15 distinct invalid-input cases (missing file, bad YAML, non-mapping, missing required field, unknown field, bad `id`/`version`, empty `steps`, duplicate step ids, `parallel` without `joinPolicy`, malformed gate reference, empty `failureHandling`, `humanApprovalPoints` without options, unbounded `retryPolicy`, malformed `inputs` schema).
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full unit suite: 46 tests passing.

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: Instance Creation Only
- **`kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/ids.py`**: `new_workflow_id()`/`new_event_id()` — prefixed ULIDs (`wf_…`, `evt_…`) per data_model.md §2, using `python-ulid`.
- **`input_validation.py`**: `validate_inputs()` checks candidate inputs against the definition's already-validated `inputs` JSON Schema (via `jsonschema.Draft202012Validator`); `validate_principal()` rejects a blank `principal_id` — `workflow_instances.principal_id` is `NOT NULL` and an empty string would satisfy that constraint while meaning nothing. Both raise `WorkflowInputValidationError`.
- **`instance.py`**: `WorkflowInstance` (frozen) and `WorkflowInstanceStatus` (the canonical nine-state enum, workflow_engine.md §7) — the in-memory shape of one `workflow_instances` row.
- **`repository.py`**: `WorkflowInstanceRepository` (`Protocol`) and its only implementation, `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository` — writes the `workflow_instances` row (`status = created`, `last_event_seq = 1`) and the first `workflow_events` row (`workflow.started`, `seq = 1`) inside one `engine.begin()` transaction, returning the instance read back via `RETURNING`. Failures are wrapped in `WorkflowInstanceCreationError`.
- **`service.py`**: `WorkflowInstanceService.create_instance()` — validates (principal, then inputs), then delegates to the injected repository. No DI container (ADR-0010): the concrete repository is constructed and handed in by the caller.
- **Deliberate design note:** `workflow_events.occurred_at` is application-supplied (`datetime.now(UTC)`), not database-generated — a departure from data_model.md §2's blanket "TIMESTAMPTZ … database-generated" convention, reasoned explicitly in `repository.py`: an event's occurrence time is a fact the application knows and the database commit clock does not. `created_at`/`updated_at` on `workflow_instances` remain database-generated, unchanged.
- 9 new unit tests (input validation + a fake-repository service test proving validation runs *before* any repository call) and 3 new integration tests against real Postgres (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_instance_creation.py`): the repository writes both rows correctly; the service produces the same result end-to-end through a real `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository`; and — the key proof — a deliberately-broken second insert inside one `engine.begin()` block leaves **no** trace of the first insert, demonstrating the one-transaction guarantee ADR-0011 requires.
- No FastAPI route added. This step built a callable service, not an HTTP surface — nothing currently asked for one, and adding one would have been scope beyond what was approved.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite at this point: 62 tests passing (55 unit + 7 integration: 4 persistence + 3 workflow_engine).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: State Transition (`created` → `running`)
- **`repository.py`**: `WorkflowInstanceRepository.transition_to_running()` and its `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository` implementation. The guard ("only `created` → `running`") is the `UPDATE workflow_instances ... WHERE status = 'created'` clause itself — one atomic statement, no separate read-then-write race window. `last_event_seq` is incremented via `last_event_seq + 1` in the same statement rather than a hardcoded `2`, so it does not assume this is always the second event. On no matching row, raises `WorkflowInvalidTransitionError` with a message distinguishing "instance does not exist" from "instance exists but is in status X, not created" (a follow-up `SELECT` on the failure path only).
- The `state.transitioned` event payload carries exactly the four things workflow_engine.md §7 says every transition event must carry: `previousStatus`, `newStatus`, `reason`, `triggeringEventId` (nullable — no leasing/trigger producer exists yet, so it is always `None` at this stage, not invented content).
- **`input_validation.py`**: added `validate_reason()` (reused the existing blank-check helper — `validate_principal` and `validate_reason` now both call a shared `_require_non_blank`).
- **`service.py`**: `WorkflowInstanceService.start(workflow_id, reason, triggering_event_id=None)` — validates `reason`, then delegates. No status check duplicated at the service layer; the repository's guard is the single source of truth.
- **`errors.py`**: added `WorkflowInvalidTransitionError` (guard rejection — distinct from `WorkflowInstanceCreationError`, which now also covers a transition write that fails unexpectedly).
- 6 new unit tests (`test_service.py`): `start()` delegates correctly, passes through an optional `triggering_event_id`, and rejects a blank `reason` before the repository is ever called.
- 4 new integration tests (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_state_transitions.py`) against real Postgres: the happy path (status becomes `running`, `last_event_seq` becomes 2, the event payload has the right shape); a second transition attempt on an already-`running` instance is rejected with no new event written; a transition on a non-existent instance is rejected; and — the atomicity proof — a pre-inserted conflicting event forces the transition's own event insert to fail *after* its guarded `UPDATE` already matched a row, and the instance is confirmed to still read `status = created`, `last_event_seq = 1` afterward, proving the "succeeded" update was rolled back.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite at this point: 69 tests passing (58 unit + 11 integration).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: First-Step Execution (No-Op Placeholder) — superseded, see next section
- **`step_executor.py`** (new): `StepExecutor` (`Protocol`) — the seam a real Agent/Tool/Decision/... executor substitutes into later; `NoOpStepExecutor` — the only implementation right now, always succeeds immediately with empty outputs. Real agent invocation, real tool invocation, and LLM calls remain out of scope. (This module is unchanged by the generalization below.)
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean at the time. Full suite at this point: 78 tests passing (61 unit + 17 integration).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: Multi-Step Progression and Completion
- **`repository.py`**: the first-step-only `complete_first_step_noop()` is **replaced** by `WorkflowInstanceRepository.advance_workflow()` (plus a new plain-read `get_instance()`), which generalises "execute the first step" into "advance by exactly one step, or complete if none remain." The guard is the same atomic `UPDATE ... WHERE` pattern, now comparing `current_step_id` against a caller-supplied `expected_current_step_id` (`None` for "no step yet," or a specific step id) instead of hardcoding `IS NULL` — the same check-and-write-in-one-statement design, generalised rather than duplicated. When `next_step` is given: appends `step.started`/`step.completed`, `last_event_seq += 2`. When `next_step` is `None` (no step left in the definition): appends `workflow.completed` — a documented event-type example in data_model.md §4.2 — and sets `status = completed`, `completed_at = now()`, `last_event_seq += 1`. `WorkflowInvalidTransitionError` distinguishes "does not exist" / "not running" / "wrong definition" / "current step is X, not the expected Y — it may have already advanced."
- The `workflow.completed` payload is deliberately `{}` — no document specifies what fields it should carry (unlike `step.started`/`step.completed`, which trace to `workflow_steps` columns), so nothing was invented. `step.started`/`step.completed` payloads are unchanged from the previous step.
- **`service.py`**: `execute_first_step()` is **replaced** by `WorkflowInstanceService.advance(workflow_id, definition)`. It reads the instance (`get_instance`) to learn `current_step_id`, resolves the next step by position in `definition.steps` (`None` → `steps[0]`; found → the following entry or `None` if last; not found → a clear rejection — the instance's current step doesn't belong to this definition), runs the executor if there is a next step, then delegates the write. One call advances by one step; a caller (test, or later an orchestrator) drives repeated calls to reach completion. No internal loop, no scheduler — still out of scope.
- 6 new/changed unit tests (`test_service.py`, generalized fake repository): first-step resolution when `current_step_id` is `None`; second-step resolution after the first; completion after the final step; rejection when the instance doesn't exist; rejection when `current_step_id` doesn't match any declared step; rejection when the definition has no steps (unchanged case, preserved through the rewrite).
- 5 new integration tests, replacing the superseded first-step-only file (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_step_progression.py`) against real Postgres: full progression first → second → `workflow.completed` in one continuous flow (asserting the exact 7-event sequence and `step_id`/`payload` shapes); rejected when not `running`; rejected when already `completed`; rejected on a stale `expected_current_step_id` (a caller acting on data read before another advance happened); and the atomicity proof — a pre-inserted conflicting event forces the `workflow.completed` insert to fail *after* the guarded `UPDATE` already matched a row, and the instance is confirmed to still read `status = running`, `completed_at = NULL`, `last_event_seq` unchanged afterward.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite at this point: 80 tests passing (64 unit + 16 integration).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: Minimal Agent Contract and Real Trivial Agent Invocation
- **`agent.py`** (new): `Agent` (`Protocol`) — `output_schema: dict[str, Any]` plus `async def execute(inputs) -> outputs`. A deliberately reduced slice of the full Agent Contract (`docs/03_architecture/agents/agent_architecture.md`): no `AgentRequest`/context/security-context/budget/deadline, no Prompt Registry, no LLM Gateway, no Tool Invoker — all separate, not-yet-built Kernel components. `input_schema` is deliberately **not** included: no per-step input-mapping mechanism exists yet (that is the Context Manager's job), so there is nothing real to validate against — adding it now would be a half-finished field. `EchoAgent` is the one trivial in-process implementation, declaring and satisfying a minimal `output_schema`.
- **`step_executor.py`**: `AgentStepExecutor` invokes the injected `Agent` with `{}` (no per-step inputs yet) and validates the returned output against `agent.output_schema` via `jsonschema.Draft202012Validator` — the same library already used everywhere else in this codebase, no new validation approach introduced. Raises `AgentOutputValidationError` (new, in `errors.py`) with every violation listed, clearly. Guards against being handed a non-Agent step (`ValueError` — an internal wiring bug, not a runtime condition, so not part of the platform's structured-error taxonomy). `DispatchingStepExecutor` routes an Agent-type step to `AgentStepExecutor` and every other step type to `NoOpStepExecutor` — the only place that knows both exist; neither executor needs to know about step types it doesn't handle.
- **No changes to `service.py` or `repository.py`.** `WorkflowInstanceService` already took any `StepExecutor` — wiring `DispatchingStepExecutor(AgentStepExecutor(EchoAgent()), NoOpStepExecutor())` happens entirely at the call site (composition root / tests), proving the Protocol boundary from the previous step was genuinely sufficient.
- 5 new unit tests (`test_agent_step_executor.py`): the agent is called and its output returned; an agent whose output violates its own declared schema is rejected with a clear message; a non-Agent step is refused; the dispatcher routes an Agent step to the agent executor and everything else to the default executor.
- 1 new integration test (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_agent_step_execution.py`) against real Postgres, through the real `WorkflowInstanceService.advance()`: a two-step definition (one `agent` step, one `tool` step) is driven to completion; the `step.completed` event for the agent step carries the agent's real output (`{"status": "ok"}`), while the tool step's `step.completed` event still carries `{}` — proving the dispatcher genuinely differentiates behaviour end to end, not just at the unit level.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite at this point: 86 tests passing (69 unit + 17 integration).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: Minimal Tool Contract and Real Trivial Tool Invocation
- **`tool.py`** (new): `Tool` (`Protocol`) — `trust_tier: TrustTier` plus `output_schema: dict[str, Any]` plus `async def execute(inputs) -> outputs`. Grounded directly in `platform_sdk/schemas/manifest.schema.json` `tools[]` — the authoritative Tool Contract, which requires `outputSchema` and `trustTier` alongside registration metadata (`id`, `name`, `version`, `description`, `entrypoint`) that belongs to the Manifest Loader, not to invoking an already-resolved tool object. `TrustTier` is a `StrEnum` with exactly the schema's two values (`tier1_sandboxed`, `tier2_trusted`). `input_schema` is excluded for the same reason as Agent's: no per-step input-mapping mechanism exists yet. `EchoTool` is the one trivial implementation, honestly declared `tier2_trusted` (it executes no command, compiles nothing, processes no untrusted content — the ADR-0016 conditions that would require `tier1_sandboxed`).
- **`step_executor.py`**: `ToolStepExecutor` invokes the injected `Tool` with `{}` and validates the returned output against `tool.output_schema`, mirroring `AgentStepExecutor` exactly. It additionally refuses any tool that is not `tier2_trusted` (`ToolSandboxRequiredError`, new) — there is no sandbox yet, so running a `tier1_sandboxed` tool in-process would silently violate the guarantee that tier is supposed to carry. `DispatchingStepExecutor`'s constructor is now three-way: `agent_executor`, `tool_executor`, `default_executor` — Tool-type steps route to the tool path, everything else unchanged.
- **`errors.py`**: added `ToolOutputValidationError` (mirrors `AgentOutputValidationError`) and `ToolSandboxRequiredError` (the trust-tier guard — a genuine, expected runtime rejection, not an internal wiring bug, so it gets a real error class rather than the `ValueError` used for the "wrong step type" guard).
- **No changes to `service.py` or `repository.py`** — same reasoning as the Agent step: the `StepExecutor` Protocol boundary was already sufficient.
- 7 new/changed unit tests: `test_tool_step_executor.py` (4 tests, mirroring the agent ones, plus the `tier1_sandboxed` rejection) and a new `test_dispatching_step_executor.py` (3 tests, fake executors throughout, proving the three-way routing in isolation — the two dispatching tests previously inline in `test_agent_step_executor.py` moved here since the dispatcher's own contract no longer belongs to either single-executor test file).
- 1 integration test, replacing the superseded agent-only file (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_agent_and_tool_step_execution.py`) against real Postgres: a three-step definition (`agent`, `tool`, `human_approval`) is driven to completion through the real `WorkflowInstanceService.advance()`; the agent step's `step.completed` event carries `{"status": "ok"}`, the tool step's carries `{"result": "ok"}`, and the human-approval step's still carries `{}` — proving all three paths are genuinely distinct end to end.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite at this point: 91 tests passing (74 unit + 17 integration).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: Lease Acquisition and Release
- **`lease.py`** (new): `WorkflowLease` (frozen, mirrors `WorkflowInstance`'s pattern), `WorkflowLeaseRepository` (`Protocol`) and its implementation `SqlWorkflowLeaseRepository`, and `WorkflowLeaseService`. Grounded in data_model.md §4.4 and workflow_engine.md §7.1: "Claimed with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`... expiry is what makes a crashed worker's work recoverable."
- **`acquire()` is one transaction, three steps:** (1) the target instance must exist and be `running` — leasing anything else is rejected (`WorkflowLeaseUnavailableError`, new); (2) `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (workflow_id) DO NOTHING RETURNING *` — the common, no-contention case, one round trip; (3) only if a lease row already exists: `SELECT ... WHERE expires_at < now() FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` — reclaims an **expired** lease, and `SKIP LOCKED` means a concurrent claimant racing for the exact same row gets no row back immediately rather than blocking on the other transaction. A non-expired existing lease, or a concurrently-locked row, both surface as the same clear rejection.
- **`release()`** deletes the lease row, but only if `worker_id` matches the holder — a worker cannot release someone else's lease. `WorkflowLeaseUnavailableError` again, distinguishing "does not exist" from "does not hold."
- **Deliberately deferred, not invented (at the time):** heartbeat *renewal* and a background expiry reaper. Renewal is now built (see next section); the reaper remains deferred.
- **No changes to `WorkflowInstanceService`/`WorkflowInstanceRepository`.** Leasing is deliberately a separate, independently composable service — `advance()` has no lease-awareness baked in. The documented "claim before calling `advance()`, release afterward" pattern is proven by composition in the tests (acquire → `advance()` → release), not by a new merged method; a future worker-loop orchestrator is the natural place to own that composition, and it does not exist yet.
- **`input_validation.py`**: added `validate_worker_id()` (reuses the shared `_require_non_blank` helper) and `validate_lease_duration()` (must be positive — a lease expiring at or before acquisition protects nothing).
- **`ids.py`**: added `new_lease_id()` (`lease_` prefix — data_model.md's prefixes are illustrative examples, not an exhaustive list, so this is a reasoned choice, not an invented schema field).
- 5 new unit tests (`test_lease_service.py`, fake repository): acquire/release delegate correctly; a blank `worker_id` is rejected before either operation reaches the repository; a non-positive `lease_duration_seconds` is rejected before acquire.
- 7 new integration tests (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_lease_acquisition.py`) against real Postgres: acquire-then-release leaves no row behind; a second claim while held is rejected; `advance()` succeeds normally with the lease held; release lets a different worker claim afterward; releasing without holding the lease is rejected; acquiring against a non-`running` instance is rejected; and an expired lease (its `expires_at` forced into the past to simulate a crashed worker, since heartbeat renewal doesn't exist to keep it current) is successfully reclaimed by a different worker.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite at this point: 103 tests passing (79 unit + 24 integration).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: Lease Heartbeat Renewal
- **`lease.py`**: `WorkflowLeaseRepository.renew()` / `WorkflowLeaseService.renew()` (plus the `SqlWorkflowLeaseRepository` implementation) complete the "acquire → heartbeat → release" cycle from workflow_engine.md §7.1. One atomic guarded `UPDATE workflow_leases SET expires_at = :new_expiry, heartbeat_at = now() WHERE workflow_id = :id AND worker_id = :worker_id AND expires_at >= now() RETURNING *` — the row is already known (unlike `acquire()`'s reclaim step, which scans an *unknown* row's state), so the guarded `UPDATE` itself is the correct, sufficient atomic primitive; `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` is deliberately **not** used here, and the module docstring now explains why, so a future reader doesn't mistake the absence for an oversight. Postgres's ordinary row lock during the `UPDATE` already serialises a renewal against a concurrent `acquire()` reclaim attempt on the same row.
- On rejection, `WorkflowLeaseUnavailableError` distinguishes three cases via a new `_describe_rejected_renewal` helper (mirroring the `_describe_rejected_*` pattern already used in `repository.py`): no lease exists at all; the lease is held by a different worker; the caller's own lease already expired (and may have been reclaimed by someone else).
- **`WorkflowLeaseService.renew()`** validates `worker_id` and `lease_duration_seconds` before delegating — identical validation to `acquire()`, reusing the same `input_validation.py` functions (no duplicated logic).
- 3 new unit tests (`test_lease_service.py`, fake repository extended with `renew`): renewal delegates correctly; a blank `worker_id` is rejected before renew reaches the repository; a non-positive `lease_duration_seconds` is rejected before renew.
- 5 new integration tests (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_lease_acquisition.py`) against real Postgres: the holder renews and `expires_at` genuinely advances; a non-holder's renewal attempt is rejected; renewing a lease that doesn't exist is rejected; renewing an already-expired lease is rejected; and — the key proof for this step — a lease acquired with a 1-second duration, then renewed to 60 seconds, is confirmed **still** claim-protected after the original 1-second window has elapsed (a second worker's `acquire()` attempt at that point is still rejected), proving the renewal genuinely extended protection rather than just updating a timestamp cosmetically.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite at this point: 111 tests passing (82 unit + 29 integration).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: Single-Shot Claim-and-Advance Composition
- **`advance_runner.py`** (new): `WorkflowAdvanceRunner.run_once(workflow_id, definition, worker_id, lease_duration_seconds)` — acquires a lease via the injected `WorkflowLeaseService`, calls `WorkflowInstanceService.advance()` exactly once, and releases the lease in a `finally` block. Release runs whether `advance()` succeeds or raises; if acquisition itself fails, `advance()` is never called and there is nothing to release. No new abstraction beyond a plain `try`/`finally` — the two composed services already do their own validation and guarding, so the runner adds no logic of its own beyond ordering the three calls correctly.
- **No changes to `WorkflowInstanceService`, `WorkflowLeaseService`, or either repository.** This step is purely composition over the two existing, already-Protocol-backed services — confirming they were genuinely independent and composable, as designed across the two previous steps.
- Deliberately not a Protocol-based dependency: `WorkflowAdvanceRunner` takes the concrete `WorkflowInstanceService`/`WorkflowLeaseService` classes directly, matching how every other call site in this codebase composes services (only the *repository* boundary underneath each service is a `Protocol`). Adding a second layer of interfaces here, with only one implementation of each service ever to exist, would be an unused abstraction.
- Explicitly not a loop: one call is one `acquire` → one `advance` → one `release`. A future worker loop would call `run_once` repeatedly and decide for itself when a long-running step needs `WorkflowLeaseService.renew()` in between — that decision and the loop itself remain out of scope.
- 3 new unit tests (`test_advance_runner.py`, fake repositories under real services): the happy path calls acquire → advance → release in order; when `advance()` raises, release still happens and the original exception still propagates to the caller (not swallowed, not replaced); when lease acquisition itself is rejected, `advance()` is never reached and no release is attempted.
- 4 new integration tests (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_advance_runner.py`) against real Postgres: a full claim→advance→release cycle leaves no lease row behind; a definition-id mismatch causes `advance()` to fail *after* the lease was already acquired, and the lease is still released; a lease already held by another worker is rejected before `advance()` runs at all (proving the existing concurrency-rejection behaviour survives being wrapped in this composition); and `run_once` called twice in sequence drives a one-step workflow all the way to `workflow.completed`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **118 tests passing** (85 unit + 33 integration: 4 persistence + 3 instance-creation + 4 state-transition + 5 step-progression + 1 agent-and-tool-step-execution + 12 lease-acquisition-and-renewal + 4 advance-runner).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: Run-to-Completion Loop for One Instance
