- **`advance_runner.py`**: `WorkflowAdvanceRunner.run_to_completion(workflow_id, definition, worker_id, lease_duration_seconds, max_iterations)` — calls `run_once()` repeatedly for **one** instance until it reaches `completed`, `max_iterations` calls have been made without completing, or a call raises. Added as a second method on the existing `WorkflowAdvanceRunner` rather than a new class: it already composes the two services this loop needs, so no additional wiring or abstraction was introduced.
- **`WorkflowRunOutcome`** (new `StrEnum`: `completed` / `max_iterations_reached` / `failed`) and **`WorkflowRunResult`** (new frozen Pydantic model: `workflow_id`, `outcome`, `iterations`, `last_instance`, `error`) — a structured, always-returned summary of what happened. `error` is typed `BaseException | None`, which requires `ConfigDict(frozen=True, arbitrary_types_allowed=True)` (the one model in the codebase holding a raw exception instance, matching the pattern of every other frozen read-model/snapshot type otherwise).
- **Deliberate broad `except Exception` boundary:** `run_to_completion` is an explicit, documented exception to this codebase's usual avoidance of broad excepts — it exists specifically to convert whatever `run_once()` raises into a non-raising `WorkflowRunResult` (`outcome=failed`, the exception captured in `error`) so a caller can inspect a multi-iteration run without wrapping every call in its own `try`/`except`. `run_once()` itself is unchanged and still always propagates exceptions normally.
- **Validation-before-loop:** `validate_max_iterations()` (new, in `input_validation.py` — non-positive is rejected, mirroring `validate_lease_duration`) runs *before* the loop starts and is allowed to raise `WorkflowInputValidationError` normally, not converted into a `FAILED` result — reasoned as a caller programming error (like a bound of zero, which would never even attempt one `advance()` call), not a workflow-runtime outcome.
- **No changes to `WorkflowInstanceService`, `WorkflowLeaseService`, `run_once()`, or either repository.** Each `run_once()` call still acquires and releases its own lease independently; hitting the `max_iterations` bound from the outer loop leaves no lease behind, proven explicitly in the new tests rather than merely assumed.
- 4 new unit tests (`test_advance_runner.py`, extended with a new stateful fake repository — `_StatefulInstanceRepository` — that tracks `current_step_id`/`status` across calls, since the original stateless fake used for `run_once` tests always reported the same fixed state regardless of what `advance_workflow` had returned): a two-step workflow reaches `completed` across multiple iterations with matching acquire/release counts; `max_iterations=1` stops the loop with `MAX_ITERATIONS_REACHED` after exactly one `advance()` call; a simulated failure on the second call is reported as `FAILED` with the exception captured in `result.error`, not raised, and the lease is still shown released; a non-positive `max_iterations` is rejected before any advance/lease call happens.
- 3 new integration tests (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_advance_runner.py`) against real Postgres: a two-step definition run through `run_to_completion` reaches `completed` in exactly 3 iterations (step_a, step_b, the completing call) with no lease row left behind; `max_iterations=1` on the same definition stops before completion, with the lease still fully released; and a definition-id mismatch causes every `advance()` call's guard to reject it, `run_to_completion` reporting `FAILED` without letting the exception propagate out of the call.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **125 tests passing** (89 unit + 36 integration: 4 persistence + 3 instance-creation + 4 state-transition + 5 step-progression + 1 agent-and-tool-step-execution + 12 lease-acquisition-and-renewal + 7 advance-runner).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: Background Lease Expiry Reaper
- **`lease.py`**: `WorkflowLeaseRepository.reap_expired(limit)` / `WorkflowLeaseService.reap_expired(limit)` (plus the `SqlWorkflowLeaseRepository` implementation) close the one remaining reactive gap in the acquire → renew → release → reclaim cycle — until this step, an expired lease was only reclaimed as a side effect of some *other* worker's next `acquire()` call on the same instance; if nothing ever called `acquire()` again (every capable worker crashed or moved on), the stale row sat there indefinitely.
- **`reap_expired()` is one bounded transaction, two steps:** (1) `SELECT lease_id ... WHERE expires_at < now() ORDER BY expires_at LIMIT :limit FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` — scans for candidate rows, capped at the caller-supplied `limit`; a row currently being touched by a concurrent `acquire()`/`renew()`/`release()` is skipped this pass (no row returned for it) rather than blocked on, the same "scanning an unknown row's state" reasoning already used for `acquire()`'s reclaim step. (2) `DELETE ... WHERE lease_id IN (...) RETURNING *` — deletes exactly the rows just locked. Under Postgres's default READ COMMITTED isolation, a concurrent transaction that renews or reclaims one of those rows and commits first causes the `DELETE`'s own re-evaluated `WHERE` to simply not match that row anymore — no separate check-then-act race window, the same guarantee every other guarded statement in this module relies on.
- **`lease_reaper.py`** (new): `WorkflowLeaseReaper.reap_once(limit)` — a thin, structured-logging wrapper over `WorkflowLeaseService.reap_expired()` (no new persistence logic), and `LeaseReapResult` (frozen, holds the reclaimed `WorkflowLease` tuple plus a `count` property). Logs one structured `workflow_lease_reaper.reaped` event (count + affected `workflow_id`s) when anything was reclaimed, `workflow_lease_reaper.no_expired_leases` otherwise — the "Structured logging/errors" requirement for this step. Composes the existing `WorkflowLeaseService` exactly as `WorkflowAdvanceRunner` composes its two services: no new abstraction layer for a job with only one implementation.
- **Deliberately not a scheduler:** `reap_once()` is exactly one bounded pass — nothing in this module decides when or how often to call it, or loops it. A future worker process framework would call it on a timer, deciding scheduling for itself (explicitly out of scope here, per the approved step).
- **`input_validation.py`**: added `validate_reap_limit()` (must be positive, mirroring `validate_max_iterations` — a bound of zero or fewer would never examine a single lease).
- **No changes to `acquire()`, `renew()`, `release()`, `WorkflowInstanceService`, or either instance repository.** The reaper only ever deletes rows that `acquire()`'s own guard already treats as worthless (`expires_at < now()`); it does not change what "expired" means or how acquisition/renewal validate it.
- 6 new unit tests: `test_lease_service.py` extended with `reap_expired` on the fake repository (delegates correctly; a non-positive `limit` is rejected before the repository is called); new `test_lease_reaper.py` (fake `WorkflowLeaseRepository` under the real `WorkflowLeaseService`): `reap_once` returns and the caller can inspect exactly what was reclaimed; an empty result when nothing is expired; the caller-supplied `limit` is honored even when more candidates exist than the bound; a non-positive `limit` is rejected before any repository call.
- 3 new integration tests (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_lease_acquisition.py`) against real Postgres: an expired lease is reclaimed while a second, still-active lease on a different instance is left completely untouched; reaping across three simultaneously-expired leases with `limit=2` reclaims exactly two in the first pass and the remaining one in a second pass (proving the bound is real, not advisory); and the `WorkflowLeaseReaper` job itself, composed over the real service and repository, reclaims an expired lease end to end. A new `_clear_all_leases()` test helper gives these tests a clean slate, since `reap_expired` reasons about *all* rows in `workflow_leases` and several earlier tests in the same module-scoped fixture deliberately leave lease rows (held or expired) behind.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **134 tests passing** (95 unit + 39 integration: 4 persistence + 3 instance-creation + 4 state-transition + 5 step-progression + 1 agent-and-tool-step-execution + 15 lease-acquisition-renewal-and-reaping + 7 advance-runner).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: `workflow_steps` — Materialised Per-Step State
- **`kernel/alembic/versions/0002_workflow_steps.py`** (new) + **`persistence/schema.py`**: `workflow.workflow_steps`, column-for-column per data_model.md §4.3 — `step_id` PK (`stp_…`, the prefix data_model.md §2 already gives as its own example), `workflow_id` (FK → `workflow_instances`), `step_name`, `step_type`, `status`, `attempt`, `agent_id`, `tool_id`, `inputs`, `outputs`, `error`, `idempotency_key`, `usage` (jsonb), `started_at`, `completed_at`. `UNIQUE (workflow_id, step_name, attempt)` — exactly the constraint data_model.md §4.3 specifies, making retries explicit and countable rather than overwriting history.
- **Two deliberate, asymmetric constraint choices, not oversights:** `step_type` gets a `CHECK` constraint (its seven values are already documented — workflow_architecture.md's Supported Step Types, `StepType` in `models.py`) but `status` does not — unlike `workflow_instances.status`, data_model.md §4.3 gives `workflow_steps.status` no canonical value list, so enumerating one would be inventing business logic beyond what was approved for this step. One index beyond what §4.3 explicitly lists (which, unlike §4.1/§4.2, gives no "Indexes:" line at all): `workflow_id`, mirroring how every other workflow-state table already indexes its own foreign key into `workflow_instances`.
- **`ids.py`**: added `new_step_id()` (`stp_` prefix).
- **`repository.py`**: `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository.advance_workflow()` now writes exactly one `workflow_steps` row in the same transaction as `step.started`/`step.completed`, only on the step-execution branch (never on the `workflow.completed` branch — there is no step to materialise there). The row is written with `status = "completed"` always: the step executor already ran to completion (or raised, in which case nothing in this transaction is ever reached) before `advance_workflow()`'s transaction opens, so there is no persisted "running" phase to model at this stage — a fact now named as a constant (`_STEP_STATUS_COMPLETED`) rather than a hardcoded literal. `idempotency_key` is `f"{workflow_id}:{step_name}:{attempt}"`, exactly the derivation the module's own pre-existing comment on `_FIRST_ATTEMPT` already named ("idempotency key is derived from (workflow_id, step_name, attempt)") — not a new invention, just implementing what was already documented in the code. `agent_id`/`tool_id` are always `NULL` and `inputs` is always `{}`, matching the already-documented deferral that no field on `WorkflowStep` yet names which agent/tool a step should call or what its real inputs are. `usage` is always `{}` — no LLM Gateway exists yet to populate it.
- **No changes to `WorkflowInstanceRepository`'s Protocol shape, `WorkflowInstanceService`, or any step executor.** Purely additive persistence inside the existing atomic transaction — the same "the guarded `UPDATE` is the seam" reasoning: if the transaction rolls back for any reason, the `workflow_steps` insert rolls back with it, exactly like every other write in this method.
- 2 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: the documented columns/PK/`step_type` `CHECK` constraint are all present; the `UNIQUE (workflow_id, step_name, attempt)` constraint genuinely rejects a duplicate. `test_migration_up_down_up_round_trip`'s downgrade target changed from `"-1"` to `"base"` — with two migrations in the chain now, `"-1"` only undoes the most recent one, not the whole schema.
- `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_step_progression.py` extended (no new test functions — existing tests strengthened): the multi-step happy-path test now asserts two `workflow_steps` rows exist with the correct `step_name`/`step_type`/`status`/`attempt`/`outputs`/`idempotency_key`/timestamps; the rollback test now asserts the rejected completion attempt leaves exactly the two already-written step rows untouched, with no phantom row for the completion branch itself.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **136 tests passing** (95 unit + 41 integration: 6 persistence + 3 instance-creation + 4 state-transition + 5 step-progression + 1 agent-and-tool-step-execution + 15 lease-acquisition-renewal-and-reaping + 7 advance-runner).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: `workflow_steps` Read Accessor (`list_steps`)
- **`step_record.py`** (new): `WorkflowStepRecord` — a frozen Pydantic read model, one per `workflow_steps` row, named distinctly from `models.WorkflowStep` (a step *declaration* inside a definition file) to keep "declared step" and "materialised per-step state" unambiguous. `step_type` is typed as the existing `StepType` enum (its seven values are already documented and already enforced by the table's own `CHECK` constraint); `status` stays a plain `str`, mirroring the same previous-step decision not to invent a canonical value list for it.
- **`repository.py`**: `WorkflowInstanceRepository.list_steps(workflow_id)` (Protocol) and its `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository` implementation — a plain, unguarded read exactly mirroring `get_instance()`: no leasing, no locking, no existence check (an unknown `workflow_id` returns an empty list, not an error — the same "existence checks belong to whichever caller cares" reasoning `get_instance()` already uses by returning `None` rather than raising). Rows are ordered by `started_at`, the instance's actual execution order.
- **No new business logic and no new writes.** `advance_workflow()` is unchanged; this step only adds a way to read back what it already writes. No changes to `WorkflowInstanceService` either — matching the precedent that `get_instance()` itself is repository-only, with no service-layer wrapper, since a plain read needs no validation to sit in front of it.
- **`__init__.py`**: exported `WorkflowStepRecord`; module docstring's step-progression bullet now also names `list_steps` and the read model it returns.
- 3 fake repositories across `test_service.py` and `test_advance_runner.py` gained a `list_steps` stub (`raise NotImplementedError("not exercised by these tests")`) purely for `Protocol` conformance under `mypy --strict` — no behavioral test needed at the unit level, matching how `get_instance()` itself has never had a dedicated unit test (it is a thin SQL read, exercised for real only against Postgres).
- 2 new integration tests (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_step_records.py`, new file — kept separate from `test_step_progression.py` so that file stays focused on `advance()`'s own transition semantics): a two-step workflow's `list_steps()` call returns exactly two `WorkflowStepRecord`s in execution order, with every field (`step_type`, `status`, `attempt`, `agent_id`/`tool_id` both `None`, `inputs`/`outputs`/`usage`, `idempotency_key`, timestamps) matching what `advance_workflow()` wrote — and confirming the `workflow.completed` branch contributed no third row; and `list_steps()` returns an empty list both for a real instance that hasn't executed any step yet and for a `workflow_id` that does not exist at all.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **138 tests passing** (95 unit + 43 integration: 6 persistence + 3 instance-creation + 4 state-transition + 5 step-progression + 2 step-records + 1 agent-and-tool-step-execution + 15 lease-acquisition-renewal-and-reaping + 7 advance-runner).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: `approvals` Table (Schema and Migration Only)
- **`kernel/alembic/versions/0003_approvals.py`** (new) + **`persistence/schema.py`**: `workflow.approvals`, column-for-column per data_model.md §4.5 — `approval_id` PK, `workflow_id` (FK → `workflow_instances`), `step_id`, `approval_class`, `title`, `description`, `context_digest`, `options` (jsonb), `status`, `decided_by`, `decision_comment`, `requested_at`, `expires_at`, `decided_at`. No writer — schema and migration only, exactly as approved; no Human Approval Point execution path exists yet to populate it.
- **Followed the doc's own naming exactly, including its asymmetry:** every other table in this schema repeats "workflow" in its own name (`workflow_instances`, `workflow_events`, `workflow_leases`, `workflow_steps`), but data_model.md §4.5's header is `workflow.approvals` — the table name itself is just `approvals`. That asymmetry is already in the source of truth, not something to "fix" by inventing a more consistent name.
- **`approvals.status` gets a `CHECK` constraint** (unlike `workflow_steps.status`, deliberately left unconstrained two steps ago): §4.5 gives `status` an explicit six-value canonical list (`pending`/`approved`/`rejected`/`changes_requested`/`timed_out`/`cancelled`), the same "canonical list is documented, so enforce it" reasoning already applied to `workflow_instances.status` — nothing invented, just enforcing what the doc already states.
- **Nullability choices, since §4.5 (like §4.3) marks none explicitly:** derived directly from the already-established Human Approval Point Contract (`ai_os_kernel.workflow_engine.models.HumanApprovalPoint`) rather than invented fresh — `title`/`description`/`options` are `NOT NULL` because that contract already requires `name`/`description`/at-least-one `options` entry; `expires_at` is nullable because the contract's own `timeout` field is optional; `decided_by`/`decision_comment`/`decided_at` are nullable because a `pending` approval has no decision yet; `step_id` is `NOT NULL` (a Human Approval Point is inherently step-scoped) but carries no foreign key, mirroring `workflow_events.step_id` — no table persists the set of declared step ids a definition's steps must belong to yet.
- Two indexes beyond what §4.5 explicitly lists (which, like §4.3, has no "Indexes:" line at all): `workflow_id` (mirrors every other workflow-state table's own FK index) and `status` (mirrors `workflow_instances.status`'s index — "all pending approvals" is as natural an operational query as "all running instances").
- **No changes to any repository, service, or the workflow_engine package's `__init__.py` exports.** This step adds no Python read/write surface at all — purely structural, matching the approved scope precisely.
- 3 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: the documented columns/PK/`status` `CHECK` constraint are all present; the `CHECK` constraint genuinely rejects an invalid `status` value; the foreign key genuinely rejects an `approvals` row referencing a nonexistent `workflow_id`. `_EXPECTED_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test) now includes `approvals`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **141 tests passing** (95 unit + 46 integration: 9 persistence + 3 instance-creation + 4 state-transition + 5 step-progression + 2 step-records + 1 agent-and-tool-step-execution + 15 lease-acquisition-renewal-and-reaping + 7 advance-runner).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine: `workflow_events` Read Accessor (`list_events`)
- **`event_record.py`** (new): `WorkflowEventRecord` — a frozen Pydantic read model, one per `workflow_events` row, mirroring `WorkflowStepRecord`'s precedent exactly. `event_type` stays a plain `str`, not an enum: data_model.md §4.2 lists example values ending in an ellipsis (an explicitly open-ended, illustrative set, unlike `step_type`'s closed seven), and the table itself has no `CHECK` constraint on this column either — enumerating one here would invent a closed vocabulary the doc deliberately leaves open.
- **`repository.py`**: `WorkflowInstanceRepository.list_events(workflow_id)` (Protocol) and its `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository` implementation — a plain, unguarded read mirroring `get_instance()` and `list_steps()`: no leasing, no locking, no existence check (an unknown `workflow_id` returns an empty list). Rows are ordered by `seq`, the append-only log's own per-instance ordering (`UNIQUE (workflow_id, seq)`).
- **No new business logic and no new writes.** Every event-appending method in `repository.py` is unchanged; this step only adds a way to read back what they already write. No `WorkflowInstanceService` changes either, same reasoning as `list_steps`.
- **`__init__.py`**: exported `WorkflowEventRecord`; module docstring's step-progression bullet now also names `list_events` and the read model it returns, alongside `list_steps`.
- 3 fake repositories across `test_service.py` and `test_advance_runner.py` gained a `list_events` stub (`raise NotImplementedError("not exercised by these tests")`) purely for `Protocol` conformance under `mypy --strict` — same reasoning as `list_steps`' stubs two steps ago: no dedicated unit test needed for a thin SQL read exercised for real only against Postgres.
- 2 new integration tests (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_event_records.py`, new file, mirroring `test_step_records.py`): a full create → start → advance ×3 run's `list_events()` call returns exactly the 7 documented events in `seq` order with correct `event_type`/`step_id`/`payload`/`schema_version`/`agent_id` values (matching the sequence already proven in `test_step_progression.py`); and a freshly created (not yet started) instance's `list_events()` returns exactly one `workflow.started` event, while an unknown `workflow_id` returns an empty list.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **143 tests passing** (95 unit + 48 integration: 9 persistence + 3 instance-creation + 4 state-transition + 5 step-progression + 2 step-records + 2 event-records + 1 agent-and-tool-step-execution + 15 lease-acquisition-renewal-and-reaping + 7 advance-runner).

### Stage A – CI Pipeline Aligned With the Current Codebase
- **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`**: every stage now either runs for real against what the repository actually contains, or is gated to a clean no-op via `if: hashFiles(...) != ''` on the specific not-yet-existing path — never silently deleted, since deleting a stage would drop the pipeline shape ADR-0015 and test_strategy.md §6 document as the target. Each gate is commented with what it's waiting for, so the stage starts running for real the moment its prerequisite lands, with no further CI change required. `contract`'s two steps (`tests/contract`, `scripts/check_import_boundaries.py`), `security`'s one step (`tests/security`), `integration`'s one step (`tests/workflow`), `build` (`Dockerfile`), and `frontend` (`dashboard/package.json`, combined with its original trigger condition) are all gated this way — nothing in this list exists yet.
- **`types`**: the `mypy --strict` invocation now targets exactly `kernel/src kernel/alembic tests` — mirroring `[tool.mypy] files` in `pyproject.toml` and every `mypy --strict` run performed by hand throughout this whole implementation log. It previously named `platform_sdk platform_services capability_packs`; `platform_services` doesn't exist as a directory at all (mypy would hard-fail just resolving the path), and `platform_sdk`/`capability_packs` currently hold only schema/manifest/YAML files, no Python.
- **`integration`**: switched fully to the `testcontainers` approach every integration test already uses — the GitHub Actions `services: postgres`/`services: redis` containers and their hardcoded `AIOS_DATABASE_URL`/`AIOS_REDIS_URL`/`AIOS_SECRET_BACKEND` env vars are removed; each test module provisions and tears down its own ephemeral Postgres and sets the URL itself, exactly as `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py` already does. The standalone "Alembic migrations up/down/up" step is removed too — it had nothing to run against once the shared service was removed, and `test_migrations.py`'s own round-trip test already proves the same thing against its own container.
- **Coverage (NFR-060, "≥ 90% kernel test coverage") moved from the `unit` job to `integration`, and now measures `tests/unit` + `tests/integration` together** — not `tests/unit` alone, which was never structurally capable of reaching 90%: ADR-0015 deliberately keeps real SQL persistence code (`SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository`, `SqlWorkflowLeaseRepository`, ...) exercised only by integration tests against a real Postgres, never by unit-test mocks. Measured against unit tests alone, coverage was **81.28%** (verified locally); measured against unit + integration together it is **95.61%**, comfortably clearing the 90% floor — confirming the *combined* suite, not either layer alone, is what "Kernel test coverage" (NFR-060) means. The `--cov`/`--cov-branch` flags now take no explicit `--cov=<package>` argument, relying on the single already-declared `[tool.coverage.run] source = ["ai_os_kernel"]` in `pyproject.toml` instead of a second, driftable list of package names in the workflow file (the previous `--cov=kernel --cov=platform_sdk --cov=platform_services` referenced one nonexistent and two Python-free directories).
- **`security`'s "Dependency vulnerabilities" step was not actually runnable at all before this change**: `pip-audit` was never declared as a dependency anywhere in the workspace (verified locally: `command not found`). Added `pip-audit>=2.7,<3.0` to the root `pyproject.toml`'s dev dependency group (`uv.lock` regenerated) — completing tooling ADR-0015/test_strategy.md §2 already named as the chosen tool, not introducing a new one. Running it against the live environment then failed for an unrelated reason: `pip-audit --strict` cannot look up the unpublished local `ai-os-kernel` workspace package on PyPI and hard-fails on that lookup under `--strict`. Fixed by auditing an exported requirements list instead (`uv export --format requirements-txt --no-emit-workspace --no-dev`), which excludes both the local package (`--no-emit-workspace`) and dev-only tooling (`--no-dev` — pytest, mypy, ruff, ... never ship in a deployed AI_OS instance, so a *runtime* vulnerability gate is the wrong place to fail on one of them). Verified locally: the runtime-only dependency set has zero known vulnerabilities; the full environment (including dev tools) currently has one, in `pytest` itself, deliberately out of scope for this gate for the reason just stated.
- **No changes to any workflow-engine source, test, or business logic.** This step is CI configuration and dev-tooling metadata only (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `pyproject.toml`'s dev dependency group, `uv.lock`), exactly as approved.
- Verified locally, command-for-command matching what CI now runs: `ruff check .`, `ruff format --check .`, `mypy --strict kernel/src kernel/alembic tests`, `pytest tests/unit -v`, `pytest tests/unit tests/integration -v --cov --cov-branch --cov-report=term-missing --cov-fail-under=90`, `uv sync --frozen --all-packages`, and the exported-requirements `pip-audit --strict` invocation — all pass. `gitleaks/gitleaks-action` is unchanged and cannot be exercised locally the same way; nothing about this step's changes affects it.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite unchanged by this step: **143 tests passing** (95 unit + 48 integration).

### Stage A – Minimal Secrets Resolution (ADR-0024)
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/secrets_manager/` subpackage** (no such module existed before this step — confirmed by scanning the repo first). Named to match the sibling `_manager` components already present (`configuration_manager`, `security_manager`, `capability_manager`, ...); no dedicated `docs/03_architecture/kernel/secrets_manager.md` exists, so ADR-0024 and `docs/09_security/secrets_management.md` are the sole source of truth this step is grounded in.
- **`reference.py`**: `parse_secret_reference()` parses the ADR-0024 URI format `secret://<provider>/<name>[#<version>]` (`SecretReference`: `provider`, `name`, `version`) — backend-agnostic and shared by every future provider, not `EnvSecretProvider`-specific. `<name>` accepts Vault-style nested paths (`llm/anthropic-api-key`), exactly the example ADR-0024 itself gives.
- **`value.py`**: `SecretValue` — `__str__`/`__repr__` return `"***"` (ADR-0024 rule 2); the raw value requires an explicit `.reveal()` call. A plain class with `__slots__`, not a Pydantic model — there is nothing to validate, only a value to guard.
- **`provider.py`**: `SecretProvider` — a `Protocol` with one method, `async def resolve(self, reference: str) -> SecretValue`. `async` even though the only implementation today does no real I/O, matching every other backend seam in this codebase (`WorkflowLeaseRepository`, `WorkflowInstanceRepository`, ...) and because every *other* backend ADR-0024 names (Vault, cloud secret managers) will need it.
- **`env_provider.py`**: `EnvSecretProvider` — the `env` backend, explicitly scoped by ADR-0024 to local development only. Maps a reference's `<name>` onto an environment variable via a fixed `AIOS_SECRET_` prefix (e.g. `llm/anthropic-api-key` → `AIOS_SECRET_LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) — namespaced so this backend can only ever read a variable clearly meant for it, never collide with an unrelated one. Rejects a reference naming a different provider, rejects a reference requesting a `#version` (a plain env var has no versions to serve), and raises clearly on a missing variable. The environment mapping is injectable (`env: Mapping[str, str] | None = None`, defaulting to `os.environ`) — ADR-0004, and it means unit tests never mutate real process state.
- **`errors.py`**: `SecretResolutionError` — one exception class covering both "the reference itself is malformed" and "a provider could not resolve it," mirroring `ConfigurationError`'s single-class-per-concern style.
- **Deliberately not built, per approved scope:** the encrypted-file backend, Vault, cloud secret managers, the Access Broker (authorization + audit per access), TTL caching with rotation invalidation, the prompt-assembly scan rejecting a resolved value from reaching a model, and wiring into any consumer (Configuration Manager, LLM Gateway, Git Integration Service) — none of those consumers exist yet either.
- 24 new unit tests (`tests/unit/kernel/secrets_manager/`): `test_reference.py` (valid nested-path/flat/versioned references parse correctly; 10 malformed variants — empty, wrong scheme, uppercase provider, trailing slash, empty version, ...) all rejected with a clear message); `test_value.py` (`.reveal()` returns the raw value; `str()`/`repr()`/f-string interpolation all return `"***"` and never contain the raw value); `test_env_provider.py` (flat and nested-path names resolve to the correct env var; a missing var, a wrong-provider reference, a versioned reference, and a malformed reference are all rejected with the expected message; a real `monkeypatch`-set process env var is read correctly when no `env` mapping is injected, proving the default path works too).
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **167 tests passing** (119 unit + 48 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **95.87%** (secrets_manager itself: 100%).

### Stage A – Real OpenTelemetry Span Instrumentation (ADR-0017)
- **New `observability/tracing.py`**: `configure_tracing()` installs a process-wide OTel `TracerProvider` (called once, from `bootstrap.build_app()`, mirroring `configure_logging()`); `get_tracer(name)` mirrors `get_logger(name)`; `bind_otel_span_context()` is a new structlog processor that adds the *active span's own* `otelTraceID`/`otelSpanID` (hex, OTel-native) to every log line emitted while a span is active — distinct from and additional to this platform's own `trace_id` field, which is unchanged.
- **Export is the OTel console exporter** (`ConsoleSpanExporter` + `SimpleSpanProcessor`), exactly as approved — not OTLP: ADR-0017's actual target needs a Collector, and none is deployed (no Compose observability profile exists yet). Swapping to OTLP later is a one-line change inside `configure_tracing()` and nowhere else, which is the literal "backend-swappable without touching application code" guarantee ADR-0017 makes — verified in spirit by how cleanly the console exporter itself drops in.
- **`middleware.py`**: `TraceIdMiddleware.dispatch()` now wraps `call_next()` in a real span (`tracer.start_as_current_span(f"{method} {path}")`) with attributes `aios.trace_id`, `http.method`, `http.target`, `http.status_code`. The existing `X-Trace-Id` header contract (an opaque, caller-supplied-or-generated token with no format requirement) is **unchanged** — it is deliberately not replaced by the OTel trace id, which has a strict 128-bit format the existing, already-tested `test_incoming_trace_id_is_honored` (`X-Trace-Id: my-custom-trace`, an arbitrary non-hex string) would violate. Both ids are recorded; they answer different questions ("what did the caller send/receive" vs. "which span is this"). An exception raised inside `call_next()` is recorded on the span and re-raised automatically by OTel's own span context manager — no extra handling needed.
- **`configure_tracing()` is idempotency-guarded**: the OpenTelemetry API only allows the global `TracerProvider` to be set once per process and otherwise just logs a warning; since every test in this repo calls `build_app()` (and therefore `configure_tracing()`) independently, an unguarded call would emit a spurious warning on every call after the first. Guarded by checking whether the current provider is already a real SDK `TracerProvider` (as opposed to OTel's default no-op proxy) before installing one.
- **New runtime dependencies**: `opentelemetry-api>=1.27,<2.0` and `opentelemetry-sdk>=1.27,<2.0` added to `kernel/pyproject.toml` (real Kernel runtime code, not dev tooling — alongside `fastapi`, `sqlalchemy`, ...); `uv.lock` regenerated. Both ship inline type hints (`py.typed`), so no separate `types-opentelemetry-*` stub packages were needed for `mypy --strict`.
- **Manually verified end to end** (not just unit-tested): built the real app via `bootstrap.build_app()` and issued a request — the console exporter printed a real span with the documented attributes and `service.name: ai-os-kernel`, and the `X-Trace-Id` response header behaved exactly as before. Separately, calling `get_logger(...).info(...)` outside vs. inside an active span showed the log line gains `otelTraceID`/`otelSpanID` only when a span is active, and those values exactly match the corresponding exported span's own `trace_id`/`span_id`.
- 7 new unit tests: `tests/unit/kernel/observability/test_tracing.py` (`get_tracer` returns a real `Tracer`; `bind_otel_span_context` adds two 32/16-char lowercase-hex fields while a span is active and leaves the event dict untouched with none active; two spans in the same trace share a `trace_id` but not a `span_id`; `configure_tracing()` is safe to call twice) and `tests/unit/kernel/observability/test_middleware.py` (a real request through `TraceIdMiddleware`, with the tracer swapped via `monkeypatch` for a locally-created `TracerProvider` + `InMemorySpanExporter` — never the process global, so fully isolated from `configure_tracing()`'s once-per-process state — produces exactly one finished span with every documented attribute correct, for both a caller-supplied and a generated `X-Trace-Id`).
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **174 tests passing** (126 unit + 48 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **95.97%** (`observability/middleware.py` and `observability/tracing.py`: 100%).

### Stage A – Real OpenTelemetry Metrics Instrumentation (ADR-0017)
- **New `observability/metrics.py`**, mirroring `tracing.py`'s shape exactly: `configure_metrics()` installs a process-wide OTel `MeterProvider` (called once, from `bootstrap.build_app()`, alongside `configure_tracing()`); `get_meter(name)` mirrors `get_tracer(name)`; `get_http_requests_counter()` returns the one `aios.http.requests` counter (unit `{request}` — the OTel convention for a dimensionless count of things — description "Number of HTTP requests handled by the Kernel API"), named per the `aios.<subsystem>.<metric>` convention `observability_stack.md` §3.1 already specifies.
- **Export is the OTel console exporter** (`ConsoleMetricExporter` via `PeriodicExportingMetricReader`), exactly as approved, for the identical "no Collector deployed yet" reason spans use one — swapping to OTLP later is a one-line change inside `configure_metrics()` and nowhere else.
- **`middleware.py`**: `TraceIdMiddleware.dispatch()` now increments `get_http_requests_counter()` by 1, with attributes `http.method` and `http.status_code`, once a response actually exists (after `call_next()` returns) — a request whose handling raises before producing a response is not counted against any status, matching how the span's own attribute-setting is already guarded the same way.
- **The counter is created once and cached** (a module-level singleton, lazily built on first use), not re-created per request: OTel instruments are meant to be created a single time per name and reused, unlike `get_tracer`/`get_meter`, which are cheap and idempotent to call fresh every request. `get_http_requests_counter()` calls `configure_metrics()` itself if nothing has yet, so it carries no assumption about call order relative to `bootstrap.build_app()`.
- **`configure_metrics()` is idempotency-guarded**, identically to `configure_tracing()` and for the same reason: the OpenTelemetry API only allows the global `MeterProvider` to be set once per process.
- **New test-infrastructure fixture, not application code**: `tests/conftest.py` (new file — none existed before) shuts down the OTel `MeterProvider`'s background export thread at the end of the test session. `PeriodicExportingMetricReader` runs a background thread that, left unmanaged, could attempt one more export after pytest has already closed the streams it captured, producing a spurious `ValueError: I/O operation on closed file` traceback after an otherwise fully-passing run (observed and reproduced locally on the full unit suite before adding this fixture; confirmed silent afterward). This did not affect `SimpleSpanProcessor` in the tracing step, which exports synchronously with no background thread.
- **No new runtime dependency**: metrics are already part of the `opentelemetry-sdk`/`opentelemetry-api` packages added in the tracing step; nothing new was added to `kernel/pyproject.toml`.
- **Manually verified end to end**: built the real app via `bootstrap.build_app()`, issued three requests, then called `MeterProvider.force_flush()` (bypassing the default 60-second export interval for immediate verification) — the console exporter printed `aios.http.requests` with `value: 3`, attributes `{"http.method": "GET", "http.status_code": 200}`, the documented unit and description, and `service.name: ai-os-kernel` in the shared resource.
- 6 new unit tests: `tests/unit/kernel/observability/test_metrics.py` (`get_meter` returns a real `Meter`; `get_http_requests_counter` returns a real `Counter`; two calls return the identical cached object; `configure_metrics()` is safe to call twice) and 2 new tests in `tests/unit/kernel/observability/test_middleware.py` (a real request through `TraceIdMiddleware`, with the counter swapped via `monkeypatch` for a locally-created `MeterProvider` + `InMemoryMetricReader` — never the process global — records exactly one data point with the documented attributes and value 1; two requests accumulate to value 2 on the same data point).
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **180 tests passing** (132 unit + 48 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.07%** (`observability/metrics.py`, `middleware.py`, `tracing.py`: all 100%).

### Stage A – `governance.audit_log` Table (Schema and Migration Only)
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/persistence/governance_schema.py`** (a new module, not added to the existing `persistence/schema.py`): `governance.audit_log`, column-for-column per data_model.md §9.1 — `audit_id` PK, `seq` (auto-incrementing, unique), `event_type`, `principal_id`, `principal_type`, `resource_type`/`resource_id` (nullable), `outcome`, `detail` (jsonb), `trace_id` (nullable), `prev_hash`/`row_hash`, `occurred_at`. No writer — schema and migration only, exactly as approved; nothing computes `row_hash`/`prev_hash` or writes a row yet.
- **Kept in its own module and schema (`governance`), not folded into `persistence/schema.py` (`workflow`)**: audit is a genuinely distinct bounded context from workflow state, and `persistence/schema.py`'s own docstring already scopes itself to "workflow-state persistence." `kernel/alembic/env.py`'s `target_metadata` is now a list of both modules' `MetaData` objects — Alembic has supported a sequence there since well before the installed version (1.18.5), so both remain live autogenerate-diff targets without merging two schemas into one `MetaData`.
- **`seq`'s documented type is `bigserial`**, implemented as a SQL-standard `GENERATED ... AS IDENTITY` column (`sa.Identity()`) rather than Postgres's legacy `BIGSERIAL` pseudo-type — both produce an auto-incrementing, sequence-backed bigint with identical insert behaviour; `IDENTITY` is what current Postgres/SQLAlchemy documentation recommends for new schemas. Verified directly: two inserts without specifying `seq` got strictly increasing values, and a hand-supplied duplicate `seq` was rejected by the `UNIQUE` constraint.
- **`outcome` gets a `CHECK` constraint; `event_type` does not** — the same reasoning already applied twice (`workflow_instances.status`/`approvals.status` vs. `workflow_steps.status`/`workflow_events.event_type`): §9.1 gives `outcome` an explicit four-value list (`allowed`/`denied`/`success`/`failure`), but lists `event_type` examples ending in an ellipsis — an explicitly open-ended set, not a closed one to enforce.
- **No foreign key**, unlike every `workflow`-schema table added so far: §9.1 does not scope this table to workflow instances at all — `resource_type`/`resource_id` is a loose, polymorphic pointer (auth, secrets, packs, sandboxes, git, not only workflows), so there is no single table it could reference.
- **Two deferrals carried over from the identical `workflow_events` precedent, not newly invented:** `UPDATE`/`DELETE` revocation for "the application role" (no such role exists yet) and the scheduled hash-chain verification job (data_model.md §9.1 — no worker process framework exists yet to run it in).
- 4 new/changed integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: the up/down/up round-trip test now also asserts the `governance` schema and its one table appear and disappear correctly; the documented columns/PK/`outcome` `CHECK` constraint are all present; the `CHECK` constraint genuinely rejects an invalid `outcome` value; `seq` auto-increments across inserts and its `UNIQUE` constraint genuinely rejects a hand-supplied duplicate.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **183 tests passing** (132 unit + 51 integration).

### Stage A – `governance.config_changes` Table (Completes Governance Schema §9)
- **`governance_schema.py`**: added `config_changes`, column-for-column per data_model.md §9.2 — `change_id` PK, `config_key`, `old_value_digest`, `new_value_digest`, `changed_by`, `reason`, `changed_at`. In the same module as `audit_log`, same `governance` `MetaData`; this is the second and last table §9 documents, so the module docstring's "later work" note about it is now removed — `governance` schema §9 is complete. No writer — schema and migration only, exactly as approved.
- **No `CHECK` constraint and no foreign key, unlike `audit_log`**: every column here is a plain scalar (no `jsonb`, no enum-like column with a documented value list), so there is nothing to constrain that way; and, for the same reason `audit_log` has none, nothing scopes a configuration key to one specific table.
- **Nullability reasoned, not invented, since §9.2 marks no column nullable**: `old_value_digest` is nullable for a config key's first-ever value (no prior value to digest); `new_value_digest` is nullable when a change removes a key entirely (no new value to digest) — an "update" row has both. `config_key`/`changed_by`/`reason`/`changed_at` are `NOT NULL`: every governance-relevant change has a key, an actor, a recorded reason (the same "every record of this kind must carry a reason" rule already enforced for `workflow_instances`' `state.transitioned` event), and a time.
- Two indexes beyond what §9.2 explicitly lists (no "Indexes:" line exists for it either): `config_key` (the natural "history of changes to this key" query) and `changed_at DESC` (mirrors every other table's own recency index).
- **Migration `0005_governance_config_changes.py`**: does not create or drop the `governance` schema itself — `0004_governance_audit_log.py` already owns that; this migration only adds/drops its own table.
- 2 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: the documented columns/PK are all present; a `NULL` `old_value_digest` (a config key's first-ever value) and a `NULL` `new_value_digest` (a key's removal) are both genuinely accepted, proving the nullability reasoning is correct, not just assumed. `_EXPECTED_GOVERNANCE_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test) now includes `config_changes`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **185 tests passing** (132 unit + 53 integration).

### Stage A – `platform.event_outbox` and `platform.idempotency_keys` (Schema and Migration Only)
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/persistence/platform_schema.py`**, mirroring `governance_schema.py`'s introduction exactly: its own `MetaData` (schema `platform`), combined into `kernel/alembic/env.py`'s `target_metadata` list alongside `workflow`/`governance`. `event_outbox` — `outbox_id` PK, `event_type`, `schema_version`, `payload` (jsonb), `trace_id`, `created_at`, `dispatched_at` (nullable), `attempts` — and `idempotency_keys` — `key` PK, `principal_id`, `request_digest`, `response` (jsonb), `status_code`, `created_at`, `expires_at` — both column-for-column per data_model.md §10.
- **`platform.schema_metadata` (the third table §10 names) is deliberately not created.** Before writing any code, I flagged that §10 gives it no column list at all — only a purpose description ("Alembic revision plus `index_generation` counters for retrieval"), with an ambiguous plural ("counters," not "a counter"). Asked how to proceed rather than inventing a shape; the answer was to defer it entirely and ship the two fully-specified tables now. It remains an open item in §4, not silently dropped.
- **`event_outbox.attempts` defaults to `0`** (`NOT NULL`, `server_default="0"`), the same "counter starts at zero" reasoning already applied to `workflow_instances.total_tokens`/`total_cost_usd` — a freshly outboxed event has not been attempted yet. `dispatched_at` is nullable: unset until the relay dispatches it.
- **No `CHECK` constraint on `event_outbox.event_type`**, the same open-ended-set reasoning already applied to `workflow_events.event_type` and `audit_log.event_type` — this table can outbox any of the same event types those tables already carry.
- **`idempotency_keys`' columns are all `NOT NULL`**, since §10 marks none of them nullable — this table's documented shape is "write the row once the response is known," not a two-phase write (unlike, say, `approvals`, which genuinely has a pending-then-decided lifecycle). `expires_at`'s "(24 h)" note is a TTL duration a future writer computes (`created_at` + 24 hours), not a schema-level default — no `GENERATED` column, matching how `workflow_leases.expires_at` is likewise a plain application-set column.
- **No foreign keys on either table** — both are cross-cutting infrastructure (any event, any request), not scoped to a specific row elsewhere, the same reasoning already applied to `audit_log`/`config_changes`.
- **Migration revision id hit a real limit, caught before it became a latent bug**: the originally-drafted id, `0006_platform_event_outbox_and_idempotency_keys` (47 characters), failed at migration time with `StringDataRightTruncationError: value too long for type character varying(32)` — Alembic's own `alembic_version.version_num` bookkeeping column defaults to `VARCHAR(32)`, and every prior revision id in this chain happened to fit only because none had been long enough to hit it. Renamed to `0006_platform_outbox_idempotency` (32 characters, at the limit) and re-verified the full up/down/up cycle actually applies.
- **No writer for either table**: `event_outbox` is ADR-0012's transactional outbox, but the Event Bus itself (in-process pub/sub, outbox relay) doesn't exist yet; `idempotency_keys` has no writer because no HTTP route yet needs idempotent-replay of a mutating request.
- 5 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: documented columns/PK for both tables; `attempts` genuinely defaults to `0` and `dispatched_at` genuinely accepts `NULL` on insert. `_EXPECTED_PLATFORM_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test, now also asserting the `platform` schema itself appears/disappears correctly) is `{"event_outbox", "idempotency_keys"}`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **188 tests passing** (132 unit + 56 integration).

### Stage A – `trace.artifacts` and `trace.links` (Schema and Migration Only)
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/persistence/trace_schema.py`**, mirroring `governance_schema.py`/`platform_schema.py`'s introduction exactly: its own `MetaData` (schema `trace`), combined into `kernel/alembic/env.py`'s `target_metadata` list (now four schemas: `workflow`, `governance`, `platform`, `trace`). `artifacts` — `artifact_key` PK, `artifact_type`, `external_id`, `title`, `location`, `version` — and `links` — `link_id` PK, `source_key`, `relationship`, `target_key`, `confidence`, `created_by`, `created_by_type`, `created_at`, `closed_at` (nullable) — both column-for-column per data_model.md §8.
- **Four `CHECK` constraints, one per documented closed value list**: `artifacts.artifact_type` (ten values), `links.relationship` (seven), `links.confidence` (three), `links.created_by_type` (three) — the same "canonical list is documented, so enforce it" reasoning already applied throughout `workflow`/`governance`.
- **`links.source_key`/`target_key` get real foreign keys to `artifacts.artifact_key`** — the first departure from the "no FK" pattern every `governance`/`platform` table followed. Those tables had no single natural target (polymorphic or unscoped pointers); here, a traceability link's source and target are unambiguously artifact keys, and §8 says impact analysis walks recursive CTEs over `links` — a link pointing nowhere real would silently corrupt that traversal.
- **The documented `UNIQUE (source_key, relationship, target_key) WHERE closed_at IS NULL` rule is a partial unique index** (`sa.Index(..., unique=True, postgresql_where=...)`), not a plain `UniqueConstraint` — standard SQL `UNIQUE` constraints cannot carry a `WHERE` clause. This is what lets the same triple be closed and later re-asserted without colliding with its own closed history, while still forbidding two simultaneously open links for one triple. Verified behaviorally, not just structurally: inserted one open link, confirmed a second open link for the identical triple is rejected, then closed the first and confirmed a new open link for the same triple is accepted.
- **Nullability**: only `closed_at` is nullable (§8 marks it so explicitly — the same "explicit `NULL` is the only nullability signal" convention already followed for `governance`/`platform`); every other column in both tables is `NOT NULL`.
- **No writer**: no Traceability Engine exists yet to run the recursive-CTE impact analysis §8 describes, and nothing writes a traceability link yet either.
- 4 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: documented columns/PK/`CHECK` constraints for both tables; the foreign key genuinely rejects a link naming a nonexistent artifact; the partial unique index genuinely allows reopening a closed triple while rejecting a second simultaneously-open one. `_EXPECTED_TRACE_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test, now also asserting the `trace` schema itself appears/disappears correctly) is `{"artifacts", "links"}`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **192 tests passing** (132 unit + 60 integration).

### Stage A – `catalog.workflow_definitions` Only (Schema and Migration Only)
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/persistence/catalog_schema.py`**, mirroring `governance_schema.py`/`platform_schema.py`/`trace_schema.py`'s introduction exactly: its own `MetaData` (schema `catalog`), combined into `kernel/alembic/env.py`'s `target_metadata` list (now five schemas: `workflow`, `governance`, `platform`, `trace`, `catalog`). Only `workflow_definitions` is defined — the other five tables data_model.md §5 documents for `catalog` (`packs`, `pack_state_transitions`, `agents`, `tools`, `prompts`) are explicitly out of scope for this step, per the approved bullet.
- **Why this one table, out of six**: `persistence/schema.py`'s own module docstring has deferred a foreign key from `workflow_instances`/`workflow_events` to `catalog.workflow_definitions` since the very first migration, purely because the table didn't exist yet. Adding it here unblocks that retrofit — as a distinct, later step; this step does not perform the retrofit itself and does not touch `persistence/schema.py` or any `workflow`-schema migration.
- **Column-for-column per §5**: `definition_id` PK, `pack_id`, `version`, `graph` (jsonb), `inputs_schema`, `outputs_schema`, `declared_permissions`, `validated_at`. §5's compact row format only explicitly marks `graph` as `(jsonb)`; `inputs_schema`/`outputs_schema`/`declared_permissions` are typed `JSONB` here too, by direct analogy to every other structured/nested field already in this persistence layer and to `WorkflowDefinition.inputs`/`outputs` in `workflow_engine/models.py`, which hold the same conceptual content (JSON Schema documents) the same way.
- **Every column is `NOT NULL`** — §5 marks none nullable, the same convention already followed for `governance`/`platform`/`trace`. `validated_at` in particular is `NOT NULL`: data_model.md §5 states "Prompt and workflow-definition versions are immutable: a change creates a new version row," read as "a row is written once a version exists as a validated artifact," not written first and validated later. Verified behaviorally: an insert providing every column succeeds; omitting `validated_at` is rejected.
- **No foreign key on `pack_id`** (would reference `catalog.packs`, which doesn't exist — deferred for the identical reason `persistence/schema.py` already defers its own FKs); **no `CHECK` constraint** (no enum-like column with a documented closed value list here, unlike `workflow_instances.status`/`approvals.status`).
- **No writer**: nothing in this codebase persists a pack yet — `ManifestLoader` reads and validates a manifest file, it does not write one to the database.
- **Migration revision id kept short again**: `0008_catalog_wf_definitions` (27 characters), applying the lesson from `0006_platform_outbox_idempotency` (Alembic's `alembic_version.version_num` defaults to `VARCHAR(32)`) before hitting it a second time rather than after.
- 2 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: documented columns/PK are all present; a full row inserts successfully, and omitting any one column (`validated_at`) is rejected. `_EXPECTED_CATALOG_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test, now also asserting the `catalog` schema itself appears/disappears correctly) is `{"workflow_definitions"}`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **194 tests passing** (132 unit + 62 integration).

### Stage A – `catalog.prompts` (Schema and Migration Only)
- **`catalog_schema.py`**: added `prompts`, column-for-column per data_model.md §5 — `prompt_id` PK, `pack_id`, `version`, `content`, `input_schema`, `content_hash`. Same module as `workflow_definitions`, same `catalog` `MetaData`; still only 2 of the 6 tables §5 documents (`packs`, `pack_state_transitions`, `agents`, `tools` remain out of scope). No writer — schema and migration only, exactly as approved.
- **`content` is typed `sa.Text`, not `JSONB`** — the one type decision here that diverges from `workflow_definitions`' all-`JSONB` structured fields. `prompt_engine.md` §12 ("Storage Format and Cache Boundary") states a prompt is stored as a raw Markdown template file, not a structured document — "the file content is exactly what is rendered." `input_schema` *is* `JSONB` (the expected-variables JSON Schema, the same reasoning already applied to every other JSON Schema field in this persistence layer). `content_hash` is a plain text digest, the same role `governance.audit_log.row_hash` plays for audit rows — and its very purpose is named in `prompt_engine.md` itself: "so what was actually sent is verifiable after the fact."
- **No `CHECK` constraint, no foreign key on `pack_id`** — identical reasoning to `workflow_definitions`: no enum-like column exists here, and `catalog.packs` doesn't exist yet to reference.
- **This step does not touch `workflow_definitions`, its open `definition_id` versioning question, or the deferred FK retrofit** — flagged in the previous step's report, still unresolved, still not invented here.
- 2 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: documented columns/PK are all present; a full row inserts successfully, and omitting `content_hash` is rejected. `_EXPECTED_CATALOG_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test) is now `{"workflow_definitions", "prompts"}`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **196 tests passing** (132 unit + 64 integration).

### Stage A – `catalog.tools` (Schema and Migration Only)
- **`catalog_schema.py`**: added `tools`, column-for-column per data_model.md §5 — `tool_id` PK, `pack_id`, `version`, `trust_tier`, `input_schema`, `output_schema`, `required_permissions`. Same module as `workflow_definitions`/`prompts`, same `catalog` `MetaData`; now 3 of the 6 tables §5 documents (`packs`, `pack_state_transitions`, `agents` remain out of scope — the first two additionally state no primary key at all in §5, which would need resolving before either could be added without inventing one). No writer — schema and migration only, exactly as approved.
- **`trust_tier` gets a `CHECK` constraint grounded in the existing `TrustTier` enum**, not a fresh interpretation: `ai_os_kernel.workflow_engine.tool.TrustTier` already declares exactly `tier1_sandboxed`/`tier2_trusted`, and those are the two values enforced. The values are duplicated as literals in both `catalog_schema.py` and the migration rather than importing `TrustTier` — the same choice `workflow_steps.step_type`'s `_STEP_TYPES` already made for `StepType` in `persistence/schema.py`, for two reasons: the `persistence` layer never imports from `workflow_engine` (that dependency direction already runs the other way), and a migration must stay a frozen historical record of what it enforced at the time it ran, not a live reflection of an enum that could change later.
- **`input_schema`/`output_schema` are `JSONB`** (JSON Schema documents, matching `ai_os_kernel.workflow_engine.tool.Tool`'s own `output_schema: dict[str, Any]`); **`required_permissions` is `JSONB`** too, the same "a list is structured data" reasoning already applied to `workflow_definitions.declared_permissions`.
- **Every column is `NOT NULL`** (§5 marks none nullable); **no foreign key on `pack_id`** (`catalog.packs` doesn't exist yet, identical deferral to `workflow_definitions`/`prompts`).
- **Scope held exactly as fenced**: no `packs`/`pack_state_transitions`, no FK retrofit, no tool-execution changes — this only adds a database table describing tools, `ToolStepExecutor`/`EchoTool` are untouched.
- 2 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: documented columns/PK/`trust_tier` `CHECK` constraint are all present; both documented `trust_tier` values are accepted and a third, invalid value is genuinely rejected. `_EXPECTED_CATALOG_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test) is now `{"workflow_definitions", "prompts", "tools"}`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **198 tests passing** (132 unit + 66 integration).

### Stage A – `catalog.agents` (Schema and Migration Only)

Same module, same `catalog` `MetaData`; this is now every *cleanly specified* table §5 documents (4 of 6) — `packs`/`pack_state_transitions` remain out of scope, both still missing a stated primary key in §5. No writer — schema and migration only, exactly as approved.

- **Noted, not treated as a conflict**: the in-process `ai_os_kernel.workflow_engine.agent.Agent` Protocol deliberately has no `input_schema` field (no per-step input-mapping mechanism exists yet to validate against — a runtime-contract deferral, already documented in that module). This catalog table still gets `input_schema` because it documents what a pack's *manifest* declares about an agent, independent of what today's trivial in-process invocation path actually uses.
- **Every column `NOT NULL`** (§5 marks none nullable); **no foreign key on `pack_id`** (`catalog.packs` doesn't exist yet, identical deferral to every other `catalog` table so far); **no `CHECK` constraint** (no enum-like column here).
- **Scope held exactly as fenced**: no `packs`/`pack_state_transitions`, no FK retrofit, no agent-invocation changes — this only adds a database table describing agents, `Agent`/`EchoAgent`/`AgentStepExecutor` are untouched.
- 2 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: documented columns/PK are all present; a full row inserts successfully, and omitting `required_tools` is rejected. `_EXPECTED_CATALOG_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test) is now `{"workflow_definitions", "prompts", "tools", "agents"}`.
