### Stage A/B – `evaluation.run_manifests` (Schema and Migration Only)
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/persistence/evaluation_schema.py`**, mirroring `governance_schema.py`/`platform_schema.py`/`trace_schema.py`/`catalog_schema.py`'s introduction exactly: its own `MetaData` (schema `evaluation`), combined into `kernel/alembic/env.py`'s `target_metadata` list (now six schemas: `workflow`, `governance`, `platform`, `trace`, `catalog`, `evaluation`). Only `run_manifests` is defined — the other five tables data_model.md §6 documents (`experiments`, `experiment_runs`, `metrics`, `gate_results`, `llm_calls`) are explicitly out of scope for this step.
- **Column-for-column per §6**: `run_manifest_id` PK, `workflow_id`, `manifest` (jsonb), `manifest_hash`. Every column is `NOT NULL` — §6 marks none nullable, the same convention already followed throughout. `manifest` is `JSONB` (a structured "pinned-conditions bundle" per ADR-0022, matching data_model.md §2's own JSONB convention for structured payloads); `manifest_hash` is a plain text digest, the same role `governance.audit_log.row_hash`/`catalog.prompts.content_hash` already play. No `CHECK` constraint: no enum-like column exists here.
- **`workflow_id` gets a real foreign key — the first departure, for a `catalog`/`evaluation`-family table, from the "target doesn't exist yet, defer it" pattern**: unlike every `catalog` table's still-missing `packs` target, `workflow_instances` already exists, so nothing is deferred here. This is also the first schema module to *import* a table from `persistence/schema.py` rather than only being imported by it — a persistence-to-persistence reference, distinct from and never confused with the persistence-importing-`workflow_engine` direction this codebase never takes.
- **This step does not retrofit the reverse, already-deferred foreign key** noted in `persistence/schema.py`'s own docstring (`workflow_instances.run_manifest_id` → `evaluation.run_manifests`) — that remained a distinct, later step at the time (now done, see the next entry below), and this step did not touch `persistence/schema.py` or any `workflow`-schema migration.
- **No writer**: §6 describes `run_manifests` as "the complete pinned-conditions bundle required by ADR-0022 (Reproducibility over Determinism)" — no Evaluation Engine exists yet to assemble or write one.
- 3 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: documented columns/PK are all present; the foreign key genuinely rejects a `run_manifests` row referencing a nonexistent `workflow_id`; a row referencing a real, pre-inserted `workflow_instances` row is genuinely accepted. `_EXPECTED_EVALUATION_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test, now also asserting the `evaluation` schema itself appears/disappears correctly) is `{"run_manifests"}`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **203 tests passing** (132 unit + 71 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.22%**.

### Stage A/B – `workflow_instances.run_manifest_id` Foreign Key Retrofit
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/persistence/cross_schema_foreign_keys.py`**: attaches the foreign key `persistence/schema.py` has deferred since the baseline migration — `workflow_instances.run_manifest_id` → `evaluation.run_manifests.run_manifest_id` (data_model.md §4.1: "FK → `evaluation.run_manifests`"). Both prerequisites already existed before this step: the column (nullable, since the first migration) and the target table (`evaluation.run_manifests`, previous step).
- **Why a new module rather than an inline `sa.ForeignKey(...)` in `schema.py`**: `evaluation_schema.py` already imports `workflow_instances` from `schema.py` (for its own `run_manifests.workflow_id` FK, previous step). Having `schema.py` import `evaluation_schema.py` back, at module level, to reach a real `run_manifests.c.run_manifest_id` `Column` object, would be a genuine circular import — confirmed empirically before writing any code (a standalone script reproducing the two-`MetaData` shape) that a plain **string**-based cross-`MetaData` `ForeignKey` (e.g. `"evaluation.run_manifests.run_manifest_id"`) is not a viable escape hatch either: SQLAlchemy raises `NoReferencedTableError` the moment anything compiles or resolves it, because string-form FK resolution only ever searches the *referencing* column's own `Table.metadata.tables`, never a different `MetaData` object. A real `Column` object reference (not a string) is required for it to compile, and only one of the two modules can safely import the other's real object without a cycle — `evaluation_schema.py` already claimed that direction.
- **The module is a standalone composition step**, imported by neither `schema.py` nor `evaluation_schema.py`, so importing it is never itself part of any cycle: `register_workflow_run_manifest_foreign_key()` is called once from `kernel/alembic/env.py`, after all six schema modules' `MetaData` objects are already imported — mirroring the explicit, idempotency-guarded `configure_tracing()`/`configure_metrics()` composition calls already established in `bootstrap.py`. Idempotency-guarded the same way: SQLAlchemy raises if a same-named constraint (`fk_workflow_instances_run_manifest_id`) is appended to a `Table` twice, and `register_workflow_run_manifest_foreign_key()` may legitimately be called more than once in one process.
- **`schema.py`**: `run_manifest_id`'s `sa.Column(...)` definition itself is unchanged (still plain `sa.Text, nullable=True`) — a code comment now points to `cross_schema_foreign_keys.py` for where its FK is actually attached. The module docstring's "foreign keys omitted" note is narrowed to the two FKs still genuinely deferred: `catalog.workflow_definitions` (blocked on the open `definition_id` versioning ambiguity) and `evaluation.experiments` (table does not exist yet) — `evaluation.run_manifests` is no longer in that list.
- **New migration `0013_workflow_run_manifest_fk.py`**: a single `op.create_foreign_key(...)` / `op.drop_constraint(...)` pair (`source_schema="workflow"`, `referent_schema="evaluation"`) — an `ALTER TABLE ADD CONSTRAINT` on an already-existing table, not a `create_table`. Does not touch `experiment_id` or `definition_id` — neither is in scope for this step and both remain deferred for the reasons already on record.
- **The resulting relationship is a genuine mutual/circular foreign key at the database level**, not just in Python: `run_manifests.workflow_id` (`NOT NULL`) → `workflow_instances.workflow_id`, and now also `workflow_instances.run_manifest_id` (nullable) → `run_manifests.run_manifest_id`. This is valid and common in Postgres precisely because the second column is nullable: a real writer would insert the `workflow_instances` row first (`run_manifest_id` left `NULL`), then the `run_manifests` row (referencing the now-existing `workflow_id`), then `UPDATE workflow_instances SET run_manifest_id = ...` — proven directly in the new tests below, not just asserted.
- **No writer, no new table, no other schema change.** `run_manifest_id` still has no application code populating it — schema and migration only, exactly as approved.
- 1 new unit test (`tests/unit/kernel/persistence/test_cross_schema_foreign_keys.py`, new file and new `tests/unit/kernel/persistence/` package — the first unit test for anything in `persistence/`, since every other table in that package is pure declarative schema exercised only against real Postgres; `register_workflow_run_manifest_foreign_key()` is genuine Python logic, the same reasoning that already justifies unit-testing `configure_tracing()`/`configure_metrics()`): calling `register_workflow_run_manifest_foreign_key()` twice attaches the constraint exactly once, with the correct column and target.
- 2 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: the foreign key genuinely rejects a `workflow_instances` row whose `run_manifest_id` names a nonexistent manifest; the insert-then-insert-then-update sequence described above is genuinely accepted end to end, with the final value read back correctly.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **206 tests passing** (133 unit + 73 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.25%** (`cross_schema_foreign_keys.py`, `schema.py`: both 100%).

### Stage A/B – `evaluation.experiment_runs` (Schema and Migration Only)
- **`evaluation_schema.py`**: added `experiment_runs`, column-for-column per data_model.md §6 — `run_id` PK, `experiment_id`, `workflow_id`, `variant_key`, `model_alias`, `resolved_model_id`, `replicate_index`, `served_from_cache` (bool, explicitly `NOT NULL` in §6 itself), `status`. Same module as `run_manifests`, same `evaluation` `MetaData`; now 2 of the 6 tables §6 documents (`experiments`, `metrics`, `gate_results`, `llm_calls` remain out of scope). No writer — schema and migration only, exactly as approved.
- **Chosen over `metrics`, the other candidate named in the approved step, for a concrete reason, not a coin flip**: `metrics.metric_value` is documented only as `numeric`, with no stated precision/scale — a real decision this step would have had to invent without a specification to ground it (unlike `total_cost_usd`'s `Numeric(14, 6)`, which had a clear existing analogue). `metrics.run_id` also references `experiment_runs.run_id` — the very table this step adds — so building `metrics` first would have meant deferring that FK anyway. `experiment_runs` has neither gap: every column is an unambiguous scalar, and its own two FK targets (`workflow_instances`, real; `evaluation.experiments`, deferred) mirror an already-established pattern exactly.
- **`replicate_index` is `Integer`, everything else besides `served_from_cache` is `Text`**: §6's own text — "`replicate_index` and `runs_per_variant` exist because comparisons must report variance across repeated runs" — describes an ordinal count, not a measurement, so no precision/scale ambiguity like `metrics.metric_value`'s. Every column is `NOT NULL` (§6 marks none nullable, the same convention followed throughout).
- **`workflow_id` gets a real foreign key to `workflow_instances`, `experiment_id` gets none** — the identical asymmetry already established for `run_manifests.workflow_id` vs. every `catalog` table's deferred `pack_id`: the target of `workflow_id` already exists, the target of `experiment_id` (`evaluation.experiments`) does not.
- **No `CHECK` constraint on `status`**: §6 gives it no documented value list, the same open-ended reasoning already applied to `workflow_steps.status`/`workflow_events.event_type`.
- **Two indexes beyond the FK columns' own convention**: `workflow_id` (mirrors every other table's own FK index) and `experiment_id` (the natural "all runs for this experiment" query, the same reasoning already used for `config_changes.config_key` — even though this column carries no FK yet).
- **Migration `0014_evaluation_experiment_runs.py`**: does not create or drop the `evaluation` schema itself — `0012_evaluation_run_manifests.py` already owns that; this migration only adds/drops its own table. Revision id kept short again: 31 characters, under the 32-character `alembic_version.version_num` limit.
- 3 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: documented columns/PK are all present; the foreign key genuinely rejects a row referencing a nonexistent `workflow_id`; a full row inserts successfully and omitting `served_from_cache` is rejected. `_EXPECTED_EVALUATION_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test) is now `{"run_manifests", "experiment_runs"}`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **209 tests passing** (133 unit + 76 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.26%**.

### Stage A/B – `evaluation.experiments` (Schema and Migration Only) + `experiment_runs.experiment_id` FK Retrofit
- **`evaluation_schema.py`**: added `experiments`, column-for-column per data_model.md §6 — `experiment_id` PK, `name`, `description`, `definition_id`, `definition_version`, `variables` (jsonb), `pinned_conditions` (jsonb), `runs_per_variant` (int, explicitly `NOT NULL` in §6 itself), `status`, `created_by`. Same module, same `evaluation` `MetaData`; now 3 of the 6 tables §6 documents (`metrics`, `gate_results`, `llm_calls` remain out of scope — `metrics` specifically deferred a second time, its `metric_value numeric` precision/scale still undocumented). No writer — schema and migration only, exactly as approved.
- **`variables`/`pinned_conditions` are `JSONB`** (§6's own parenthetical calls `variables` "what is deliberately varied" and marks both jsonb); **`runs_per_variant` is `Integer`** (a count, the same reasoning as `experiment_runs.replicate_index`); every other column is `Text`. Every column is `NOT NULL` (§6 marks none nullable). No `CHECK` constraint on `status`: same open-ended reasoning as `workflow_steps.status`/`experiment_runs.status`.
- **No foreign key on `definition_id`/`definition_version` — deliberately, and explicitly not this step's decision to make.** These are the same conceptual pair already flagged ambiguous on `workflow_instances` (data_model.md §4.1 vs. §5: `workflow_instances` treats `definition_id` as stable across versions; §5 gives `catalog.workflow_definitions` a single `definition_id` PK, implying a new one per version). `persistence/schema.py` and `catalog_schema.py` both already defer this identical foreign key for the identical reason; `experiments` keeps that deferral consistent rather than re-deciding it fresh.
- **Two indexes beyond the primary key**: `definition_id` (the natural "all experiments run against this workflow definition" query — the same reasoning already applied to every `catalog` table's own `pack_id` index despite carrying no foreign key either) and `status` (the natural "all running/active experiments" query, mirroring `workflow_instances.status`/`approvals.status`).
- **`experiment_runs.experiment_id` retrofit, done in this same step**: unlike `workflow_instances.run_manifest_id` → `evaluation.run_manifests` (a genuinely cross-module retrofit needing `cross_schema_foreign_keys.py` to avoid a circular import), `experiment_runs` and `experiments` already share one module and one `MetaData` — so the constraint is attached directly in `evaluation_schema.py` via `experiment_runs.append_constraint(sa.ForeignKeyConstraint(...))`, right after `experiments` is defined. No new wiring module needed. This is exactly the "trivial, fully-safe, no ambiguity" retrofit the approved step allowed for.
- **Two migrations**: `0015_evaluation_experiments.py` (creates the table, does not touch the `evaluation` schema itself — `0012` already owns that) and `0016_experiment_runs_exp_fk.py` (a single `ALTER TABLE ... ADD CONSTRAINT`, mirroring `0013_workflow_run_manifest_fk.py`'s shape but same-schema, so no cross-schema `source_schema`/`referent_schema` complexity beyond naming both as `evaluation`).
- **Two pre-existing tests needed a small fix, not a design change**: `test_evaluation_experiment_runs_requires_an_existing_workflow_instance` and `test_evaluation_experiment_runs_requires_all_columns` (from the previous step) both inserted `experiment_runs` rows with an `experiment_id` that didn't exist in any `experiments` row — harmless before this step (no FK existed yet), but would now also trip the new `experiment_id` foreign key. Fixed by inserting a real `evaluation.experiments` row first in each, so each test again isolates exactly the one constraint it's about.
- 6 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: `experiments`' documented columns/PK; a full `experiments` row inserts successfully and omitting `runs_per_variant` is rejected; the `experiment_runs.experiment_id` foreign key genuinely rejects a row referencing a nonexistent experiment; a row referencing a real, pre-inserted `experiments` row is genuinely accepted. `_EXPECTED_EVALUATION_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test) is now `{"run_manifests", "experiment_runs", "experiments"}`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **213 tests passing** (133 unit + 80 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.27%**.

### Stage A/B – `evaluation.gate_results` (Schema and Migration Only)
- **`evaluation_schema.py`**: added `gate_results`, column-for-column per data_model.md §6 — `result_id` PK, `workflow_id`, `step_id`, `gate_id`, `gate_version`, `status`, `severity`, `metrics` (jsonb), `messages` (jsonb), `duration_ms`. Same module, same `evaluation` `MetaData`; now 4 of the 6 tables §6 documents (`metrics`, `llm_calls` remain out of scope). No writer — schema and migration only, exactly as approved.
- **Chosen over `metrics`/`llm_calls` for a concrete, checked reason, as directed**: `duration_ms` is an unambiguous `Integer`, unlike `metrics.metric_value`'s undocumented `numeric` precision/scale (deferred a third time now). `llm_calls` was also checked and found buildable but with more invented judgment calls than `gate_results` needs: its `cost_usd` has no stated type at all (would need inferring by analogy to `workflow_instances.total_cost_usd`), and its `agent_id`/`prompt_id` could now technically FK to `catalog.agents`/`catalog.prompts` — a real decision about whether those tables' id formats actually line up, not resolved in passing. `gate_results` has neither gap.
- **`metrics`/`messages` are `JSONB`** (§6 marks both explicitly); **`duration_ms` is `Integer`**; every other column is `Text`. Every column is `NOT NULL` (§6 marks none nullable). No `CHECK` constraint on `status` or `severity`: §6 gives neither a documented value list — deliberately not extended into inventing a severity taxonomy (e.g. info/warning/error) that §6 does not state, the same open-ended reasoning already applied to `workflow_steps.status`/`experiment_runs.status`.
- **`workflow_id` gets the by-now-standard real foreign key** to `workflow_instances`; **`step_id`, `gate_id`, `gate_version` get none** — `step_id` for the identical reason `workflow_events.step_id`/`approvals.step_id` carry none (no table persists the set of declared step ids a definition's steps must belong to), `gate_id`/`gate_version` because no Quality Gate Engine or gate-registry table exists yet.
- **Two indexes beyond the primary key, mirroring `approvals`** — the closest existing table in shape (also `workflow_id` + `step_id` + a lifecycle `status` column): `workflow_id` (standard FK index) and `status` (the natural "all failed/pending gate results" query, mirroring `workflow_instances.status`/`approvals.status`). `step_id` is deliberately left unindexed, following `approvals`' own precedent of leaving its own `step_id` column unindexed too.
- **New migration `0017_evaluation_gate_results.py`**: does not touch the `evaluation` schema itself (`0012` already owns that). Revision id kept short: 28 characters.
- 3 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: documented columns/PK are all present; the foreign key genuinely rejects a row referencing a nonexistent `workflow_id`; a full row inserts successfully and omitting `duration_ms` is rejected. `_EXPECTED_EVALUATION_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test) is now `{"run_manifests", "experiment_runs", "experiments", "gate_results"}`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **216 tests passing** (133 unit + 83 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.28%**.

### Stage A/B – Documentation Decisions: definition_id Versioning, catalog.packs PK, metrics/llm_calls Precision and FKs (No Code)
- **`docs/08_database/data_model.md` updated** — the authoritative source of truth, per six decisions approved by the user, recorded verbatim with no additions beyond them:
  1. `evaluation.metrics.metric_value` → `NUMERIC(20,6)` (§6).
  2. `evaluation.llm_calls.cost_usd` → `NUMERIC(18,6)` (§6).
  3. `evaluation.llm_calls.agent_id` → real FK to `catalog.agents.agent_id` (§6).
  4. `evaluation.llm_calls.prompt_id` → real FK to `catalog.prompts.prompt_id` (§6).
  5. `catalog.packs` primary key → `pack_id` (text PK) (§5).
  6. `definition_id` versioning → `definition_id` is stable across versions, `version` is separate, and `catalog.workflow_definitions`' primary key is the composite `(definition_id, version)` — `workflow_instances.definition_id`/`definition_version` (§4.1) form a composite foreign key against exactly that pair, resolving the tension between the two tables that every report since the `catalog.workflow_definitions` step flagged as open.
- **A seventh, explicitly *not* decided item was kept open, not resolved by inference**: `catalog.pack_state_transitions` still has no documented primary key. The approved decisions named only `catalog.packs`' PK — extending that to `pack_state_transitions` as well would have been inventing a decision beyond what was approved, so §5 now states plainly that this second gap remains, distinct from the first.
- **A note was added to §6** explaining why `metric_value`/`cost_usd`'s precisions diverge from §2's general `NUMERIC(14,6)` money convention, so a future reader sees an approved exception rather than an apparent inconsistency.
- **`docs/19_roadmap/implementation_status.md` updated** (this document) — §2, this subsection, §4's gap table, and §6, per the same six decisions.
- **No other documents needed changes.** Checked `docs/07_api/api_architecture.md` (references `definition_id`/`definition_version` as already-separate fields — already consistent with the resolution, nothing to change), `docs/03_architecture/kernel/capability_manager.md`, `docs/03_architecture/capability_framework/manifest_schema.md`, and `docs/02_requirements/functional/functional_requirements.md` (all reference `pack_id` only as a manifest/identifier concept, never as a database primary-key detail) — none required edits.
- **No code, no migrations, no tests this step, as approved.** `catalog.workflow_definitions` still has its original single-column `definition_id` primary key in the actual database (migration `0008_catalog_wf_definitions`, unaltered); `catalog.packs`, `catalog.pack_state_transitions`, `evaluation.metrics`, and `evaluation.llm_calls` still do not exist as tables. This step only changed what the documentation specifies should be built.

### Stage A/B – `catalog.packs` (Schema and Migration Only)
- **`catalog_schema.py`**: added `packs`, column-for-column per data_model.md §5's now-resolved shape — `pack_id` **PK**, `version`, `state`, `manifest` (jsonb), `sdk_version`, `min_kernel_version`, `installed_at`, `activated_at`, `health` (jsonb). Same module as the other four `catalog` tables, same `catalog` `MetaData`; now 5 of the 6 tables §5 documents — `pack_state_transitions` remains the only unbuilt one, still blocked on its own undocumented primary key, a distinct gap the approved decisions did not resolve. No writer — schema and migration only, exactly as approved.
- **`state` gets a `CHECK` constraint** against the eight-value canonical lifecycle both data_model.md §5 and `capability_manager.md` §4 document identically (`capability_manager.md` §4 states it is "the single authority" for that list) — the same "canonical list is documented, so enforce it" reasoning already applied throughout. No existing Python enum to mirror instead of duplicating literals: the Capability Manager component itself is still an empty, not-yet-built stub, so `_PACK_STATES` is sourced directly from the two architecture documents, the same way `_INSTANCE_STATUSES`/`_APPROVAL_STATUSES` were before any corresponding enum existed either.
- **`installed_at`, `activated_at`, and `health` are nullable — the one table in `catalog_schema.py` that isn't "every column `NOT NULL`"**: `capability_manager.md` §4 documents `discovered` as the *first* lifecycle state, before `installed`/`activated` are ever reached, so a freshly-discovered pack's row cannot yet have real values for either timestamp. Reasoned by the identical analogy already used for `workflow_instances.completed_at` (also not explicitly marked `NULL` in its own compact data_model.md row, also nullable in the actual implementation for the same "hasn't happened yet" reason). `health` is nullable for the same reason `workflow_instances.error`/`outputs` are: `capability_manager.md` §3 scopes health monitoring to "activated packs," so there's nothing to hold before then. `version`, `state`, `manifest`, `sdk_version`, `min_kernel_version` are all `NOT NULL` — required manifest content, present the moment a pack is first discovered.
- **No foreign key retrofit on `workflow_definitions`/`prompts`/`tools`/`agents`' own `pack_id` columns**, even though `packs` now exists and those FKs are technically no longer blocked by a missing target — explicitly out of scope for this step ("no other catalog table changes"). A distinct, later step, the same shape as the `run_manifests`/`experiment_runs` FK retrofits already done in `evaluation_schema.py`.
- **One index beyond the primary key**: `state` (the natural "all packs in a given lifecycle state" query, mirroring `workflow_instances.status`/`approvals.status`/`experiments.status`/`gate_results.status`). `version` is not indexed — no table in this module indexes a bare `version` column, and `packs` doesn't depart from that.
- **New migration `0018_catalog_packs.py`**: does not touch the `catalog` schema itself (`0008_catalog_wf_definitions` already owns that). Revision id kept short: 18 characters.
- 4 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: documented columns/PK are all present; the `state` `CHECK` constraint accepts two documented values (`discovered`, `activated`) and genuinely rejects an invalid one; a full row inserts successfully and omitting `sdk_version` is rejected; a freshly-discovered pack's `installed_at`/`activated_at`/`health` genuinely read back as `NULL`, proving the nullability reasoning is correct, not just asserted. `_EXPECTED_CATALOG_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test) is now `{"workflow_definitions", "prompts", "tools", "agents", "packs"}`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **220 tests passing** (133 unit + 87 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.29%**.

### Stage A/B – `evaluation.llm_calls` (Schema and Migration Only) — `evaluation` Schema Complete
- **`evaluation_schema.py`**: added `llm_calls`, column-for-column per data_model.md §6 — `call_id` PK, `workflow_id`, `step_id`, `agent_id`, `prompt_id`, `prompt_version`, `model_alias`, `provider`, `model_id`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, `cache_write_tokens`, `cost_usd`, `latency_ms`, `stop_reason`, `retries`, `fallback_used`, `degradations` (jsonb). Same module, same `evaluation` `MetaData`. This is the sixth and last table §6 documents — the `evaluation` schema is now complete.
- **`cost_usd` is `NUMERIC(18, 6)` and `agent_id`/`prompt_id` are real foreign keys** to `catalog.agents.agent_id`/`catalog.prompts.prompt_id` — the three documentation decisions this table was blocked on for two prior steps, now implemented exactly as recorded in data_model.md §6.
- **`input_tokens`/`output_tokens`/`cache_read_tokens`/`cache_write_tokens` are `BigInteger`**, mirroring `workflow_instances.total_tokens` — the only existing precedent for a token-count column in this persistence layer, applied to four per-call counts instead of one running total. `latency_ms` is `Integer`, the identical type/reasoning as `gate_results.duration_ms`. `retries` is `Integer` (a count, mirroring `experiment_runs.replicate_index`/`experiments.runs_per_variant`). `fallback_used` is `Boolean` (mirroring `experiment_runs.served_from_cache`). Every column is `NOT NULL` (§6 marks none nullable, and unlike `catalog.packs`, nothing here is "hasn't happened yet" — a completed call has real values for all of these).
- **The first `evaluation`-schema foreign keys pointing at `catalog`, not `workflow`**: `agent_id`/`prompt_id` import real `Column` objects from `catalog_schema.py` (`agents.c.agent_id`, `prompts.c.prompt_id`) — the identical persistence-to-persistence pattern already used for `workflow_id` → `persistence.schema.workflow_instances`, just against a different sibling module. No circular import risk: `catalog_schema.py` does not import `evaluation_schema.py` (or `schema.py`) back, so this import is one-directional regardless of which module happens to load first — a materially simpler situation than the `workflow_instances.run_manifest_id` retrofit, which needed a dedicated wiring module specifically because `schema.py` and `evaluation_schema.py` import each other in opposite directions.
- **`step_id` gets no foreign key**, the identical reason `workflow_events.step_id`/`approvals.step_id`/`gate_results.step_id` carry none. No `CHECK` constraint on `stop_reason`: §6 gives it no documented value list.
- **Three indexes beyond the primary key**: `workflow_id`, `agent_id`, `prompt_id` — every real foreign key column in this table gets one, the same convention already applied to every other real FK in this persistence layer. `step_id` is not indexed, following the same precedent every other table's own `step_id` column already leaves unindexed.
- **New migration `0020_evaluation_llm_calls.py`**: does not touch the `evaluation` schema itself (`0012` already owns that). Revision id kept short: 25 characters.
- 6 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: documented columns/PK are all present; each of the three foreign keys — `workflow_id`, `agent_id`, `prompt_id` — is tested in isolation (the other two references pre-inserted as real rows, so only the one under test can be the cause of the rejection); a full row inserts successfully and omitting `degradations` is rejected; and a row referencing a real workflow instance, agent, and prompt together is genuinely accepted, read back and confirmed. `_EXPECTED_EVALUATION_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test) is now `{"run_manifests", "experiment_runs", "experiments", "gate_results", "metrics", "llm_calls"}` — all six tables data_model.md §6 documents.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **230 tests passing** (133 unit + 97 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.31%**.

### Stage A/B – `catalog.workflow_definitions` Composite Primary Key — FK Retrofit Attempted, Reverted, and Reported
- **`catalog_schema.py`**: `workflow_definitions`' primary key changed from single-column `definition_id` to composite `(definition_id, version)`, exactly the documented resolution of the `definition_id` versioning ambiguity. New migration `0021_catalog_wf_definitions_pk.py`: a single, atomic `ALTER TABLE ... DROP CONSTRAINT workflow_definitions_pkey` / `ADD CONSTRAINT pk_workflow_definitions PRIMARY KEY (definition_id, version)` pair — not an expand/migrate/contract sequence, because this table has no writer anywhere in this codebase (no live data to migrate), and the change is safe by construction regardless: a single-column `definition_id` primary key already guarantees uniqueness on that column alone, and a value unique on one column is trivially still unique on that column plus another, so no row that could exist under the old constraint could ever violate the new, strictly broader composite one. Fully reversible (downgrade restores the original single-column constraint by name).
- **The paired foreign-key retrofit — `workflow_instances.definition_id`/`definition_version` → `catalog.workflow_definitions (definition_id, version)` — was implemented, tested, and then deliberately reverted.** This is the one significant finding of this step, surfaced per this session's "stop and report if something is inconsistent" rule:
  - The composite FK was straightforward to express in SQLAlchemy (`sa.ForeignKeyConstraint` on `workflow_instances`, importing the real `workflow_definitions` `Column` objects directly from `catalog_schema.py` — safe, one-directional, no circular import, since `catalog_schema.py` never imports back).
  - Before committing to it, the full test suite was run with the FK in place. Result: **37 of 39 tests in `tests/integration/workflow_engine/` failed** with `ForeignKeyViolationError` — including the most basic instance-creation test. Every failure traced to the same cause: `catalog.workflow_definitions` has no writer anywhere in this codebase (`ManifestLoader` only reads/validates a manifest file in memory; nothing persists one), while `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository.create()` — real, already-accepted application code that has worked this way since the very first migration — writes a `workflow_instances` row with an arbitrary `definition_id`/`definition_version` pair unconditionally, with no matching catalog row ever able to exist.
  - This is categorically different from every other FK retrofit done in this implementation log (`run_manifest_id`, `experiment_id`, `agent_id`/`prompt_id`): those all pointed at tables with no existing write path to break. This one points at a column pair that real, tested, working code writes to constantly, with no corresponding writer for the referenced side. Adding it would not be a schema-only change — it would silently and immediately break the Workflow Engine's ability to create any instance at all, a functional regression far outside "schema and migration only" and outside this step's approved scope (no agent/tool/LLM/runtime changes).
  - **Reverted**: the `sa.ForeignKeyConstraint` was removed from `workflow_instances` in `schema.py`, and the migration that would have added it (`0022_workflow_definition_fk.py`) was deleted rather than merged, after confirming via a second full test run that reverting restores all 39 Workflow Engine tests to passing.
  - Both `schema.py` and `catalog_schema.py`'s docstrings were updated to record this precisely: the versioning ambiguity that used to block this FK is resolved, but the FK is now blocked by a *different*, newly-discovered reason (no catalog writer, no instance-creation-time validation) — not silently left looking "still pending the old reason."
- **A separate, unrelated documentation inaccuracy was found and corrected while investigating**: earlier reports (and both modules' own docstrings, until this step) described the deferred FK as owed to "`workflow_instances`/`workflow_events`." Checked directly against data_model.md §4.2 while implementing this step: `workflow_events` has never had a `definition_id` or `definition_version` column at all. Only `workflow_instances` ever needed this foreign key. Both docstrings now say so explicitly.
- 2 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: `test_catalog_workflow_definitions_composite_primary_key` (two rows sharing the same `definition_id` with different `version` values are both accepted — the entire point of this migration — while a genuine duplicate `(definition_id, version)` pair is still rejected); the existing `test_catalog_workflow_definitions_matches_the_documented_columns` updated to assert the composite PK. Round-trip test now covers 21 migrations.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **231 tests passing** (133 unit + 98 integration — includes all 39 Workflow Engine integration tests passing, confirming the revert is complete and correct). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.31%**.

### Stage A/B – `catalog.pack_state_transitions` (PK Decision Recorded and Implemented) — `catalog` Schema Complete
- **`docs/08_database/data_model.md` §5 updated first**, per the approved decision: `catalog.pack_state_transitions` | `transition_id` PK, `pack_id` (FK → `catalog.packs.pack_id`), `from_state`, `to_state`, `reason`, `actor`, `occurred_at`. The note that used to flag this as "a distinct, still-open documentation gap, not resolved by the `packs` decision" now records the resolution instead. No other decision recorded — the other five columns kept exactly as already documented, per the constraint.
- **`catalog_schema.py`**: added `pack_state_transitions`, column-for-column per the now-resolved §5 row. Same module, same `catalog` `MetaData`; this is the sixth and last table §5 documents — the `catalog` schema is now complete. No writer — schema and migration only, exactly as approved.
- **`from_state`/`to_state` get a `CHECK` constraint against the exact same `_PACK_STATES` tuple already defined for `packs.state`** — reused directly (not duplicated, since both tables share this one module), the identical "canonical list is documented, so enforce it" reasoning: a pack transition is necessarily *from* one pack lifecycle state *to* another, the same eight-value domain `packs.state` already enforces, not a new list.
- **`occurred_at` has no server default** — application-supplied, the same deliberate divergence from the general "database-generated" timestamp convention already used for `workflow_events.occurred_at`, `governance.audit_log.occurred_at`, and `evaluation.metrics.recorded_at`. Every column is `NOT NULL` (§5 marks none nullable) — including `from_state`, deliberately: whether the very first-ever transition into `discovered` should log a real `from_state` value or something else is a question for whoever eventually builds the writer, not resolved here by making the column nullable on spec.
- **`pack_id` gets a real foreign key to `packs.pack_id`** — the one FK this step's approved decision explicitly required. Same-module, same-`MetaData` retrofit pattern already used for `experiment_runs.experiment_id` → `experiments.experiment_id`: trivial, no import-ordering hazard. Does **not** retrofit `workflow_definitions`/`prompts`/`tools`/`agents`' own `pack_id` columns into foreign keys against `packs.pack_id` — those remain out of scope, unchanged.
- **Two indexes beyond the primary key**: `pack_id` (the standard real-FK index every real FK in this persistence layer already gets) and `occurred_at DESC` (the natural "this pack's history, in order" query, mirroring `governance.audit_log.occurred_at`/`governance.config_changes.changed_at`'s own recency indexes).
- **New migration `0022_pack_state_transitions.py`**: does not touch the `catalog` schema itself (`0008_catalog_wf_definitions` already owns that). Revision id kept short: 27 characters.
- 4 new integration tests in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`: documented columns/PK are all present; the foreign key genuinely rejects a row referencing a nonexistent `pack_id`; both `CHECK` constraints genuinely reject an invalid `from_state`/`to_state` while accepting documented values; a full row inserts successfully and omitting `occurred_at` is rejected. `_EXPECTED_CATALOG_TABLES` (used by the up/down/up round-trip test) is now all six tables data_model.md §5 documents.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **235 tests passing** (133 unit + 102 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.32%**.

### Stage A/B – Minimal `catalog.workflow_definitions` Writer + `workflow_instances` Composite FK (Now Safely Enforced)
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/definition_catalog.py`**: `WorkflowDefinitionCatalog` (`Protocol`) + `SqlWorkflowDefinitionCatalog`, the only implementation. `register(definition, pack_id)` is an **upsert keyed on `(definition_id, version)`** (`ON CONFLICT ... DO NOTHING`, not `DO UPDATE` — data_model.md §5's "versions are immutable" means a second registration of an already-recorded version is a no-op, not a silent overwrite). Deliberately **not** a Capability Manager or Manifest Loader extension — the smallest seam that lets `WorkflowInstanceService` register a definition before creating an instance that references it.
- **Column mapping from `WorkflowDefinition` to the five columns this writer supplies** (`definition_id`/`version` are the PK): `pack_id` — not a field on `WorkflowDefinition` at all (a workflow file has no notion of which pack it belongs to; that's manifest-level knowledge) — supplied explicitly by the caller. `inputs_schema`/`outputs_schema` — `definition.inputs`/`definition.outputs` directly. `graph` — `model_dump(mode="json", by_alias=True, exclude={"id","version","inputs","outputs"})`: everything the definition validated, minus what already has its own column — the least-invented reading that keeps the durable catalog record complete (nothing dropped, which matters for the reproducibility this table exists for, ADR-0022). `declared_permissions` — `WorkflowDefinition` has no permissions field of its own today; stored as an empty JSONB array rather than guessed from `agents`/`requiredTools` component references. `validated_at` — application-supplied (no server default), the same reasoning already applied to every other "when did this actually happen" column in this persistence layer.
- **The `workflow_instances` → `catalog.workflow_definitions` composite foreign key is now enforced** (new migration `0023_workflow_definition_fk.py`) — the same constraint attempted and reverted two steps ago. It is safe now, verified by actually running the full test suite with it in place (not assumed): all 39 `tests/integration/workflow_engine/` tests pass, because `WorkflowInstanceService.create_instance()` now registers the definition, every call, before the instance insert ever runs.
- **`WorkflowInstanceService` changes**: constructor takes a third dependency, `WorkflowDefinitionCatalog`; `create_instance()` takes a new required `pack_id: str` (validated via new `validate_pack_id`, mirroring `validate_principal`), and calls `definition_catalog.register(...)` before `repository.create(...)`. The two writes are deliberately **not** one atomic transaction — registration's own upsert is idempotent, so a registration that commits and an instance-creation that then fails for an unrelated reason leaves nothing incorrect behind, unlike `workflow_instances`/`workflow_events` (data_model.md §4, which does require one transaction). New `WorkflowDefinitionRegistrationError` (mirrors `WorkflowInstanceCreationError`) wraps a registration failure cleanly, never a bare stack trace — satisfying the approved shape's "FK violation / missing definition must fail cleanly" requirement.
- **Every caller of `WorkflowInstanceService`/`create_instance()` across the codebase was updated** — 2 unit test files (`test_service.py`: new `_FakeDefinitionCatalog`, 2 new tests proving registration happens before the repository call and that a registration failure stops instance creation; `test_advance_runner.py`: a minimal never-exercised fake to satisfy the constructor) and 8 integration test files (7 needed `pack_id`/`SqlWorkflowDefinitionCatalog` added to their own `create_instance`/`WorkflowInstanceService(...)` call sites; `test_state_transitions.py` needed no code change, since it calls the repository directly). A new `tests/integration/workflow_engine/conftest.py` autouse fixture ensures the one shared test definition (`se.product_creation`/`1.0.0`) is registered before every test in that package — idempotent, so it doesn't matter whether a given test goes through the service (which would also register it) or the repository directly (which would not).
- **`tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`** gained an equivalent autouse fixture for its own shared `def_test`/`1.0.0` literal (used by ~16 raw-SQL `workflow_instances` inserts testing other tables' foreign keys), re-run before every test (not just once) because the round-trip test's own downgrade-to-`base` cycle would otherwise wipe it.
- 2 new integration tests in `test_migrations.py` (FK genuinely rejects an unregistered definition; genuinely accepts a registered one) and a new `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_definition_catalog.py` (3 tests: the writer's documented columns and reasoned content; idempotent re-registration; two versions of the same `definition_id` are distinct rows).
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **242 tests passing** (135 unit + 107 integration, including all 39 Workflow Engine integration tests). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.25%**.

### Stage B – LLM Gateway: Minimal First Slice (Protocol + Reduced Contract + Trivial Implementation)
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/llm_gateway/` package (previously an empty stub)**: `models.py` (`LLMRequest`, `LLMResponse`, `Message`, `MessageRole`, `StopReason`, `UsageRecord`) and `gateway.py` (`LLMGateway` Protocol, `EchoLLMGateway`). Mirrors the exact "one Protocol, one trivial implementation" reduction already used for `ai_os_kernel.workflow_engine.agent`/`.tool` — not the full fourteen-subsystem architecture `llm_gateway.md` §3 documents.
- **`LLMRequest` keeps exactly four of §4's documented fields**: `model_alias` (required, never a literal model id — ADR-0002's entire LLM-agnosticism mechanism, not part of any deferred subsystem), `messages`, `system` (reduced from the documented `SystemBlock[]` to a plain `str | None` — a list only matters for per-block prompt-cache breakpoints, and caching is deferred), and `max_output_tokens` (a request's own basic length bound, not the deferred Budget Enforcer's workflow/experiment cost ceiling). Excluded, each tied to an explicitly deferred subsystem: `tools`/`tool_choice` (tool-calling), `response_format` (structured output), `thinking`/`effort` (capability-negotiated routing), `stream` (streaming), `cache_hints` (caching), `budget`/`timeout_seconds` (budgets), `metadata`/`require_capabilities` (capability negotiation).
- **`LLMResponse` reduces `content` from the documented `ContentBlock[]` (`text | tool_call | thinking`) to a plain `str`** — only `text` has meaning without tool-calling/thinking, both deferred; a one-variant discriminated union would be unnecessary structure. **`stop_reason` reduces the documented five-value enum to two** (`end_turn`, `max_tokens`) — the only two a trivial, provider-free implementation can honestly produce; `tool_use`/`refusal`/`pause_turn` each need a subsystem that doesn't exist yet, and `stop_details` (which only ever accompanies `refusal`) is dropped with it. **`usage` is kept complete** against §5's full `UsageRecord` shape (including `cache_read_tokens`/`cache_write_tokens`, matching `evaluation.llm_calls`' own already-built columns, and `retries`/`fallback_used`) — a trivial implementation can honestly report zero/false for all of them, a true statement rather than an invented one. Excluded from the response: `served_from_cache` (caching), `degradations` (capability negotiation), `raw` (no real provider payload exists to pass through).
- **`EchoLLMGateway` genuinely exercises `max_output_tokens`, not a half-finished field**: it treats the field as a character-count length bound (explicitly documented as *not* a real token count — §12 forbids approximating a real tokenizer for actual accounting, "an approximation would corrupt both budget enforcement and cost reporting") and truncates its echoed content when it would exceed that bound, reporting `stop_reason = max_tokens` when truncation occurred and `end_turn` otherwise — both values in the reduced enum are genuinely reachable, not dead code. `usage.input_tokens`/`output_tokens` stay honestly `0` regardless (no real tokenizer was used for *counting*, only the field's length-cap *effect* is real). `latency_ms` is a real wall-clock measurement, not invented. `provider`/`model_id`/`model_version` are clearly-fake, honestly-labelled sentinels (`"echo"`/`"echo-1"`/`"1.0.0"`), the same transparency `EchoAgent`/`EchoTool` already use.
- **No writer to `evaluation.llm_calls`.** Observability is one of §3's fourteen subsystems and explicitly out of scope; nothing here produces a call worth recording. **No wiring into `DispatchingStepExecutor` or the Workflow Engine at all** — this step is a standalone Protocol + implementation, not yet consumed anywhere, matching the approved scope precisely ("Prompt Engine / Context Manager integration beyond what is strictly required for this minimal contract" — nothing was required, so nothing was added).
- 12 new unit tests: `tests/unit/kernel/llm_gateway/test_models.py` (6 — a well-formed request is accepted; `system` is optional; blank `model_alias`, empty `messages`, and non-positive `max_output_tokens` are all rejected; the request is genuinely frozen) and `test_gateway.py` (6 — completion echoes the conversation's *last* message regardless of earlier turns; content is truncated exactly at the bound with `stop_reason = max_tokens`; content exactly at the bound is *not* truncated; `usage` is honestly zeroed/false throughout; `provider`/`model_id` are the documented echo sentinels).
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **254 tests passing** (147 unit + 107 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.45%** (`llm_gateway/models.py`, `llm_gateway/gateway.py`: both 100%).

### Stage B – LLM Gateway: Minimal `evaluation.llm_calls` Writer, Wired to `EchoLLMGateway.complete()`
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/llm_gateway/call_recorder.py`**: `LLMCallRecorder` Protocol + `SqlLLMCallRecorder`, the minimal write path for `evaluation.llm_calls` — the Observability subsystem named in `llm_gateway.md` §3/§13 ("Every call emits a span and writes one `evaluation.llm_calls` row"), reduced to exactly that one write. No span, no metrics — "no full observability platform work beyond this writer" is this step's own fence.
- **Deliberately not an `LLMGateway`-conforming decorator.** A wrapper that both calls an inner gateway and records the result would need per-call correlation context (workflow, step, agent, prompt) that the already-shipped `LLMRequest` does not carry (`metadata: TraceContext` was excluded when the request contract was reduced last step). Composed explicitly by the caller instead: call `gateway.complete(request)` first, then `recorder.record(...)` with the workflow/step context the caller already has — exactly "after a successful `EchoLLMGateway.complete()` call, write one `evaluation.llm_calls` row," the approved step's own wording, not a hidden extra abstraction. `LLMRequest`/`LLMResponse`/`EchoLLMGateway` themselves are untouched.
- **Column mapping — every value comes directly from the already-produced `LLMRequest`/`LLMResponse`/`UsageRecord`, nothing invented**: `model_alias` ← `request.model_alias`; `provider`/`model_id` ← `response.provider`/`response.model_id`; `stop_reason` ← `response.stop_reason.value`; every `UsageRecord` field maps to its identically-named column. `degradations` has no source in the reduced `LLMResponse` (capability negotiation was deferred at the contract level too) — stored as an empty JSONB array, the same "no field maps to this yet" honesty already used for `catalog.workflow_definitions.declared_permissions`. `call_id` is a new prefixed ULID (`call_`, a reasoned choice — data_model.md's own prefix list is illustrative, not exhaustive, the same reasoning already used for `lease_`/`stp_`).
- **`agent_id`/`prompt_id`/`prompt_version` are optional on the call path, exactly as approved, but the schema makes them `NOT NULL` with real foreign keys** (data_model.md §6) — there is no way to store a partial or absent combination today. All three are required together: passing fewer than three raises `LLMCallRecordingError` (new, mirrors `WorkflowDefinitionRegistrationError`'s shape) before any database access, clearly, rather than surfacing a raw `NOT NULL` violation. When all three are provided, they must reference real `catalog.agents`/`catalog.prompts` rows — enforced by the foreign keys `evaluation.llm_calls` already had (built when that table itself was created), not re-implemented here; a violation is still wrapped in the same clean error. `workflow_id`/`step_id` are always required, matching the schema exactly, and are blank-checked before any database access too.
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/llm_gateway/errors.py`** (`LLMCallRecordingError`) and **`kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/llm_gateway/ids.py`** (`new_call_id()`), mirroring the equivalent `workflow_engine` modules exactly.
- **No unit test file for `SqlLLMCallRecorder`** — consistent with the precedent set for `SqlWorkflowDefinitionCatalog` (a thin SQL writer, exercised only against real Postgres, ADR-0015). 7 new integration tests in `tests/integration/llm_gateway/test_call_recorder.py`: a real `EchoLLMGateway.complete()` call is recorded with every column correct; providing some but not all of `agent_id`/`prompt_id`/`prompt_version` is rejected; providing none of the three is rejected identically (not silently accepted as a special "no correlation" case); blank `workflow_id`/`step_id` are each rejected before any database access; an unknown `agent_id` and an unknown `prompt_id` are each rejected by the real foreign keys.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **261 tests passing** (147 unit + 114 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.53%** (`llm_gateway/call_recorder.py`, `errors.py`, `ids.py`: all 100%).

### Stage B – Prompt Engine: Minimal First Slice (Protocol + Reduced Render Contract + Trivial Implementation)
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/prompt_engine/` package (previously an empty stub)**: `models.py` (`PromptRenderRequest`, `PromptRenderResponse`), `errors.py` (`PromptNotFoundError`, `PromptVariableMissingError`), `renderer.py` (`PromptEngine` Protocol, `InMemoryPromptEngine`). Mirrors the exact "one Protocol, one trivial implementation" reduction already used for `LLMGateway`/`EchoLLMGateway` and `Agent`/`Tool` — not the full six-subsystem architecture `prompt_engine.md` §5 documents (Prompt Registry, Version Manager, Template Renderer, Variable Validator, Prompt Resolver, Composition Engine, Observability Hook).
- **`PromptRenderRequest`/`PromptRenderResponse` model the runtime call envelope of §7's Resolution Flow (steps 1-5), not the persisted §6 Prompt Contract.** `prompt_id` and `version` are both required, and `version` is caller-supplied explicitly — no Version Manager/Prompt Resolver resolution logic, no config-driven defaults, no experiment-forced pinning (§9), all deferred. `variables` carries the runtime substitution values. The response echoes `prompt_id`/`version` back alongside the rendered `content`, exactly §7 step 5 ("rendered prompt is returned together with metadata (prompt id + version)"). Excluded, each tied to an explicitly deferred subsystem: `cache_boundary_index` (§12's stable/volatile split for the LLM Gateway's prompt-cache breakpoint, ADR-0025 — caching is this step's own fence); the full §6 Prompt Contract fields (`name`, `description`, `owner`, `template`, `input_schema`, `tags`, `metadata` — these describe the *stored* prompt, which needs real `catalog.prompts` loading, also deferred); role/alias-based requests (Prompt Resolver).
- **`InMemoryPromptEngine` holds templates in a plain `dict[tuple[str, str], str]` supplied at construction time — not `catalog.prompts`** (that table exists, schema-only, with no reader; "real prompt catalog loading beyond what is strictly needed" is this step's own explicit fence). Rendering does plain `{{variable_name}}` substitution only — §12 requires "a restricted, non-executing syntax: variable substitution and simple conditionals only," and even the documented "simple conditionals" half of that restricted syntax is deferred here, not invented as a second template language. Required variables are derived from the placeholders literally present in the template text (not a declared `input_schema`, which would need the same deferred catalog loading); a missing one raises `PromptVariableMissingError` naming every missing variable at once, before any substitution runs. An unregistered `(prompt_id, version)` pair raises `PromptNotFoundError`. Extra variables not referenced by the template are silently ignored — no over-strict rejection was documented, so none was invented.
- **Deliberately not a proxy to the LLM Gateway.** `system_architecture.md`'s "LLM Abstraction Path" is explicit: "The Prompt Engine returns a rendered prompt to the caller; it is not a proxy in the call path. The caller then invokes the LLM Gateway." Nothing here constructs an `LLMRequest` or calls `LLMGateway` — mirroring the same "composed explicitly by the caller" shape already established for `LLMCallRecorder` (not an `LLMGateway`-conforming decorator, for the analogous reason that the correlation context it would need isn't carried by the reduced request contract).
- 11 new unit tests (no integration test file — a pure in-memory component with no I/O, the same precedent as `EchoLLMGateway`/`EchoAgent`/`EchoTool`): `test_models.py` (5 — a well-formed request is accepted; `variables` defaults to empty; blank `prompt_id`/`version` are each rejected; the request is genuinely frozen) and `test_renderer.py` (6 — variables are substituted correctly; a template with no placeholders needs no variables; extra unreferenced variables are ignored; an unknown prompt id or version raises and names it; a missing required variable raises and names it; no substitution is attempted when a variable is missing).
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **272 tests passing** (158 unit + 114 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.67%** (`prompt_engine/models.py`, `errors.py`, `renderer.py`: all 100%).

### Stage B – PromptedCompletionService: Minimal Explicit Composition of the Prompt Engine and the LLM Gateway
- **New top-level module `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/prompted_completion.py`** (`PromptedCompletionResult`, `PromptedCompletionService`) — a single bare module, not a new subpackage, the same "composition, not a subsystem" shape `bootstrap.py` already uses for whole-app wiring. Proves `system_architecture.md`'s "LLM Abstraction Path" end to end: `PromptEngine.render()` → build an `LLMRequest` from the rendered content → `LLMGateway.complete()`.
- **Not a `Protocol`, not a new `Agent`.** A composition of two already-injected Protocols needs no seam of its own (ADR-0004: an interface is justified only when a second implementation is real or clearly imminent). It does not implement or extend `ai_os_kernel.workflow_engine.agent.Agent` — "full Agent runtime redesign" is explicitly out of scope this step, and no real Agent exists yet to be the documented caller; nothing in the Workflow Engine calls this service.
- **Rendered content becomes the sole user message** (`Message(role=USER, content=render_response.content)`). `PromptEngine`'s reduced contract renders one block of text with no documented system/user split at this stage — that split needs the deferred Context Manager, so none was invented. `model_alias`/`max_output_tokens` are supplied by the caller directly, since a rendered prompt could never produce them.
- **Recording via the existing `evaluation.llm_calls` writer is optional, per call, and adds no new logic to `LLMCallRecorder` itself.** `call_recorder: LLMCallRecorder | None` is an optional constructor dependency; a given call is recorded only when the caller also supplies `workflow_id`/`step_id` for that call — omitting either just leaves that one call unrecorded, it does not raise. The recorder's own already-tested "`agent_id`/`prompt_id`/`prompt_version` must all be provided together" rule is untouched: `prompt_id`/`prompt_version` are always supplied automatically (echoed straight from the render response), so recording without an `agent_id` still fails inside the recorder exactly as it already did.
- **`PromptedCompletionResult`** bundles both artifacts a caller needs — `render: PromptRenderResponse` and `response: LLMResponse` — a frozen Pydantic model, consistent with every other reduced-contract shape in this codebase.
- 6 new unit tests (`tests/unit/kernel/test_prompted_completion.py`), composed entirely from already-existing trivial implementations (`InMemoryPromptEngine`, `EchoLLMGateway`) plus a new in-memory fake recorder — no real database, no real provider, no integration test file needed: a prompt renders and completes correctly; the rendered content reaches the LLM request as its user message (proven via `EchoLLMGateway`'s own truncation behavior); a template needing no variables works with none supplied; a call is recorded when a recorder and `workflow_id`/`step_id` are both given; no recording is attempted with no recorder configured; no recording is attempted when `workflow_id`/`step_id` are withheld even though a recorder is configured.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **278 tests passing** (164 unit + 114 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.72%** (`prompted_completion.py`: 100%).

### Stage B – Prompt Engine: Minimal `catalog.prompts`-Backed Reader (`SqlPromptCatalog`, a Second `PromptEngine` Implementation)
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/prompt_engine/catalog.py`**: `SqlPromptCatalog`, a second implementation of the existing `PromptEngine` Protocol, reading a template row from `catalog.prompts` by `(prompt_id, version)` instead of an in-process map. Mirrors `SqlWorkflowDefinitionCatalog`'s shape as closely as a *read* mirrors that module's *write*: a thin SQLAlchemy 2.0 Core component (ADR-0011), no Prompt Registry, no writing/admin API, no Capability Manager extension — a reader/renderer path only, exactly the approved shape.
- **Shared rendering logic extracted**: `render_template()` (plain `{{variable}}` substitution + missing-variable validation) moved out of `InMemoryPromptEngine.render()` into a module-level function in `prompt_engine/renderer.py`, now called by both implementations. The two `PromptEngine` implementations can now only ever differ in *where a template comes from*, never in what rendering means — the concrete justification ADR-0004 asks for a Protocol to earn (a second implementation is now real, not hypothetical).
- **Only `content` is read from `catalog.prompts`.** The table also has `pack_id`/`input_schema`/`content_hash` columns, but this reduced render contract (`PromptRenderRequest`/`PromptRenderResponse`) has no field for any of them yet — reading columns the contract can't expose would be dead data. Required variables still come from the placeholders literally present in `content`, not the row's own `input_schema` — validating against a declared schema remains real prompt catalog loading beyond this step's scope, the same fence `InMemoryPromptEngine` already drew.
- **`PromptNotFoundError`'s docstring generalized** (it now covers "no row" from either implementation, not just the in-memory map) and a new `PromptCatalogError` added for a genuine persistence-layer failure (a `SQLAlchemyError` during the query, distinct from "no such row"), mirroring `WorkflowDefinitionRegistrationError`/`LLMCallRecordingError`'s "fail cleanly" shape.
- **Discovered, not fixed, in this step: `catalog.prompts` still has `prompt_id` alone as its primary key** (data_model.md §5, `catalog_schema.py`) — the identical single-column-PK shape `catalog.workflow_definitions` had before its own composite-key migration. This means two versions of the same `prompt_id` cannot coexist as separate rows today, discovered empirically while writing this step's own integration test (a second version insert raised `UniqueViolationError` on `prompts_pkey`). Not fixed here: a PK migration is a distinct, higher-risk schema change needing its own explicit approval — precisely the precedent already set for `workflow_definitions`. The test was adjusted to prove the reader distinguishes two *different* `prompt_id`s correctly instead (see that test's own docstring for the full explanation); the gap itself is now tracked in §4.
- **No writer, no unit test file** — a thin SQL reader, integration-tested only against real Postgres (ADR-0015), the same precedent as `SqlWorkflowDefinitionCatalog`/`SqlLLMCallRecorder`. 4 new integration tests (`tests/integration/prompt_engine/test_catalog.py`): a real row renders with variables substituted; an unknown `prompt_id` and an unknown `version` of a known `prompt_id` each raise `PromptNotFoundError`; a missing required variable raises `PromptVariableMissingError` and names it; two distinct `(prompt_id, version)` rows render independently.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **282 tests passing** (164 unit + 118 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.65%** (`prompt_engine/catalog.py`: 91% — only the `SQLAlchemyError`-wrapping branch is uncovered, since nothing in this reader triggers a genuine connection-level failure without mocking the database, which ADR-0015 forbids).

### Stage A/B – `catalog.prompts` Composite Primary Key + `evaluation.llm_calls` Prompt FK Retrofit (Closes the Gap Discovered Last Step)
- **`docs/08_database/data_model.md` §5/§6 updated first**: `catalog.prompts` now documents the composite primary key `(prompt_id, version)`, mirroring `catalog.workflow_definitions`' own row exactly; the shared "prompt and workflow-definition versions are immutable" narrative paragraph now covers both tables' composite keys and both downstream composite foreign keys (`workflow_instances` → `workflow_definitions`, `llm_calls` → `prompts`); `evaluation.llm_calls`' table row now documents `prompt_id`/`prompt_version` as a **composite FK** against `catalog.prompts (prompt_id, version)`, replacing the old single-column description.
- **`catalog_schema.py`**: `prompts.version` changed from a plain `nullable=False` column to a second `primary_key=True` column, giving the table the composite `(prompt_id, version)` primary key. **`evaluation_schema.py`**: `llm_calls.prompt_id` no longer carries an inline `sa.ForeignKey(prompts.c.prompt_id)`; a table-level `sa.ForeignKeyConstraint(["prompt_id", "prompt_version"], [prompts.c.prompt_id, prompts.c.version], name="fk_llm_calls_prompt_id_version")` replaces it — the identical pattern already used for `workflow_instances`' own composite FK to `workflow_definitions`. Both modules' docstrings updated with the same "safe by construction, discovered while building `SqlPromptCatalog`" reasoning now also recorded in the migration itself.
- **New migration `0024_catalog_prompts_pk.py`**: unlike the `workflow_definitions`/`workflow_instances` precedent (`0021`/`0023`, kept as two separate, independently-reversible migrations because the foreign key didn't exist yet when the primary key changed), this one **cannot** be split the same way — `evaluation.llm_calls.prompt_id` already carried a live foreign key to `catalog.prompts.prompt_id` (added in `0020_evaluation_llm_calls`), and Postgres refuses to drop a primary key a foreign key still depends on. One atomic migration: drop the old FK (`llm_calls_prompt_id_fkey`) → drop the old PK (`prompts_pkey`) → create the new composite PK (`pk_prompts`) → create the new composite FK (`fk_llm_calls_prompt_id_version`). The exact auto-generated constraint names were verified empirically against a real migrated Postgres container before being hardcoded into `op.drop_constraint(...)` calls, not assumed from naming-convention memory. Fully reversible (`downgrade()` restores both original constraints in reverse order).
- **The composite FK retrofit is a genuine correctness improvement, not just a mechanical necessity**: the old single-column FK only checked that `prompt_id` existed *somewhere* in `catalog.prompts`, never that `prompt_version` matched that row's actual `version`. Verified safe against every existing caller before writing the migration (`SqlLLMCallRecorder`'s callers, `tests/integration/llm_gateway/test_call_recorder.py`, `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py` — all already use matching `version`/`prompt_version` pairs) and confirmed by running the full test suite with the migration in place, not assumed — the same "verify, don't assume" discipline the `workflow_definitions` FK saga established.
- **`tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`**: `test_catalog_prompts_matches_the_documented_columns` updated to assert the composite PK; new `test_catalog_prompts_composite_primary_key` (mirrors the `workflow_definitions` equivalent — two versions of the same `prompt_id` now both accepted, a genuine duplicate `(prompt_id, version)` pair still rejected); new `test_evaluation_llm_calls_requires_a_matching_prompt_version` proves the new composite FK's own correctness improvement — a real `prompt_id` with a `prompt_version` that was never registered is now rejected, which the old single-column FK could not have caught. Round-trip test now covers 24 migrations.
- **`SqlPromptCatalog` needed no code change** — it already queried by `(prompt_id, version)` via a `WHERE` clause, never relying on `prompt_id` alone being unique; the approved shape's "keep SqlPromptCatalog working against the new key shape" requirement was satisfied by the reader's existing design, confirmed by re-running `tests/integration/prompt_engine/test_catalog.py` unchanged.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **284 tests passing** (164 unit + 120 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.65%**.

### Stage A/B – Two Remaining Deliberately-Deferred Foreign Key Retrofits (`evaluation.experiments`, `evaluation.metrics`)
- **`evaluation_schema.py`**: `experiments` now imports `workflow_definitions` from `catalog_schema.py` (added to the existing `agents, prompts` import) and carries an inline `sa.ForeignKeyConstraint(["definition_id", "definition_version"], [workflow_definitions.c.definition_id, workflow_definitions.c.version], name="fk_experiments_definition_id_version")` — the identical composite-FK pattern `workflow_instances` already uses against the same target. `metrics.run_id` gained a plain `sa.ForeignKey(experiment_runs.c.run_id)` (`experiment_runs` is defined earlier in the same module, so no import or ordering issue). Both modules' docstrings updated to record why each was deferred and why each is safe now.
- **New migration `0025_experiments_definition_fk.py`**: purely additive (`op.create_foreign_key`, no prior constraint to drop, unlike `0024`) — `experiments.definition_id`/`definition_version` never carried a foreign key before. Safe against the existing test suite unmodified: every `evaluation.experiments` row `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py` inserts already uses the `(def_test, 1.0.0)` pair that file's own autouse fixture guarantees is registered in `catalog.workflow_definitions` before every test.
- **New migration `0026_metrics_run_id_fk.py`**: also purely additive, but **not safe against the existing test suite unmodified** — discovered by attempting the retrofit and running the full suite first, not assumed. Three tests inserted a `metrics` row referencing `run_id = 'run_1'`, a value with no matching `evaluation.experiment_runs` row anywhere (nothing writes to either table in real application code yet, so there was no risk to any real code path — only to test fixtures). Two of the three needed a real `experiment_runs` row seeded first (and, transitively, a real `experiments` row for *its own* foreign key); the third already expected failure for an unrelated reason (an unregistered `workflow_id`) and needed no change.
- **New `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py` helper `_insert_real_experiment_run`**: seeds a real `evaluation.experiments` row and a real `evaluation.experiment_runs` row referencing it — the minimal chain `metrics.run_id`'s new foreign key requires. Used to fix the two affected metrics tests and to write two new dedicated tests, `test_metrics_run_id_foreign_key_rejects_an_unknown_run`/`test_metrics_run_id_foreign_key_accepts_a_real_run`. Two new dedicated tests were added for the `experiments` retrofit too: `test_experiments_definition_foreign_key_rejects_an_unregistered_definition`/`_accepts_a_registered_definition`. Round-trip test now covers 26 migrations.
- **`docs/08_database/data_model.md` §6 updated**: `experiments`' and `metrics`' table rows now document their new foreign keys; a new narrative paragraph explains both retrofits and why each was deferred until now.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **288 tests passing** (164 unit + 124 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.65%**.

**This closes the last of the standing "still open, not to be invented away" foreign-key retrofit list** carried across the last several steps' reports — `evaluation.metrics.run_id`, `evaluation.experiments.definition_id`/`definition_version`, and (last step) `catalog.prompts`' own composite key are all resolved. The only remaining, deliberately out-of-scope schema item of this kind is `workflow_definitions`/`prompts`/`tools`/`agents`' own `pack_id` columns, still un-FK'd to `catalog.packs.pack_id` (see §4).

### Stage B – Workflow Step Contract: Minimum Invocation Fields (Documentation Only)
- **`docs/03_architecture/workflow/workflow_architecture.md`**: new "Step Contract (Minimum Invocation Fields)" section, placed right after "Supported Step Types." Defines exactly five optional fields a step may declare beyond `id`/`type`: `agentId` (required when `type` is `agent`), `toolId` (required when `type` is `tool`), and `promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias` (optional, `agent` steps only — inputs to that agent's own Prompt Engine render and LLM Gateway call, never consumed by the Workflow Engine itself). Five explicit rules pin down scope: `type` remains the sole discriminator; the agent/tool requiredness above; `promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias` are meaningless on a `tool` step and must not be declared there; the five other step types (`decision`/`parallel`/`sub_workflow`/`quality_gate`/`human_approval`) declare none of these fields and keep their own already-documented contracts unchanged; and these fields identify *what* to invoke, never *how* — no templating, branching, or per-step scripting, consistent with the "no large orchestration language" fence this step was explicitly approved under.
- **`docs/03_architecture/agents/agent_architecture.md`**: one cross-reference sentence added to the Invocation lifecycle, after step 7 — when a step declares `promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias`, those are the values an agent uses at invocation step 4, constructed into its `AgentRequest` at step 2. Deliberately not a restatement of the contract table itself (Constitution Article 1, "Single Source of Truth" — `workflow_architecture.md` remains the one authoritative definition).
- **`docs/08_database/data_model.md` §4.3**: one clarifying paragraph, no schema change — notes that `workflow_steps.agent_id`/`tool_id` will record what a step *actually* invoked once this contract is wired into real execution (still always `NULL` today), and that this table has no `prompt_id`/`prompt_version`/`model_alias` columns of its own — whether a per-step execution record should carry them (mirroring `evaluation.llm_calls`) is left to whoever eventually builds that wiring, not decided here.
- **Field naming**: `agentId`/`toolId`/`promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias` in the YAML-facing docs, matching every other multi-word field already in the Workflow Contract table (`qualityGates`, `humanApprovalPoints`, `failureHandling`, `retryPolicy`) and the existing `_CamelModel` alias-generator pattern (`WorkflowStep.join_policy` ↔ manifest `joinPolicy`) — the eventual Python/DB-side names would be the snake_case mirror (`agent_id`, `tool_id`, `prompt_id`, `prompt_version`, `model_alias`), matching `workflow_steps.agent_id`/`tool_id`'s already-existing column names exactly.
- **`agentId` format** matches the Agent Contract's own `id` field (`<pack_id>/<slug>`, fully qualified) exactly — the same string `catalog.agents.agent_id` already stores; **`toolId`** uses the dot-namespaced convention Coding Standards already assigns to tool/workflow/gate ids. Neither invents a new id shape.
- **Not implemented, deliberately**: `WorkflowStep` (`ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/models.py`) still validates only `id`/`type`/`joinPolicy` — its own docstring's "no document defines a field-level contract... deferred until a doc defines them" note is now stale (a doc does define them, as of this step) but was left untouched, since fixing it would mean editing code in a documentation-only step; the next implementation step should update it alongside adding the fields themselves. `workflow_steps` gained no new columns. `DispatchingStepExecutor`/`EchoAgent`/`EchoTool` are untouched — every Agent-type step still invokes the same `EchoAgent`, ignoring these fields entirely, exactly as before this step.

### Stage B – `WorkflowStep` Validates the Step Contract's Five Invocation Fields (Model/Validation Only)
- **`kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/models.py`**: `WorkflowStep` gained five new optional fields — `agent_id`, `tool_id`, `prompt_id`, `prompt_version`, `model_alias` (camelCase on disk via the existing `_CamelModel` alias generator: `agentId`/`toolId`/`promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias`) — plus a shared blank-check `field_validator` across all five (mirroring every other blank-check already in this codebase) and a new `_invocation_fields_match_step_type` `model_validator`, added alongside the existing `_parallel_step_requires_join_policy` one rather than replacing it. Enforces `workflow_architecture.md`'s Step Contract exactly: `agentId` required and `toolId` forbidden on an `agent` step; `toolId` required and `agentId`/`promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias` forbidden on a `tool` step; all five forbidden on `decision`/`parallel`/`sub_workflow`/`quality_gate`/`human_approval` steps; `promptId`/`promptVersion` must be declared together or not at all. The module docstring's previously-stale "no document defines a field-level contract" note (flagged last step) is now corrected and cites the Step Contract's five rules directly.
- **Model/validation-only, exactly as approved**: no change to `DispatchingStepExecutor`, `AgentStepExecutor`, `ToolStepExecutor`, `EchoAgent`, or `EchoTool` — every Agent-type step still invokes the same `EchoAgent` and every Tool-type step still invokes the same `EchoTool`, regardless of what `agentId`/`toolId` a step now declares. Nothing calls the Prompt Engine or LLM Gateway from a workflow step. `workflow_steps` (the persisted per-execution table) gained no new columns.
- **Every existing test fixture that constructs an `agent`/`tool`-typed step needed an `agentId`/`toolId` value** — discovered systematically by running the full suite after the model change, not assumed: 6 unit test files and 8 integration test files across `tests/unit/kernel/workflow_engine/` and `tests/integration/workflow_engine/` all had at least one bare `{"id": ..., "type": "agent"}`/`{"id": ..., "type": "tool"}` fixture (or a direct `WorkflowStep(...)` construction) predating this contract. All updated to declare a real-shaped id (`se.software_engineering/analyst` for agents, `se.build` for tools) — the same fictitious-but-correctly-shaped values already used elsewhere in this test suite for `catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools` rows. `test_definition_catalog.py`'s own graph-fidelity assertion was extended to include the five new `None`-or-set keys, the same "every field, including None defaults" reasoning already applied when `joinPolicy` was added to that same assertion.
