- **14 new unit tests in `tests/unit/kernel/workflow_engine/test_loader.py`**: an agent step without `agentId` fails clearly; a tool step without `toolId` fails clearly; an agent step declaring `toolId` fails clearly; a tool step declaring `promptId`/`promptVersion` fails clearly; a `human_approval` step declaring `agentId` fails clearly; `promptId` without `promptVersion` fails clearly (and the reverse); a blank (whitespace-only) `agentId` fails clearly; and a full positive case — an agent step declaring `agentId`, `promptId`, `promptVersion`, and `modelAlias` together — loads successfully with every field readable back off the validated `WorkflowStep`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **297 tests passing** (173 unit + 124 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.75%** (`workflow_engine/models.py`: 100%, up from 134 statements with the five new fields and two validators, every branch exercised).

### Stage B – `workflow_steps.agent_id`/`tool_id` Now Populated From a Step's Declared Invocation Fields (Writer-Only)
- **`kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/repository.py`**: in `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository.advance_workflow()`, the `workflow_steps` insert's `agent_id=None, tool_id=None` literals became `agent_id=next_step.agent_id, tool_id=next_step.tool_id` — the two hardcoded values this document has flagged as a known gap since before the LLM Gateway existed. `next_step` is the already-validated `WorkflowStep` the caller resolved and executed moments earlier in the same call, so no new lookup, no new dependency, no new field was needed — the declared values were already in scope, just not read.
- **Writer-only, exactly as approved**: this records what a step *declared*, not what actually ran. `DispatchingStepExecutor`/`AgentStepExecutor`/`ToolStepExecutor`/`EchoAgent`/`EchoTool` are untouched — every Agent-type step still dispatches to the same `EchoAgent` and every Tool-type step still dispatches to the same `EchoTool`, regardless of which `agentId`/`toolId` it names. Resolving a declared id to a *different* real implementation needs a Capability Manager registry that does not exist yet (Stage C) — explicitly not attempted here. No Context Manager, Prompt Engine, or LLM Gateway call was added; `prompt_id`/`prompt_version`/`model_alias` are not written anywhere, since `workflow_steps` has no columns for them (data_model.md §4.3 already flags that as a separate, undecided question).
- **`advance_workflow()`'s own docstring updated** to state this explicitly, so a future reader doesn't mistake "records what was declared" for "records what was dispatched."
- **One existing test fixed, none added**: `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_step_records.py::test_list_steps_returns_the_materialised_records_in_execution_order` previously asserted `step.agent_id is None` for a step that (as of the previous step's fixture updates) already declares a real `agentId` — updated to assert the declared value is now read back correctly. No other test asserted the old always-`NULL` behavior: `test_event_records.py`'s own `event.agent_id is None` check is about the unrelated `workflow_events.agent_id` column (never written by any code path, untouched by this change), and every raw-SQL `workflow_steps` insert in `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py` bypasses this repository method entirely.
- **`docs/08_database/data_model.md` §4.3** updated: `agent_id`/`tool_id` are no longer described as "always `NULL` today" — now described as recording what a step declared, with an explicit note that this is not yet what a step actually ran through.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **297 tests passing** (173 unit + 124 integration, same count as last step — one assertion fixed, none added). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.75%**.

### Stage B – `workflow_steps` Gains `prompt_id`/`prompt_version`/`model_alias` (Completes the Step Contract's Five Fields, Writer-Only)
- **New migration `0027_workflow_steps_prompt.py`**: purely additive — three nullable `sa.Text` columns on `workflow.workflow_steps`. No FK, no new index, mirroring `agent_id`/`tool_id`'s own shape on this same table exactly (added two steps ago). Revision id kept to 26 characters (the first draft, `0027_workflow_steps_prompt_fields`, was 33 — over the `alembic_version.version_num VARCHAR(32)` limit — caught before running any migration, not after).
- **`kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/persistence/schema.py`**: `workflow_steps` gains `prompt_id`, `prompt_version`, `model_alias` (all `sa.Text, nullable=True`), placed next to `agent_id`/`tool_id`. Typed to match `evaluation.llm_calls.prompt_id`/`prompt_version`/`model_alias` exactly (data_model.md §6) — the approved shape's "consistent with `evaluation.llm_calls` where practical." Nullability necessarily differs: `llm_calls` records one completed call that always has these; a step may declare none, some, or all of them.
- **Deliberately no foreign key on `prompt_id`/`prompt_version`** to `catalog.prompts (prompt_id, version)`, unlike `llm_calls`' own composite FK to the same target. Reasoned explicitly, not left implicit: that FK is safe on `llm_calls` because a real call actually happened against an already-rendered prompt; here, the columns merely record what a step *declared*, and no writer exists for `catalog.prompts` in this codebase (only the reader, `SqlPromptCatalog`) — a real FK would make it impossible to ever record a declared `prompt_id` without a matching catalog row, the identical failure mode the `workflow_definitions` FK saga already established as unsafe. This keeps the three new columns consistent with their `agent_id`/`tool_id` siblings, which carry no FK for the identical reason (no writer for `catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools` either).
- **`kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/step_record.py`**: `WorkflowStepRecord` gains `prompt_id: str | None`, `prompt_version: str | None`, `model_alias: str | None`.
- **`kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/repository.py`**: `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository.advance_workflow()`'s `workflow_steps` insert now also copies `next_step.prompt_id`/`next_step.prompt_version`/`next_step.model_alias` — the same writer-only pattern as `agent_id`/`tool_id`, extended to the remaining three Step Contract fields in the same call. Docstring updated to describe all five fields together rather than just two.
- **Writer-only, exactly as approved**: no dispatch change, no Capability Manager registry, no Context Manager, no real Prompt Engine or LLM Gateway call from a workflow step. `DispatchingStepExecutor`/`EchoAgent`/`EchoTool` are untouched.
- **`tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py`**: `test_workflow_steps_matches_the_documented_columns` updated to include the three new columns in its expected column set. **`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_step_records.py`**: one step in the shared test fixture now also declares `promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias`; the existing round-trip test extended to assert those three fields are recorded faithfully for the step that declared them and stay `None` for the step that did not — proving the column addition works without inventing a new test file.
- **`docs/08_database/data_model.md` §4.3** updated: documents all five columns, the shared "declared, not dispatched" caveat, and the no-FK reasoning.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **297 tests passing** (173 unit + 124 integration, same count as last step — one existing test extended, none added). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.75%** (`workflow_engine/step_record.py`: 100%).

### Stage B – Minimal `AgentRegistry`/`ToolRegistry`: Real Dispatch by Declared `agentId`/`toolId`
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/registry.py`**: two Protocols, `AgentRegistry` (`resolve_agent(agent_id) -> Agent`) and `ToolRegistry` (`resolve_tool(tool_id) -> Tool`), kept separate rather than one combined "capability" Protocol — mirroring the codebase's existing separation between `Agent` and `Tool` themselves, and satisfying Interface Segregation (`AgentStepExecutor` only ever needs to resolve agents, never tools, and vice versa). Both methods are `async`, even though the one implementation here does no I/O, the same forward-looking shape already used for `PromptEngine`/`LLMGateway` — a real Capability-Manager-backed registry would plausibly need I/O (checking pack activation state, lazily importing a pack's module).
- **`InMemoryAgentRegistry`/`InMemoryToolRegistry`**: the one trivial implementation for this step — a plain `Mapping[str, Agent]`/`Mapping[str, Tool]` copied at construction time by the composition root (no pack discovery, no activation state, no permissions). Registered instances may still be `EchoAgent`/`EchoTool` — this registry does not care what an `Agent`/`Tool` actually does, only that one exists for a given id. Raises the new `AgentNotRegisteredError`/`ToolNotRegisteredError` (added to `workflow_engine/errors.py`) for an unregistered id, never a bare `KeyError`.
- **Deliberately not the Capability Manager** (kernel_architecture.md: "Controls installation, activation, deactivation, upgrades and health of Capability Packs"). No pack lifecycle, no install/activate state machine, no permissions enforcement — only a lookup from an already-validated declared id to an already-constructed instance. A real Capability-Manager-backed registry is the natural second implementation of these same two Protocols later (Stage C), not attempted here.
- **`AgentStepExecutor`/`ToolStepExecutor` now take a registry instead of a single fixed instance.** Each `execute()` call reads `step.agent_id`/`step.tool_id` (already required and validated by `WorkflowStep` — see the last few steps' own work) and resolves it through the injected registry before invoking, validating output exactly as before. A defensive `ValueError` guards the statically-impossible-but-not-provable case of `step.agent_id`/`tool_id` being `None` despite the step's own type (`WorkflowStep` validation should already have prevented this) — mirroring the existing "cannot execute step of type X" `ValueError` pattern, not silently trusting an invariant blindly.
- **`DispatchingStepExecutor` itself is unchanged** — it still only knows to route by `step.type` to whichever `StepExecutor`-conforming object it was given; it has no knowledge of registries at all. This keeps the "the only place that knows all three executors exist" property intact.
- **Real dispatch by declared id, not real capability discovery** — the approved distinction, made explicit throughout the new module's own docstring: different steps declaring different `agentId`/`toolId` values now genuinely resolve to different registered instances (proven by new tests using distinguishable fakes, not two indistinguishable `EchoAgent`s), but nothing here discovers, installs, activates, or permission-checks a capability. `DispatchingStepExecutor`'s own routing logic is untouched.
- **Every existing test constructing `AgentStepExecutor`/`ToolStepExecutor` with a bare `Agent`/`Tool` instance updated** to wrap it in a registry keyed by the step's own declared id — 2 unit test files and 1 integration test file.
- **New `tests/unit/kernel/workflow_engine/test_registry.py`** (5 tests): resolving a registered agent/tool returns the exact registered instance; resolving an unregistered id raises the new, named error; the registry's constructor copies its argument (mutating the caller's dict afterward does not affect what resolves). **2 new tests each** in `test_agent_step_executor.py`/`test_tool_step_executor.py`: an unregistered id raises clearly; two steps declaring different ids resolve to two distinguishable fakes (a `_NamedAgent`/`_NamedTool` that echoes its own name, proving real per-id routing rather than assuming two `EchoAgent`s "look the same either way").
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **306 tests passing** (182 unit + 124 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.59%** (`workflow_engine/registry.py`: 100%; `step_executor.py`: 95% — the two defensive "step declares no agentId/toolId" `ValueError` branches are unreached, since `WorkflowStep` validation already prevents that state from ever existing).

### Stage B – `catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools`-Backed `SqlAgentRegistry`/`SqlToolRegistry`
- **`kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/registry.py`**: two new classes, `SqlAgentRegistry`/`SqlToolRegistry` — a second implementation of `AgentRegistry`/`ToolRegistry`, mirroring `SqlPromptCatalog`'s shape (SQLAlchemy 2.0 Core, ADR-0011; look up one row by its natural key; fail cleanly if missing or the query itself fails). Each confirms a declared `agentId`/`toolId` is a real, registered row in `catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools` before returning the same trivial `EchoAgent`/`EchoTool` stand-in the in-memory registries already use — `catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools` carry no `entrypoint` column in the current schema, so there is nothing to dynamically load a real implementation from (a genuine, pre-existing gap between the Agent Contract's own required `entrypoint` field and the persisted catalog schema — see "Alignment checkpoint" below).
- **The one honest exception: `catalog.tools.trust_tier` is carried through, not discarded.** `SqlToolRegistry` reads the row's real declared `trust_tier` and constructs the returned `EchoTool` with it, rather than always defaulting to `tier2_trusted`. This means a catalog-declared `tier1_sandboxed` tool is still refused by `ToolStepExecutor`'s existing ADR-0016 sandbox guard even when resolved through this catalog-backed path — silently downgrading a real trust declaration would have quietly defeated an already-built safety guarantee for no reason.
- **`EchoTool` (`workflow_engine/tool.py`) changed from a fixed class attribute to a constructor-settable `trust_tier`**, defaulting to `tier2_trusted` — every existing direct `EchoTool()` construction (no args) behaves identically to before; only `SqlToolRegistry` passes a non-default value. `EchoAgent` needed no equivalent change: nothing on `catalog.agents` is safety-relevant the way `trust_tier` is, and `AgentStepExecutor` enforces no analogous gate.
- **New errors `AgentRegistryError`/`ToolRegistryError`** (`workflow_engine/errors.py`), mirroring `PromptCatalogError`'s role: a genuine persistence-layer failure during lookup, distinct from `AgentNotRegisteredError`/`ToolNotRegisteredError` ("no such id").
- **Still deliberately not the Capability Manager**: no pack lifecycle, no activation-state check against `catalog.packs`, no permissions enforcement — a catalog row existing is the only thing either registry checks, exactly the same boundary the in-memory registries already drew, just against a real table instead of a caller-supplied mapping.
- **New `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_registry.py`** (5 tests, real Postgres, ADR-0015): a registered agent resolves to `EchoAgent`; an unregistered agent id raises `AgentNotRegisteredError`; a registered `tier2_trusted` tool resolves to an `EchoTool` carrying that tier; a registered `tier1_sandboxed` tool resolves to an `EchoTool` honestly carrying *that* tier (proving no silent trust-tier laundering); an unregistered tool id raises `ToolNotRegisteredError`.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **311 tests passing** (182 unit + 129 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.43%** (`registry.py`: 93% — only the `SQLAlchemyError`-wrapping branches are uncovered, the same precedent as `SqlPromptCatalog`'s own 91%, since nothing triggers a genuine connection failure without mocking, which ADR-0015 forbids).

### Stage B – `catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools` Gain a Required `entrypoint` Column (Schema and Migration Only)
- **New migration `0028_catalog_entrypoint.py`**: purely additive — one `TEXT NOT NULL` column on each of `catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools`. Safe unmodified for the identical reason `workflow_steps.prompt_id`/`prompt_version`/`model_alias` were: neither table has a writer anywhere in this codebase (the Manifest Loader validates a manifest file; it does not persist one), so there is no live data a `NOT NULL` addition could violate.
- **Shape resolved from the already-authoritative source, not invented**: `platform_sdk/schemas/manifest.schema.json`'s own `agents[]`/`tools[]` entries already require and validate an `entrypoint` (pattern `^[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_.]*:[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]*$`, documented there as "Python import path, `module.path:ClassName`") — the "what shape should entrypoint be" question flagged as open in last step's own report turned out to already be answered, just not yet mirrored into the catalog schema. `entrypoint` is `sa.Text`, `NOT NULL` (required in the manifest schema, the same "required there → `NOT NULL` here" pattern already followed by every other column on these two tables), with **no `CHECK` constraint** mirroring the manifest's regex: format validation for a patterned string is the Manifest Loader's job at load time, the same convention `agent_id`'s/`tool_id`'s own documented id shapes already follow without a Postgres-level format constraint.
- **`kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/persistence/catalog_schema.py`**: `agents`/`tools` table definitions and docstring updated to match.
- **Schema and migration only, exactly as approved**: no entrypoint-loading logic (dynamic import, construction) was added. `SqlAgentRegistry`/`SqlToolRegistry` (see last step) are unchanged — they still confirm a row exists and return the same trivial `EchoAgent`/`EchoTool` stand-in regardless of what `entrypoint` says.
- **`docs/08_database/data_model.md` §5** updated: both table rows now list `entrypoint`; a new narrative paragraph explains the gap, the shape source, and why no `CHECK` constraint was added.
- **Every existing raw-SQL `INSERT INTO catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools` fixture updated** to supply a value for the new `NOT NULL` column — discovered systematically by running the full suite after the migration, not assumed: 2 test files beyond `test_migrations.py` itself (`tests/integration/llm_gateway/test_call_recorder.py`, `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_registry.py`), plus 7 call sites within `test_migrations.py` (the `catalog_tools`/`catalog_agents` column-set and not-null tests, the trust-tier `CHECK` constraint test, and the shared `_insert_real_agent_and_prompt` helper). Two column-set assertions (`test_catalog_agents_matches_the_documented_columns`, `test_catalog_tools_matches_the_documented_columns`) updated to include `entrypoint`. No new tests needed — this is a schema-only, mechanically-verified change.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **311 tests passing** (182 unit + 129 integration, unchanged — existing fixtures updated, none added). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.43%**.

### Stage B – Minimal Entrypoint Loader for Agent/Tool Construction
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/entrypoint_loader.py`**: `EntrypointLoader.load(entrypoint: str) -> Any`, the smallest possible "resolve `module.path:ClassName`, import it, construct it with no arguments" mechanism — not a plugin framework. The regex mirrors `platform_sdk/schemas/manifest.schema.json`'s own `agents[].entrypoint`/`tools[].entrypoint` pattern exactly (the authoritative shape, not invented here). Every distinct failure mode — a malformed string, an unimportable module, a missing attribute, a name that is not a class, a constructor that raises — becomes one `EntrypointLoadError` (new, `workflow_engine/errors.py`), always chained via `from` when there is an underlying exception. Deliberately **not** a `Protocol`: there is exactly one way to dynamically import a Python module, and no second implementation is real or imminent (ADR-0004) — a fake test double for "how you import a module" is not a capability this codebase has any reason to swap, and this also directly satisfies the step's own "do not invent a large plugin framework" constraint. The loader is synchronous by design (`importlib.import_module` does blocking file I/O); the two async call sites that use it are responsible for running it off-thread.
- **`Agent`/`Tool` (`workflow_engine/agent.py`/`tool.py`) are now `@runtime_checkable` Protocols**, enabling `isinstance()` structural-presence checks against whatever a loaded entrypoint actually constructs — turning "resolved to something structurally unrelated" into a clear registry error at resolution time rather than a confusing failure the first time something calls `.execute()`.
- **`SqlAgentRegistry`/`SqlToolRegistry` (`workflow_engine/registry.py`) now do real dispatch**: each reads the row's declared `entrypoint` (tools also read `trust_tier`), loads and constructs it via `EntrypointLoader` through `asyncio.to_thread` (keeping the blocking import off the event loop), then `isinstance()`-validates the result against `Agent`/`Tool` before returning it — an entrypoint resolving to something that satisfies neither Protocol now raises `AgentRegistryError`/`ToolRegistryError`, not a bare attribute error later. Both constructors gained an optional `loader: EntrypointLoader | None = None` parameter (defaulting to a real `EntrypointLoader()`), the same "inject, but default sensibly" shape already used elsewhere in this codebase.
- **`SqlToolRegistry`'s trust-tier handling changed from override to agreement-check.** The previous step carried the catalog's `trust_tier` through by constructing `EchoTool` with it; now that a real entrypoint's own code declares its own `trust_tier` (which may not even be safely mutable — e.g. a frozen dataclass), the registry instead **compares** the loaded object's own `trust_tier` against the catalog row's declared value and raises `ToolRegistryError` ("refusing to trust either value alone") on any disagreement, rather than silently overriding either side. A real pack's own code declaring a different trust tier than its own catalog registration is exactly the kind of inconsistency ADR-0016's sandbox guard exists to surface, not paper over.
- **Zero-argument construction (`cls()`) has a real downstream consequence**: a `Tool` used as a real entrypoint must declare `trust_tier` as a class-level default (not something only settable via a constructor argument) to ever match a non-default catalog declaration, since the loader never passes constructor arguments. `tests/integration/workflow_engine/_entrypoint_stubs.py` (new, not a test module itself) provides `SandboxedStubTool`/`MismatchedTrustTierStubTool` (fixed `tier1_sandboxed` class attribute, no `__init__`) alongside `NamedStubAgent` (a distinguishable real `Agent`) and `NotAnAgentOrTool` (satisfies neither Protocol), all genuinely importable so they can serve as real `entrypoint` values in integration tests.
- **`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_registry.py` rewritten** (10 tests, real Postgres, ADR-0015) to seed real, importable `entrypoint` values instead of the previous fake `'pack.agents:Agent'`/`'pack.tools:Tool'` placeholders, and to exercise: loading the real production `EchoAgent`/`EchoTool` entrypoints; loading a genuinely different, distinguishable custom entrypoint (`NamedStubAgent`) and running it; an unregistered id still raising `AgentNotRegisteredError`/`ToolNotRegisteredError`; a malformed/unimportable entrypoint propagating `EntrypointLoadError`; an entrypoint resolving to `NotAnAgentOrTool` raising `AgentRegistryError`/`ToolRegistryError`; a `tier1_sandboxed` entrypoint whose own code and catalog row agree resolving successfully; and a `trust_tier` disagreement raising `ToolRegistryError`. **New `tests/unit/kernel/workflow_engine/test_entrypoint_loader.py`** (12 tests, no database — pure Python import mechanics, ADR-0015 does not apply): uses its own module's dotted path as a real, importable entrypoint target for locally-defined stub classes, empirically verified (not assumed) to work under pytest's default "prepend" import mode.
- **Audited, not just assumed, which other test files' fake entrypoint placeholders needed to become real values**: `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py` and `tests/integration/llm_gateway/test_call_recorder.py` both insert raw `catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools` rows for unrelated schema/FK-satisfaction purposes and never call `SqlAgentRegistry`/`SqlToolRegistry` — confirmed unaffected by re-running both files' suites, not just by tracing the code.
- **Still deliberately not the Capability Manager**: no pack lifecycle, no activation-state check, no permissions enforcement, no sandboxing, no network/code download — a catalog row existing plus its declared `entrypoint` resolving cleanly and agreeing on `trust_tier` (for tools) is the entire boundary.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **328 tests passing** (194 unit + 134 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.54%** (`entrypoint_loader.py`: 100%; `registry.py`: 95% — only the `SQLAlchemyError`-wrapping branches remain uncovered, the same precedent as `SqlPromptCatalog`'s own gap, since nothing triggers a genuine connection failure without mocking, which ADR-0015 forbids).

### Stage B – Capability Manager Minimal Slice: Pack Activation Gating for Registry Resolution
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/pack_state.py`**: `PackState`, a `StrEnum` mirroring capability_manager.md §4's canonical eight-value pack lifecycle (`discovered` … `uninstalled`) — the identical role `TrustTier` (`workflow_engine/tool.py`) already plays for `catalog.tools.trust_tier`'s own `CHECK` constraint, and for the identical reason lives in `workflow_engine`, not `persistence/catalog_schema.py` (the persistence layer never imports from `workflow_engine`; a migration's own duplicated literal list stays a frozen historical record regardless of whether this enum's membership ever changes). Only `ACTIVATED` is consumed by anything yet — the other seven exist so the enum is a complete, honest mirror of the documented lifecycle, not a partial one reverse-engineered from this step's own narrow need.
- **`SqlAgentRegistry.resolve_agent`/`SqlToolRegistry.resolve_tool` (`workflow_engine/registry.py`) now gate on pack activation before loading anything.** Each query changed from a single-table lookup to one `LEFT JOIN` against `catalog.packs` on `pack_id` (still one round trip, not a second query) — a new shared `_require_activated_pack()` helper raises the new `PackNotActivatedError` (`workflow_engine/errors.py`) when the join found no matching pack row (`state IS NULL` — `pack_id` is not FK-enforced against `catalog.packs`, so a genuinely unregistered pack is a real, reachable case) or when the matched row's `state` is anything other than `activated`, with a distinct message for each. This check runs *before* `EntrypointLoader.load()` is ever called — an otherwise-perfectly-valid, otherwise-perfectly-loadable entrypoint is still refused while its owning pack is not `activated`, the exact "practical meaning" this step was scoped around.
- **`InMemoryAgentRegistry`/`InMemoryToolRegistry` are unchanged** — a caller-supplied mapping entry has no owning pack row to check against; the gate is specific to the two catalog-backed implementations, consistent with them already being the only two that read `catalog.packs`/`catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools` at all.
- **Still deliberately not the rest of the Capability Manager**: no pack install/upgrade UI or workflow, no health monitoring, no permissions system, no sandboxing, no network/code download — a resolved agent/tool's declared pack being a real, `activated` `catalog.packs` row is the one and only additional fact checked. Nothing writes to `catalog.packs`/`catalog.pack_state_transitions` yet either; this step only reads `state`, exactly as scoped.
- **`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_registry.py` extended** (16 tests total, real Postgres, ADR-0015): a new `_seed_pack()` helper and an autouse fixture keep a shared default pack (`se.software_engineering`) `activated` so every pre-existing test keeps exercising entrypoint loading rather than this new gate; `_seed_agent`/`_seed_tool` gained an optional `pack_id` parameter (default unchanged) so new tests can seed a distinct pack per scenario. **6 new tests** prove: an agent/tool whose pack is genuinely `activated` still resolves successfully (the required "activated pack → resolution succeeds" case, for both); an agent/tool whose pack exists but is `deactivated` raises `PackNotActivatedError` (the required "non-activated pack → resolution fails" case, for both); an agent/tool whose declared `pack_id` has no `catalog.packs` row at all raises the same error with a distinguishing message, for both.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **334 tests passing** (194 unit + 140 integration). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.58%** (`pack_state.py`: 100%; `registry.py`: 96% — only the pre-existing `SQLAlchemyError`-wrapping branches remain uncovered, unchanged from last step).

### Stage B – One Real LLM Provider Adapter: `AnthropicAdapter`
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/llm_gateway/adapters/` package** — the CI-enforced import boundary llm_gateway.md §2 documents ("Provider SDKs may be imported only inside `kernel/llm_gateway/adapters/`"): the only place in this codebase that imports `anthropic` (added as a real dependency, `anthropic>=0.69,<1.0`, `uv.lock` regenerated). The mechanical CI check itself (`scripts/check_import_boundaries.py`) still does not exist — `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s own "Import boundary check" step remains gated on that script's absence, unchanged by this step — so the boundary is honoured by placement today, not yet enforced by tooling.
- **`AnthropicAdapter`** (`anthropic_adapter.py`) is the first real, non-`Echo*` `LLMGateway` implementation: `complete()` resolves `request.model_alias` to a real model id, builds the `messages.create()` call from exactly the fields the already-reduced `LLMRequest` carries (no `thinking`/`effort` — the contract gives no caller a way to control them, so none is invented), and maps the real response back through `_map_response()`, a pure function unit-tested directly against genuine `anthropic.types.Message`/`Usage`/`TextBlock` objects. `EchoLLMGateway` is untouched and remains the deterministic implementation tests and local development use.
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/llm_gateway/adapters/model_config.py`**: `load_provider_config()` reads a small, dedicated `config/llm.yaml` — a flat `aliases: {alias: model_id}` map plus per-model `pricing` (USD per million input/output tokens) — deliberately **not** the documented Router (llm_gateway.md §7's alias *chains*, fallback, provider health, experiment pinning) and deliberately not routed through `ConfigurationManager`/`PlatformConfig` (a different, larger, not-yet-approved schema decision). This is the smallest configuration-driven mechanism satisfying ADR-0002 ("never a literal model id") and computing an honest `cost_usd` for a real call, without hardcoding either in source.
- **API key access goes through the existing `SecretProvider`, not `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` auto-detection.** `build_anthropic_adapter()` calls `secret_provider.resolve(api_key_secret_reference)` and constructs `anthropic.AsyncAnthropic(api_key=secret.reveal())` — `AnthropicAdapter` itself never imports `secrets_manager` or sees a reference string, only an already-authenticated client. Local development resolves `secret://env/llm/anthropic-api-key` via the existing `EnvSecretProvider` to `AIOS_SECRET_LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` — the exact example that provider's own docstring already used, now a real, working path (`.env.example` updated).
- **New errors** `LLMProviderError`/`LLMRefusalError` (`llm_gateway/errors.py`): every non-2xx response (`anthropic.APIStatusError`) and every network/timeout failure (`anthropic.APIConnectionError`) becomes one `LLMProviderError`, chained via `from`; a real safety decline (`stop_reason: "refusal"`) is the distinct `LLMRefusalError`, matching llm_gateway.md §5's own framing that a refusal is "a policy decision about the request, not a fault." A `stop_reason` this reduced contract cannot represent (`tool_use`, `pause_turn`, `stop_sequence`, `model_context_window_exceeded` — none reachable yet, since no tools/stop-sequences are declared) also raises `LLMProviderError`, clearly, rather than crashing on an unmapped enum value.
- **No live credentials or cassette library are used for the required test suite.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s own comment ("Adapter tests use recorded cassettes") describes infrastructure (e.g. `vcrpy`) that does not exist in this repository yet — building it was judged bigger than this one step. Instead: `_map_response` is tested with real SDK response objects (no I/O at all); `complete()`'s pre-flight validation (unconfigured alias/pricing) needs no network; and `complete()`'s real network path is exercised against **real local endpoints** — a closed TCP port (genuine `APIConnectionError`) and a tiny local `http.server` returning a genuine 401 body and a genuine well-formed 200 `Message` body (genuine `APIStatusError` and a full, real success round trip) — real transport and real SDK parsing throughout, never a mocked client. **New `tests/integration/llm_gateway/test_anthropic_adapter_live.py`** is the opt-in exception: skipped unless `AIOS_SECRET_LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set, it makes one real, cheap (`fast-cheap` alias, `max_output_tokens=16`) call to the live Anthropic API — this is also how a real call is manually verified (see this step's own report).
- **Still deliberately not a Gateway redesign**: no Request Validator, Capability Negotiator, Policy & Budget Enforcer, real Router, Prompt Cache Planner, Retry & Fallback Manager, or Rate Limiter — the SDK's own default retry behaviour (`max_retries=2`) is the only retry present, and it is the SDK's, not this Gateway's. Nothing in the Workflow Engine calls `LLMGateway.complete()` yet, so this adapter is not wired into any composition root — the same position `EchoLLMGateway` itself has always been in.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **356 tests passing, 1 skipped** (the opt-in live test, skipped by design without credentials). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.80%** (`anthropic_adapter.py`: 100%; `model_config.py`: 100%).

### Stage B – One Real End-to-End Path: `PromptEngine` → `LLMGateway(AnthropicAdapter)` → `llm_calls` Recording
- **New `build_anthropic_prompted_completion_service()`** (`ai_os_kernel.prompted_completion`) — the "connect the existing composition path" deliverable this step approves. Composes three already-existing, already-tested real implementations into one `PromptedCompletionService`: `SqlPromptCatalog(engine)` (real `catalog.prompts` reads), `AnthropicAdapter` via `build_anthropic_adapter()` (API key resolved through the injected `SecretProvider`, never `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` directly — reused unchanged from last step), and `SqlLLMCallRecorder(engine)` (real `evaluation.llm_calls` writes). `model_ids`/`pricing` are accepted as parameters, not loaded from a hardcoded path — a caller decides where its configuration comes from (production: `config/llm.yaml` via `load_provider_config()`), mirroring `build_anthropic_adapter()`'s own shape for the identical two parameters.
- **`PromptedCompletionService` itself is completely unchanged.** It was already generic over the `PromptEngine`/`LLMGateway` Protocols — this step needed no new composition logic, only a convenient, secrets-resolved, production-quality way to *construct* the one real combination the approved framing names explicitly. `EchoLLMGateway`/`InMemoryPromptEngine` remain fully available and are exactly what the service's own pre-existing unit test suite (`tests/unit/kernel/test_prompted_completion.py`) continues to use for deterministic composition-logic tests — unchanged, still passing.
- **New unit test** (`test_build_anthropic_prompted_completion_service_wires_the_real_components`) proves the factory's own wiring — real `EnvSecretProvider` (fake key value), a never-connected `AsyncEngine` (construction is lazy; no real I/O happens until a method is awaited) — asserting the returned service's components are genuinely `SqlPromptCatalog`/`AnthropicAdapter`/`SqlLLMCallRecorder`, not silently something else.
- **New `tests/integration/test_prompted_completion.py`** (real Postgres, ADR-0015, no live credentials): seeds a real `catalog.prompts` row with a template (`"Hello, {{name}}!"`) and a real `catalog.agents`/`workflow_instances` row, then proves the *database-backed* two-thirds of the chain end to end with `EchoLLMGateway` standing in for the paid call — a real row renders with real variable substitution, and a real `evaluation.llm_calls` row is written with the right `agent_id`/`prompt_id`/`prompt_version`/`model_alias`, plus the existing "no recording without workflow/step context" rule still holding against a real database.
- **New `tests/integration/test_prompted_completion_live.py`** — the opt-in live test bullet 5 of the approved framing calls for: skipped unless `AIOS_SECRET_LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set, it seeds a real prompt, builds the service via `build_anthropic_prompted_completion_service()`, and makes one real, cheap Anthropic call — asserting the rendered content, a real non-empty response, a real positive `cost_usd`, and a real recorded `evaluation.llm_calls` row with `provider = 'anthropic'`. This is every piece of the approved chain real at once, and how a real end-to-end call is manually verified (see this step's own report).
- **Still not wired into any composition root.** Like `AnthropicAdapter` itself, nothing in the Workflow Engine or any API surface calls `PromptedCompletionService`/`build_anthropic_prompted_completion_service()` yet — this step proves the composition works, it does not make anything real call it. No Router, no budgets, no streaming, no Workflow Engine changes, per the approved framing's own exclusions.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **359 tests passing, 2 skipped** (both opt-in live tests, skipped by design without credentials). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.81%** (`prompted_completion.py`: 100%).

### Stage B – One Thin Real Agent: `PromptedAgent`, Wired Through `AgentStepExecutor`
- **New `kernel/src/ai_os_kernel/workflow_engine/prompted_agent.py`**: `PromptedAgent`, the first real, non-`Echo*` `Agent`. Reads `promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias` from its `inputs` dict, validates all three are present (`PromptedAgentInputError` otherwise, new in `workflow_engine/errors.py`), and delegates to an injected `PromptedCompletionService.complete_from_prompt()` — returning `{"content": <the completion's text>}` against a matching `output_schema`. `variables`/`workflowId`/`stepId`/`agentId` are also read from `inputs` as a direct, unmodified passthrough to that method's own already-existing, already-optional parameters — no new input-mapping system, just forwarding keys that were already there. `maxOutputTokens` is deliberately not one of these: it is not part of the documented Step Contract, so it is a required constructor parameter instead (configuration the composition root supplies once per agent instance).
- **`AgentStepExecutor.execute()` now forwards a step's declared invocation fields instead of always calling `agent.execute({})`.** A new private `_invocation_inputs()` builds `{"promptId": ..., "promptVersion": ..., "modelAlias": ...}` from whichever of `step.prompt_id`/`step.prompt_version`/`step.model_alias` the step actually declared (all three remain optional on `WorkflowStep`) — realising agent_architecture.md's own already-documented sentence: "the Workflow Engine passes them through without acting on them itself." A step declaring none of them still executes with an empty `inputs` dict, byte-for-byte the same behaviour as before this step; `EchoAgent` and every other existing `Agent` implementation are unaffected since they already ignore `inputs`.
- **Constructor-injected, deliberately not entrypoint-loadable, and that boundary is pre-existing, not new.** `EntrypointLoader` always constructs with zero arguments — its own docstring already states that passing manifest-declared configuration into a dynamically-loaded entrypoint's constructor is "real Capability Manager design work, not attempted here." A real `PromptedCompletionService` needs a database engine, a resolved secret, and model/pricing configuration — genuine constructor dependencies with nowhere honest to come from inside a zero-argument `cls()` call. `PromptedAgent` is therefore used through `InMemoryAgentRegistry` — the identical "existing registry path" `EchoAgent` itself is already exercised through — not `SqlAgentRegistry`. Making a dependency-carrying agent real-entrypoint-loadable would mean building exactly the constructor-configuration mechanism the entrypoint loader was explicitly scoped to exclude two steps ago.
- **New `tests/unit/kernel/workflow_engine/test_prompted_agent.py`** (9 tests, `InMemoryPromptEngine`/`EchoLLMGateway`/a fake recorder — no database, no network): proves rendering + completion with and without template variables, all three "missing required field" error cases (individually and all-at-once), recording when workflow/step context is supplied, no recording without it, and the declared `output_schema` shape itself.
- **`tests/unit/kernel/workflow_engine/test_agent_step_executor.py` extended** (3 new tests, a new `_InputCapturingAgent` fake): proves a step declaring none of the three fields still passes an empty `inputs` dict, a step declaring all three forwards exactly those three camelCase keys, and a step declaring only one forwards only that one — real evidence of `AgentStepExecutor`'s own new behaviour, not an assumption about it.
- **New `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_prompted_agent_live.py`** — the opt-in live test this step's own bullet 4 calls for, and the top of the chain the last two steps' own live tests already proved the middle and bottom of: skipped unless `AIOS_SECRET_LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set, it builds a real `WorkflowStep` declaring `promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias`, executes it through a real `AgentStepExecutor` and `InMemoryAgentRegistry` resolving to a real `PromptedAgent`, and asserts a real, non-empty completion came back from the live Anthropic API — proving the full path from a declared step to a real model call, not just the pieces in isolation.
- **Still deliberately narrow**: no Router, no budgets, no streaming, no Capability Manager expansion, and no general per-step input-mapping mechanism — only the three Step Contract invocation fields flow from a step into an agent, exactly as documented and no further. Nothing writes `PromptedAgent` into `kernel/bootstrap.py` or any real API surface; it is proven, not yet consumed, the same position every other piece of this chain has been left in.
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **371 tests passing, 3 skipped** (all three opt-in live tests, skipped by design without credentials). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.87%** (`prompted_agent.py`: 100%; `step_executor.py`: 96% — only the two pre-existing, statically-unreachable "step declares no agentId/toolId" defensive branches remain uncovered, unchanged from before this step).

### Stage B – Real Composition-Root Registration: `PromptedAgent` Wired Into `bootstrap.py`
- **`kernel/bootstrap.py` gained a `_lifespan` hook** — FastAPI's own recommended async-startup mechanism, used here for a specific, structural reason: `build_app()` runs synchronously at import time (`app = build_app()` in `entrypoints/api.py`, before any event loop exists), but building a real `PromptedCompletionService` needs async secret resolution. `_lifespan` runs once when the app actually starts serving, builds the real chain, and attaches it as `app.state.agent_registry` — the same "attach a real composed object to `app.state`" pattern `manifest_loader`/`health_service` already use, just deferred to the point where async construction becomes possible.
- **The real chain, reusing every already-built piece unchanged**: `DatabaseSettings()` (env-driven `AIOS_DATABASE_URL`, already established for persistence) → `build_engine()` → `load_provider_config(config/llm.yaml)` → `build_anthropic_prompted_completion_service()` (Secrets Resolution + `AnthropicAdapter` + `PromptedCompletionService`, unchanged from two steps ago) → `PromptedAgent` (unchanged from last step) → `InMemoryAgentRegistry({"platform/prompted-agent": agent})`. No new production logic was needed in any of the composed pieces — this step is entirely "wire it," matching its own approved framing.
- **Degrades to an empty registry, not a crash, when unconfigured.** A missing `AIOS_DATABASE_URL`, a missing/unresolvable Anthropic secret, or a missing `config/llm.yaml` are all caught by one `try`/`except Exception` around the whole construction, logging a warning and leaving `app.state.agent_registry` as an empty `InMemoryAgentRegistry` — mirroring `manifest_loader_check`'s own established "catch, report, don't crash the process" shape in the same file. Stage A functionality (health checks, manifest discovery) must not depend on a Stage B integration being present.
- **`platform/prompted-agent`** is the registered id — a fully-qualified `pack_id/agent_slug` shape (data_model.md §5) even though no real Capability Pack owns it yet; there is no Capability Manager to assign a real one (Stage C), and inventing a fake pack registration would be further from the truth than naming it honestly as platform-level. `_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY_SECRET_REFERENCE` reuses the exact `secret://env/llm/anthropic-api-key` reference every prior step already used. `_PROMPTED_AGENT_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (not part of the Step Contract, so not derivable from a step) is a named, documented constant — the Constitution's own explicitly-permitted carve-out ("Hardcoding is prohibited unless technically unavoidable and explicitly documented") until a real per-agent configuration mechanism exists.
- **Verified, not assumed: a bare `TestClient(app)` never triggers `_lifespan` at all** (confirmed with a minimal standalone reproduction before relying on it) — only `with TestClient(app) as client:` does. Every existing health-check test in `tests/unit/kernel/entrypoints/test_api.py` uses the bare form and is therefore completely unaffected by this step; a new explicit test proves this invariant directly rather than leaving it implicit.
- **New `tests/unit/kernel/test_bootstrap.py`** (3 tests, no real database, no real network): the degrade-gracefully path (unconfigured environment → empty registry → `AgentNotRegisteredError` on resolve, not a crash); the construct-for-real path (a syntactically valid but never-connected database URL plus a fake-but-present secret — construction itself does no I/O — → a genuine `PromptedAgent` instance resolves successfully); and the bare-`TestClient`-never-triggers-lifespan invariant.
- **New `tests/integration/test_bootstrap_prompted_agent_live.py`** — the opt-in live test verifying this step's own deliverable specifically: skipped unless `AIOS_SECRET_LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set, it is the *only* live test in this codebase that goes through `bootstrap.build_app()` itself (every prior live test constructed its chain by hand) — proving the real composition root, not a hand-assembled equivalent, produces a working agent against a real (`testcontainers`) Postgres and the live Anthropic API.
- **Still deliberately narrow**: no Router, no budgets, no streaming, no Capability Manager entrypoint-configuration framework, and the Workflow Engine's own step-execution machinery (`AgentStepExecutor`, `WorkflowInstanceService`, ...) is still not wired into `bootstrap.py` at all — only the `AgentRegistry` a future step-execution wiring would need is. Nothing reads `app.state.agent_registry` from a real HTTP route yet ("No API surface," §4, remains open).
- `mypy --strict`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **374 tests passing, 4 skipped** (all four opt-in live tests, skipped by design without credentials). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.92%** (`bootstrap.py`: 92% — the only gaps are `_load_configuration()`, uncalled because every test supplies an explicit `config`, and `manifest_loader_check`'s own pre-existing exception branch, both unrelated to this step).

### Stage B – Workflow Engine Step-Execution Machinery Wired Into `bootstrap.py`; One Minimal Real Trigger
- **`_lifespan` now builds the database engine itself**, once, separately from `_build_prompted_agent_registry` — a deliberate refactor: previously that function built its own engine internally and returned `tuple[AgentRegistry, AsyncEngine | None]`, collapsing "database unavailable" and "LLM/secret unavailable" into one degrade path, silently discarding a perfectly good database connection whenever only the unrelated LLM secret was missing. `_build_prompted_agent_registry(engine: AsyncEngine) -> AgentRegistry` now takes an already-built engine and only degrades its own LLM-specific failure to an empty registry; a missing database is now handled one level up in `_lifespan`, which skips building both the registry and the trigger when there is no real engine to run either against.
- **`_build_workflow_trigger(engine, agent_registry)`** composes the real `WorkflowInstanceService` (`SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository`, `SqlWorkflowDefinitionCatalog`, `DispatchingStepExecutor` over `AgentStepExecutor(agent_registry)` / `ToolStepExecutor(InMemoryToolRegistry({}))` / `NoOpStepExecutor`) and the real `WorkflowAdvanceRunner` (`WorkflowLeaseService(SqlWorkflowLeaseRepository(engine))`), closes over one demo `WorkflowDefinition`, and returns a plain async `trigger(inputs, principal_id)` closure — a new class was not justified (ADR-0004): this is "call three already-built async methods in sequence," the identical reasoning `PromptedCompletionService` itself was built on.
- **The demo `WorkflowDefinition`** (`platform.prompted_agent_smoke_test`, version `1.0.0`, one `AGENT` step naming `agentId: platform/prompted-agent`) is constructed directly in Python in `bootstrap.py`, not loaded from a file via `WorkflowDefinitionLoader` — that loader's own design (confirmed by reading it and its tests) is for loading a real pack's own definition file from a manifest-declared path, and there is no real Capability Pack directory for one to live in yet at the platform level; every existing test in this codebase already constructs `WorkflowDefinition` objects directly in Python for the identical reason. `workflow_steps` has no foreign key on `agent_id`/`prompt_id`/`prompt_version`/`model_alias` (confirmed by inspecting `persistence/schema.py`), so declaring these without a matching `catalog.agents`/`catalog.prompts` row is safe for persistence — though a real `catalog.prompts` row is still needed for the agent to actually render successfully against the live Anthropic API, which the new live test seeds.
- **Deliberately not a real HTTP route.** Re-reading `docs/07_api/api_architecture.md` in full (this step's own mandated alignment checkpoint) found its documented `POST /api/v1/workflows` contract requires OIDC bearer authentication and `workflow:start` permission-based authorization through a Security Manager that does not exist in this codebase yet — building a real, unauthenticated route would violate that already-approved contract, not just leave it incomplete. `app.state.trigger_prompted_agent_workflow` is the "otherwise a minimal explicit service entrypoint that bootstrap owns" fallback this step's own approved framing explicitly anticipated: a real, callable, composition-root-owned function, reachable by a test or a future Security-Manager-fronted route, never by an unauthenticated request.
- **New `tests/integration/test_bootstrap_workflow_trigger.py`** (2 tests, real Postgres via `testcontainers`, no live Anthropic credential): a hand-built `PromptedAgent` wrapping `InMemoryPromptEngine`/`EchoLLMGateway` is substituted into `_build_workflow_trigger`'s `agent_registry` parameter, proving the real Workflow Engine machinery (create → start → advance → step dispatch → agent resolution → output validation → persistence) genuinely reaches and invokes whatever agent is registered — one test asserts a `COMPLETED` `WorkflowRunResult` with the Echo agent's real output persisted in `workflow_steps`; the other asserts a `FAILED` `WorkflowRunResult` carrying `AgentNotRegisteredError` when the demo agent isn't registered, rather than a crash.
- **New `tests/integration/test_bootstrap_workflow_trigger_live.py`** — opt-in, skipped unless `AIOS_SECRET_LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set: seeds a real `catalog.prompts` row matching the demo step's declared `promptId`/`promptVersion`, goes through the real `bootstrap.build_app()` + `_lifespan` composition root (not a hand-assembled equivalent), and calls the real `app.state.trigger_prompted_agent_workflow` to drive the demo workflow to a genuine Anthropic-backed `COMPLETED` result.
- **Still deliberately narrow**: no Router, no budgets, no streaming, no Capability Manager expansion, no real HTTP route, no worker scheduler, no multi-instance queue framework — "create/start/advance one workflow instance path is enough," exactly as approved.
- `mypy --strict` (190 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean on first attempt. Full suite: **376 tests passing, 5 skipped** (five opt-in live tests, skipped by design without credentials). Combined unit+integration coverage: **96.93%**.

### Stage A – Minimal Security Manager Slice: Bearer-Token Auth + One Permission Check, Fronting `POST /api/v1/workflows`
- **New `ai_os_kernel.security_manager` package** (replacing the docstring-only Stage-C placeholder scaffolded at Step 1): `models.py` (`Principal`, `PrincipalType`, `SecurityContext`), `permissions.py` (`permissions_for_roles()` and the `_ROLE_PERMISSIONS` table), `token_verifier.py` (`TokenVerifier` Protocol, `JWTBearerTokenVerifier`, `build_jwt_bearer_token_verifier()`), `dependencies.py` (`require_permission()`, the FastAPI dependency chain).
- **`SecurityContext`/`permissions_for_roles()` deliberately model only the principal's own role-derived permissions**, not ADR-0023's full `principal ∩ workflow ∩ agent ∩ tool` monotonic-narrowing chain — that needs manifest-declared permissions on workflows/agents/tools, which is Capability Manager territory not yet built. The role table itself is authentication_authorization.md §4.2's documented five roles, reduced to the two permissions (`workflow:read`, `workflow:start`) this step actually enforces, not the SDK's full closed vocabulary — deliberately not invented ahead of a route that would need it.
- **`JWTBearerTokenVerifier` is bearer-token authentication, not full OIDC.** It verifies a JWT's HS256 signature and expiry against a pre-shared secret resolved through the existing `SecretProvider` seam (`secret://env/security/jwt-signing-key`, the identical pattern `_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY_SECRET_REFERENCE` already established) — a real, credible bearer-token mechanism, just not backed by a live identity provider (no JWKS fetch, no issuer/audience validation). This is the "clean minimal auth boundary that does not violate the architecture and can later be replaced by full OIDC without rewriting callers" the approved framing's own escape hatch anticipated: every caller depends on the `TokenVerifier` Protocol, not `JWTBearerTokenVerifier` — a real OIDC implementation later replaces one class, not any call site.
- **`require_permission()` fails closed at every stage**: no bearer token → `401`; a token that fails signature/expiry/claim verification → `401`; a verified principal lacking the required permission → `403`; **no signing secret configured at all → `503`**, deliberately distinct from `401` — the caller did nothing wrong, the service itself cannot authenticate anyone. Every 401/403 decision is logged (`security_manager.authentication_failed`/`security_manager.authorization_denied`) — real-but-partial auditability, not yet a `governance.audit_log` write (that table still has no writer at all, an unchanged, already-documented gap).
- **`kernel/bootstrap.py`** gained `_build_token_verifier()`, called unconditionally in `_lifespan` (authentication needs neither the database engine nor the LLM secret, so it does not wait on either), attaching the result — or `None`, on a missing/unresolvable secret — as `app.state.token_verifier`. `_JWT_SIGNING_KEY_SECRET_REFERENCE` follows the same named-constant, `secret://env/...`-reference convention as `_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY_SECRET_REFERENCE`.
- **New `ai_os_kernel/routes/workflows.py`**: `POST /api/v1/workflows`, requiring `workflow:start`, then calling the unchanged `app.state.trigger_prompted_agent_workflow` — the first real HTTP route in this codebase to reach the Workflow Engine. Deliberately narrower than api_architecture.md §6.1's documented contract: no `definition_id`/`definition_version`/`experiment_id` fields (there is exactly one real workflow definition), no `Idempotency-Key`, and `200` with a reduced, JSON-safe `StartWorkflowResponse` (`workflow_id`, `outcome`, `iterations`, `error`) instead of `202` fire-and-forget — since the trigger still runs the instance to completion synchronously (no worker loop exists yet), returning `202` for already-finished work would misrepresent what happened. `StartWorkflowResponse` is deliberately not the internal `WorkflowRunResult` itself, since that model's `last_instance`/`error` fields carry shapes (a full `WorkflowInstance`, a raw exception object) not meant to cross the API boundary unfiltered.
- **New `pyjwt>=2.9,<3.0` dependency** (`kernel/pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` regenerated) — the first dependency this Security Manager slice needed; no other new third-party dependency was required.
- **New `tests/unit/kernel/security_manager/`** (17 tests: `test_models.py`, `test_permissions.py`, `test_token_verifier.py`) and **`tests/unit/kernel/routes/test_workflows.py`** (6 tests) — no real database, no real network, no real signing secret needed beyond an in-test constant: permission-table correctness for every documented role, JWT verification edge cases (wrong signature, expired, missing `sub`, unknown `principal_type`, malformed token), and the full 401/403/503 decision tree exercised against a real `bootstrap.build_app()` with `AIOS_DATABASE_URL` deliberately left unset.
- **New `tests/integration/test_workflows_route.py`** (2 tests, real Postgres via `testcontainers`, no Anthropic credential): an authorized (`workflow:start`) request genuinely reaches the real Workflow Engine through the real composition root and gets back a real, structured `failed` outcome — the demo agent isn't registered without a real LLM secret — proving the security boundary does not block or distort a legitimate request while remaining fully deterministic; an unauthorized (`viewer`) request is rejected with `403` before the engine is ever touched.
- **New opt-in `tests/integration/test_workflows_route_live.py`** — skipped unless `AIOS_SECRET_LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set: seeds a real `catalog.prompts` row and drives the demo workflow to a genuine Anthropic-backed `completed` result through the real HTTP route with a real bearer token — the full stack, end to end, for the first time.
- **Still deliberately narrow**: no full OIDC, no service-account API-key mechanism as a distinct concept, no ADR-0023 monotonic-narrowing chain beyond the principal's own permissions, no role assignment/administration, no `governance.audit_log` writer, no rate limiting, no RFC 9457 `application/problem+json` error bodies (401/403/503 responses here are FastAPI's default `{"detail": ...}` shape, not yet the documented Problem Details format — a distinct, not-yet-attempted gap, noted honestly rather than invented away), and every other api_architecture.md §6 endpoint still does not exist.
- `mypy --strict` (204 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean on first attempt (after one expected `B008` false-positive on FastAPI's own `Depends(...)` idiom, suppressed with `# noqa: B008` and an explanatory comment, mirroring the existing `# noqa: S105` precedent for a different well-understood linter false positive). Full suite: **402 tests passing, 6 skipped** (six opt-in live tests, skipped by design without credentials). Combined unit+integration coverage: **97.17%** (`security_manager/`: 100% across every new file; `routes/workflows.py`: 100%; `bootstrap.py`: 95%).

### Stage A – Three Authenticated Read-Only Workflow Routes: `GET /workflows/{id}`, `.../steps`, `.../events`
- **`ai_os_kernel/routes/workflows.py`** gained `get_workflow`, `get_workflow_steps`, `get_workflow_events` — `GET /api/v1/workflows/{id}`, `.../steps`, `.../events` (api_architecture.md §6.1). Each calls the already-built `WorkflowInstanceRepository.get_instance()`/`list_steps()`/`list_events()` unchanged; no new Workflow Engine code was needed, exactly as the approved framing's "reuse existing Workflow Engine read accessors / repositories" bullet specified.
- **Gated on `workflow:read`**, already modelled (and already granted to every role, including `viewer`) by the previous step's `permissions_for_roles()` table — no new Security Manager code, no new permission, no new role.
- **`kernel/bootstrap.py`**: `_lifespan` now also attaches `app.state.workflow_instance_repository = SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository(engine)` alongside the existing `agent_registry`/`trigger_prompted_agent_workflow` assignments, when a real engine is available — a plain, stateless wrapper over the same engine, safe to construct separately from the `WorkflowInstanceService` `_build_workflow_trigger` already builds internally for writes.
- **Response shapes are the real domain models, unfiltered**: `WorkflowInstance`, `WorkflowStepRecord`, `WorkflowEventRecord` are returned directly as `response_model`s — unlike the write route's `StartWorkflowResponse`, none of these three carry an internal-only field (a raw exception, a full nested object) that needs projecting before crossing the API boundary, so no new DTO was invented (ADR-0004: a seam is justified only when it does something a direct return does not).
- **A `workflow_id` naming no real instance returns `404` from all three routes** — each first calls `get_instance()` and raises `404` before touching `list_steps()`/`list_events()`, so a workflow that never existed is distinguishable from one that exists with zero steps or events (which would otherwise both return an empty `200 []` from `list_steps()`/`list_events()` alone, since neither method checks instance existence itself).
- **Dependency ordering matters and is now explicit**: the `workflow:read` permission check is declared *before* the repository-availability check in each route's parameters, so FastAPI resolves authentication/authorization first — an unauthenticated or unauthorized request gets `401`/`403` even when the Workflow Engine itself is unavailable, never a `503` that would leak "is the backend even up" information ahead of a security decision. (Caught by the first test run this step — the two checks were originally declared in the opposite order, which leaked `503` instead of `401`/`403`; reordered before writing further tests.)
- **Deliberately narrower than api_architecture.md §6.1's documented shape**: the events route returns the complete list, not the cursor-paginated (`limit`/`cursor`/`next_cursor`) shape §9 documents for collections generally. Building real cursor pagination for one endpoint, ahead of every other collection endpoint (`GET /workflows`, `/approvals`, `/experiments`, ...) that would need the identical mechanism, is a distinct, later step — not attempted here, and every one of those other collection endpoints still does not exist regardless.
- **New `tests/unit/kernel/routes/test_workflows.py` additions** (9 more tests, parametrized across all three routes — 24 total in the file now): no bearer token → `401`; a role granting nothing → `403`; a valid, permitted request with no real database configured → `503` "workflow engine is not available" — all with no real database and no real network.
- **New `tests/integration/test_workflows_route.py` additions** (2 more tests, real Postgres via `testcontainers`, no Anthropic credential — 4 total in the file now): one drives a real instance to a genuine `completed` state using a substituted Echo-backed `PromptedAgent` (the identical technique `test_bootstrap_workflow_trigger.py` uses), then reads it back exclusively through the three authenticated HTTP routes, asserting the real persisted status, the real step's `agentId`/`outputs`, and the real event log (`workflow.started`, `workflow.completed` both present); the other asserts all three routes return `404` for a `workflow_id` that never existed.
- **Still deliberately narrow**: no `GET /api/v1/workflows` (list, cursor-paginated), no cursor pagination on the events route, no filtering (`status`, `created_after`, ...), no Dashboard, and every other api_architecture.md §6 endpoint still does not exist — exactly the framing's own "no full API surface expansion beyond these reads" constraint.
- `mypy --strict` (204 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **413 tests passing, 6 skipped** (six opt-in live tests, skipped by design without credentials). Combined unit+integration coverage: **97.25%** (`routes/workflows.py`: 100%).

### Stage A – `GET /api/v1/workflows`: Cursor-Paginated Collection Endpoint Over Every Instance
- **`WorkflowInstanceRepository` gained one new method, `list_instances(*, limit, before=None)`**, implemented in `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository` alongside `get_instance`/`list_steps`/`list_events` (same "plain, unguarded read" shape) and added to the Protocol every existing fake `WorkflowInstanceRepository` implementation in the unit test suite (`test_advance_runner.py`, `test_service.py`) had to gain a stub for, to stay structurally conformant. Orders newest-first: `created_at` DESC, `workflow_id` DESC — the second column a deliberate tiebreaker for rows sharing one timestamp, using the fact that `workflow_id` is a ULID (data_model.md §2: "sortable, opaque") rather than inventing a new ordering concept. Matches the schema's own pre-existing `ix_workflow_instances_created_at_desc` index — that index was already there, unused until now.
- **`WorkflowListCursor`** (`ai_os_kernel.workflow_engine.repository`) is the keyset *value* — `created_at` + `workflow_id` — as a small frozen Pydantic model. Deliberately not the wire encoding: comparing two keysets is the repository's job (a genuine SQLAlchemy `tuple_(...) < (...)` row-wise comparison, not two independent column filters that could double-count or skip a row), while encoding one into an opaque string for an HTTP response is a transport concern that belongs in `ai_os_kernel.routes.workflows`, not the persistence layer — the same separation `SecurityContext`'s "value vs. wire format" split already established for the Security Manager.
- **`GET /api/v1/workflows`** (`ai_os_kernel.routes.workflows.list_workflows`) requests `limit + 1` rows from the repository — the classic "one extra row, never returned" technique for detecting a next page without a separate `COUNT` query — and returns api_architecture.md §9's documented envelope `{"items": [...], "next_cursor": "…" | null}`. `limit` is validated by FastAPI's own `Query(gt=0, le=100, default=50)` (`422` outside range, no new validation function needed); the cursor is decoded by a small pure `_decode_cursor()`/`_encode_cursor()` pair (base64 + JSON), rejecting anything malformed with `400` before any query runs.
- **Never offset-paginated** — api_architecture.md §9 is explicit that a growing collection makes offsets skip or duplicate rows; every page boundary here is a real row's own keyset position, not a row count.
- **Gated on the existing `workflow:read` permission** — no new Security Manager work, no new role, no new permission, exactly as approved. The permission check is declared before the repository-availability dependency in the route's parameters, the same ordering rule the previous step's own bug fix established, so an unauthenticated/unauthorized request never learns whether the Workflow Engine backend is even up.
- **Deliberately no filtering** — no `status`/`definition_id`/`created_after` query parameters (api_architecture.md §5's own documented filter examples) and no query DSL. The approved framing was a *correct list endpoint*, not search; a filtered list is a distinct, later step layered on the same cursor mechanism, not attempted here.
- **New `tests/unit/kernel/routes/test_workflows_cursor.py`** (5 tests): the cursor codec round-trips, is URL-safe, and rejects garbage input, non-JSON base64, and JSON missing a required field — all pure-function tests, no app, no database.
- **`tests/unit/kernel/routes/test_workflows.py` extended** (7 more tests, 26 total in the file): the three existing 401/403/503 parametrized tests now also cover `GET /workflows` itself; two new tests confirm `limit` outside `1`–`100` returns `422` (using a syntactically-valid-but-never-connected database URL, the same lazy-engine-construction technique `test_bootstrap.py` established, so the repository dependency succeeds and FastAPI's own query-parameter validation is what's actually being exercised, not a race against the 503 unavailable-backend path).
- **`tests/integration/test_workflows_route.py` extended** (2 more tests, real Postgres via `testcontainers`, no Anthropic credential — 6 total in the file now): one drives three real completed instances, then paginates through the entire real collection with `limit=2`, asserting every page respects the limit, the walk terminates, and every created instance appears in the result exactly once (no duplicates, no gaps) — genuine proof the keyset mechanism is correct, not just plausible; the other confirms a malformed cursor returns `400` end to end through the real route.
- **Still deliberately narrow**: no filtering, no free-form search, no `GET /api/v1/workflows/{id}/...` sub-resource pagination (the per-instance `/steps`/`/events` routes remain un-paginated — a single instance's own history is bounded, unlike the top-level collection), and every other api_architecture.md §6 endpoint besides the four workflow routes still does not exist.
- `mypy --strict` (205 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Full suite: **426 tests passing, 6 skipped** (six opt-in live tests, skipped by design without credentials). Combined unit+integration coverage: **97.26%** (`routes/workflows.py`: 100%; `workflow_engine/repository.py`: 95%).

### Stage B/C – Capability Manager Minimal Pack Lifecycle Writer: Register, Activate, Deactivate
- **New `ai_os_kernel.capability_manager` package** (replacing the docstring-only Stage-B/C placeholder scaffolded at Step 1): `errors.py` (`CapabilityManagerError`, `PackAlreadyRegisteredError`, `PackNotFoundError`, `InvalidPackTransitionError`, `PackRegistrationError`), `models.py` (`PackRecord`, mirroring `WorkflowInstance`'s "snapshot returned after a write" role), `ids.py` (`new_transition_id()`, prefix `pkt_` — data_model.md §2's prefix list is illustrative, not exhaustive, the same reasoning `call_` was already chosen under for `evaluation.llm_calls`), `repository.py` (`PackLifecycleRepository` Protocol, `SqlPackLifecycleRepository`).
- **Three operations, not the full eight-state canonical lifecycle** (capability_manager.md §4: `discovered → validated → installed → configured → activated → {deactivated, failed} → uninstalled`): `register()` (installs a fresh pack — no persisted `discovered`/`validated` row exists anywhere in this codebase to transition from, since the Manifest Loader validates a manifest in memory without writing anything, so `register()`'s own recorded transition reads as `discovered → installed`, the lifecycle's first canonical state, not two invented rows), `activate()`, `deactivate()`. No `configured`/`failed`/`uninstalled` states, no version-upgrade path, no health monitoring, no permissions matrix, no sandboxing, no remote/marketplace installation — exactly the approved framing's own exclusions.
- **`activate()` accepts a pack currently `installed` *or* `deactivated`** — capability_manager.md §4's lifecycle diagram draws only the primary forward arrow (`activated → deactivated`, none back), but its own state table calls `deactivated` explicitly "reactivatable." The prose, not the diagram's arrows, is what makes re-activation a real, intended operation; proven directly by a new integration test that activates, deactivates, and re-activates the same pack.
- **Every transition is recorded in `catalog.pack_state_transitions`** with `actor`/`reason`/`occurred_at` — capability_manager.md §8's own documented audit requirement, now genuinely satisfied for the first time rather than an unenforced aspiration. `activate()`/`deactivate()` lock the pack's row (`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`) before validating its current state and writing, inside one transaction with the transition insert (ADR-0011's "both written in one transaction" pattern, mirroring `workflow_instances`+`workflow_events`) — so two concurrent calls against the same pack cannot both observe the same pre-transition state and both proceed.
- **`register()` rejects a duplicate `pack_id` outright** (`PackAlreadyRegisteredError`) rather than an idempotent upsert — registration is a one-time lifecycle event; re-registering an already-known pack under a new version is an *upgrade*, a distinct, later capability_manager.md §3 responsibility ("Manage pack versions and upgrades") this step does not implement.
- **No new HTTP surface.** No `POST /api/v1/packs`/`.../activate`/`.../deactivate` route (api_architecture.md §6.5 documents these, but a real route would need the `pack:manage` permission modelled and a governance story for "activation of a pack that affects platform behaviour requires human approval," ADR-0007 — neither exists yet). This step is a pure Python write path, called directly (by a test today; by a future route or CLI command later) — the identical "build the real mechanism before deciding how it's triggered" sequencing already used for the Workflow Engine's own trigger before its route existed.
- **Directly proves the one integration point the approved framing named** ("keep agent/tool resolution gated on activated state"): a new integration test registers and activates a pack through `SqlPackLifecycleRepository`, resolves a real agent through the *unchanged* `SqlAgentRegistry`, deactivates the pack, and confirms resolution is refused again with the same `PackNotActivatedError` a hand-seeded row already produced — the new writer's output is provably indistinguishable from test-only seeding for gating purposes, not merely assumed to be.
- **New `tests/integration/capability_manager/test_pack_lifecycle.py`** (10 tests, real Postgres via `testcontainers`, no mocking — ADR-0015): register creates an `installed` row and records `discovered → installed`; a duplicate `pack_id` is rejected; activate transitions `installed → activated`; activating an already-`activated` pack is rejected; activating an unregistered pack raises `PackNotFoundError`; deactivate transitions `activated → deactivated`; deactivating a pack that was never activated is rejected; a deactivated pack can be reactivated (the full four-transition history asserted in order); `get_pack` returns `None` for an unregistered id; and the `SqlAgentRegistry` gating integration test described above. No unit-test file — mirrors the established convention that every `Sql*`-backed class in this codebase (`SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository`, `SqlAgentRegistry`, `SqlPromptCatalog`, ...) is tested exclusively against a real database, never mocked.
- `mypy --strict` (211 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean on first attempt. Full suite: **436 tests passing, 6 skipped** (six opt-in live tests, skipped by design without credentials). Combined unit+integration coverage: **97.22%** (`capability_manager/`: 95–100% across every new file).

### Stage B/C – Capability Manager Pack Lifecycle Writer Wired Into `kernel/bootstrap.py`
- **`kernel/bootstrap.py`'s `_lifespan` now attaches `app.state.pack_lifecycle_repository = SqlPackLifecycleRepository(engine)`** — one line, added immediately after the existing `workflow_instance_repository` assignment, inside the same `if engine is not None:` branch every other Stage B/C `app.state` attribute already lives in. No new construction logic was needed: `SqlPackLifecycleRepository` already took a bare `AsyncEngine` (see the previous step), so wiring it in was purely "reuse the engine `_lifespan` already builds," exactly as approved.
- **Degrades identically to every sibling attribute**: a missing/misconfigured database leaves `pack_lifecycle_repository` simply absent from `app.state` (never set, never a placeholder, never a crash) — the same "nothing real to run against without a real engine" reasoning `workflow_instance_repository`/`trigger_prompted_agent_workflow` already established. A missing LLM secret alone does not affect this attribute at all, since pack lifecycle writes need no LLM Gateway or Prompt Engine dependency.
- **New `tests/integration/test_bootstrap_pack_lifecycle.py`** (3 tests, real Postgres via `testcontainers`, no live credential needed): the real composition root — `build_app()` + `_lifespan`, not a hand-assembled `SqlPackLifecycleRepository` — genuinely registers, activates, and deactivates a pack, and separately confirms `PackNotFoundError`/`InvalidPackTransitionError` still surface correctly through the bootstrap-built instance; a third test confirms the bare-`TestClient`-never-triggers-`_lifespan` invariant holds for this attribute too, mirroring the identical invariant test already established for `agent_registry`.
- **`tests/unit/kernel/test_bootstrap.py` extended** (2 more tests, 5 total in the file): the degrade-path (no `AIOS_DATABASE_URL` → attribute absent) and the construct-for-real path (a syntactically valid but never-connected database URL — engine construction is lazy, so this exercises real `SqlPackLifecycleRepository` construction with no real infrastructure) — the identical two-sided pattern already used for `agent_registry`.
- **Still deliberately narrow**: no HTTP route reads this attribute (no `/api/v1/packs/...` route exists — explicitly out of scope this step), no discovery, no upgrades, no permissions, no sandboxing. The only caller today is a test with `app.state` in hand; a future route or CLI command is the natural next caller, not built here.
- `mypy --strict` (212 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean on first attempt. Full suite: **441 tests passing, 6 skipped** (six opt-in live tests, skipped by design without credentials). Combined unit+integration coverage: **97.22%** (`bootstrap.py`: 96%).

### Stage B/C – Minimal `pack:manage`-Gated HTTP Surface for the Capability Manager's Pack Lifecycle Writer
