- **Architecture review performed first, per this step's own approved framing — re-evaluated from scratch, not defaulting to the previously-recommended Runtime Configuration Resolver.** Found a stronger, more specific signal than context_manager.md's own source list: implementation_roadmap.md §3 names the Stage B deliverable for this exact component as *"Context Manager with deterministic assembly, budget enforcement, and trust tagging."* Deterministic assembly (verified by an ADR-0022 test in the prior step) and trust tagging (every `ContextItem.trust` populated) were already done; budget enforcement was the one named, in-stage, unmet piece — a smaller, more explicitly-documented target than Runtime Configuration (named nowhere in the roadmap), and unblocked, unlike Security Context propagation (explicitly a Stage C deliverable — "Security Manager enforcing monotonic permission narrowing," and §5's own "What Must Not Be Deferred" table: "Monotonic permission narrowing | Stage C" — not yet due) or Knowledge/Memory retrieval (blocked on an entirely unbuilt Platform Service, ADR-0013, plus the LLM Gateway's unbuilt `embed()`). Full review delivered and approved before any code.
- **`ContextRequest` gains one new field: `token_budget: int | None = None`, rejecting `<= 0` (`pydantic.Field(gt=0)`).** context_manager.md §5 documents `token_budget` as something a request *may* carry, not something every request must declare — an optional field is therefore the faithful reading, not a compromise. `required_context_types` and experiment/run identifiers remain absent as fields, unchanged from the prior step's own reasoning.
- **`DefaultContextManager` gains one new, optional, defaulted-`None` constructor parameter: `default_token_budget`.** Mirrors `PerScopeBudgetEnforcer`'s own shape — a ceiling the *assembler* is configured with, applied only when a request's own `token_budget` is absent. This avoids forcing every existing caller to start declaring a per-request budget, the identical "assembler-level default, request-level override" reasoning already applied to the LLM Gateway's own budget ceilings, adapted to a component where the request contract, not the constructor, is the documented home for the value.
- **The admission algorithm: rank by `relevance_score` (descending, stable sort), greedily admit whatever still fits, return survivors in original resolver order.** A stable sort means items tied on score (every item today, since only one resolver with a constant score exists) preserve their original resolver order rather than being reordered or arbitrarily chosen among — ADR-0022's "context assembly ... is deterministic given the same inputs," and this step's own explicit "preserve stable ordering" requirement. Admission does not stop at the first item that doesn't fit: a smaller, lower-ranked item can still fill room a larger, higher-ranked one left unused (first-fit continues past a skip) — proven by a dedicated test. The final `items` list is reconstructed in the *original* resolver order among only the admitted items — truncation decides *which* items survive, never reorders the ones that do; rank governs admission, not final presentation order, which is what "preserve stable ordering" was read to require alongside ADR-0022's determinism obligation.
- **No budget at all (`ContextRequest.token_budget=None` and `DefaultContextManager`'s own `default_token_budget=None`, every caller before this step) is unaffected — the same "disabled means `None`, zero behaviour change" shape every other Kernel policy limit in this codebase already uses** (`budget_enforcer`/`workflow_budget_enforcer` on `DispatchingLLMGateway`, `circuit_breaker`, `backoff_policy`). Every existing test/caller from the prior Context Manager step passes unmodified.
- **This is genuinely budget *enforcement*, not the still-unbuilt Context Filter/Ranker — a distinction recorded explicitly, not blurred.** context_manager.md §4 names these as two separate internal components. `relevance_score` is reused here only as a stable truncation tie-break ("*which* items survive when there isn't room for all of them"), not as a first-class ranking capability with its own logic, weighting, or multi-signal scoring — that remains unbuilt, deliberately, until a second resolver gives `relevance_score` genuine variance to rank by.
- **`kernel/bootstrap.py`** adds one new named constant, `_CONTEXT_TOKEN_BUDGET = 8000` — the identical "named, documented, placeholder safety limit, not a tuned production value" carve-out every other Kernel policy constant in this file already uses — passed to `DefaultContextManager(..., default_token_budget=_CONTEXT_TOKEN_BUDGET)`. No Workflow Engine, `AgentStepExecutor`, or `PromptedAgent` file changed — the entire increment lives inside `ai_os_kernel.context_manager` and its one composition-root call site.
- **New tests**: `tests/unit/kernel/context_manager/test_models.py` (+4): `token_budget` defaults to `None`, accepts a positive value, rejects zero and negative values. `test_manager.py` (+11): no budget includes everything unfiltered; a request-level budget excludes what doesn't fit; the assembler's own default applies when the request specifies none; a request-level budget overrides the default; higher-relevance items are admitted before lower-relevance ones regardless of input position; survivors are returned in original resolver order, not rank order; tied scores preserve original order when truncating; a smaller lower-ranked item fills room a skipped larger item left unused; an item larger than the entire budget is excluded outright, never partially included; budget enforcement is deterministic across repeated calls on the same request. `tests/unit/kernel/test_bootstrap.py` (+1): the real composition root wires a real `DefaultContextManager` with a real, positive `default_token_budget`. `tests/integration/test_bootstrap_workflow_trigger.py` (+1 new real-database test): a real oversized workflow input is genuinely excluded against a real 1-token budget, and the workflow step genuinely fails (a required-but-now-absent `context` prompt variable) rather than silently rendering without it — the Size & Token Budget Enforcer's own end-to-end proof, not a stub.
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's approved scope**: no additional Context Resolvers (Runtime Configuration, Knowledge, Memory, AI Context Packs); no semantic ranking; no retrieval; no compression; no Context Audit Logger persistence (still no data_model.md schema); no `required_context_types` or experiment/run identifiers on `ContextRequest`; no `index_generation` on `AssembledContext`.
- `mypy --strict` (237 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean.

### Stage B – LLM Gateway: Capability Negotiator (implementation_roadmap.md §3's "Capability Matrix")

- **Architecture review performed first, re-evaluated from scratch rather than continuing the Context Manager by default.** Surveyed every Stage B deliverable line in implementation_roadmap.md §3, not just the Context Manager's own. Found: Quality Gate Engine and Tool Invoker are nominally Stage B-listed, but ADR-0006's own "Consequences" section ties *real* gate tooling to Stage C completion ("requires real tooling ... before Stage C can complete"; gates "run inside the sandbox defined in ADR-0016, since most of them execute untrusted generated code") and no Capability Pack producing real generated code exists before Stage C either — attempting a "trivial first slice" now would have nothing real to validate. Retrieval (ADR-0013) and Platform SDK v1.0.0 are both real, Stage B-named gaps, but each is large enough to need its own dedicated scoping conversation (the same treatment already given to Capability Manager discovery). Event Bus is Stage B-named and unblocked, but `platform.event_outbox` has no writer and nothing in the implemented system has hit a real need for async pub/sub. The LLM Gateway's own "capability matrix" — confirmed 0% built by direct inspection (`grep` found no `ProviderCapabilities`, `capabilities()`, or `count_tokens()` anywhere in the codebase) — was the only item simultaneously explicitly roadmap-named, confirmed unbuilt, and free of any upstream blocker or later-stage pacing. Full review delivered and approved before any code.
- **New `ai_os_kernel.llm_gateway.capability_negotiator`**: `ProviderCapabilities` (llm_gateway.md §6's full thirteen-field matrix: `supports_tools`/`supports_parallel_tool_calls`/`supports_strict_tools`/`supports_structured_output`/`supports_streaming`/`supports_thinking`/`supports_effort`/`supports_prompt_caching`/`prompt_cache_min_tokens`/`supports_vision`/`max_input_tokens`/`max_output_tokens`/`accepts_sampling_params`, with cross-field validation: `prompt_cache_min_tokens` must be set if and only if `supports_prompt_caching` is true; both token ceilings must be positive), a `CapabilityNegotiator` Protocol (`capabilities(model_alias: str) -> ProviderCapabilities`, mirroring `Router`'s own shape), and `StaticCapabilityNegotiator` — resolves `model_alias` through a real `Router` to a model id (identical to how every real adapter already resolves pricing), then looks up that model id in a fixed, configuration-driven mapping. Raises clearly (`LLMProviderError`, `llm.no_capabilities`, a new Error Taxonomy classification mirroring `llm.no_pricing` exactly) for an unconfigured model id, rather than fabricating a matrix.
- **Full llm_gateway.md §6 matrix implemented, not a further-reduced slice — a deliberate departure from this project's usual "reduced slice" precedent, reasoned explicitly.** Every field describes a real, verifiable fact about a model's own provider API, independent of whether the Gateway's own request contract exposes a way to use that capability yet (e.g. `supports_vision` is true for Claude models even though `LLMRequest`/`ContentBlock` only carry text today) — this is cataloguing truth, not building for a consumer that doesn't exist, the distinction this step's own approved framing draws between "the matrix" (in scope) and "capability-dependent features" (explicitly out of scope). Every field is real; nothing is fabricated to fill an unused slot the way an always-`None` placeholder would be.
- **`config/llm.yaml` gains a new `capabilities:` section, keyed by real model id — the identical `pricing:` shape, for the identical reason ("Model names ... shall always come from configuration").** Populated for every model id already configured in this file: `claude-opus-5`, `claude-sonnet-5`, `claude-haiku-4-5`, and `llama3.1:8b` (a specific, real, known local model — not "any local model"; the entry describes Llama 3.1 8B Instruct itself and must be updated or removed if a different model is ever run behind `local_provider.base_url`). **Numeric/boolean values are the author's best available knowledge, not machine-verified against live provider documentation — flagged explicitly in the file's own header comment and here, not silently asserted as fact.** Corrections belong in this configuration file, not a code change, the identical reasoning already established for `pricing`'s own dollar figures.
- **`DispatchingLLMGateway` gained one new, optional, defaulted-`None` constructor parameter: `capability_negotiator`, plus a new public, synchronous method: `capabilities(model_alias: str) -> ProviderCapabilities`.** Synchronous, matching platform_sdk.md §5.1's own documented signature exactly (unlike `complete()`/`stream()`/`embed()`/`count_tokens()`, answering it needs no I/O — the matrix is already-loaded configuration). Raises clearly (`llm.no_capability_negotiator`) when called with none configured, rather than returning a fabricated default. **Not added to the shared `LLMGateway` Protocol** — `stream()`/`embed()`/`count_tokens()` are also documented in platform_sdk.md §5.1 alongside `capabilities()` and are *also* still absent from this codebase's own reduced `LLMGateway` Protocol, each deferred to its own not-yet-arrived step; `capabilities()` follows the identical precedent (ADR-0004: ADR-0004's "second real use imminent" test does not yet apply — no second `LLMGateway` implementation needs to answer this polymorphically this step).
- **Nothing in `complete()` consults the matrix.** No tool-calling, structured-output emulation, streaming, `require_capabilities` field, or context-window-fit check exists yet to make a use/emulate/fail decision — llm_gateway.md §6's own "degradation rules" (four of them) are entirely unbuilt, deliberately, per this step's own approved exclusions. This is the matrix and the lookup only.
- **`kernel/bootstrap.py`** constructs one real `StaticCapabilityNegotiator` (from the same `router` already built for routing, plus `provider_config.capabilities`) in `_build_prompted_agent_registry`, passed through `build_anthropic_prompted_completion_service` as `capability_negotiator` (a new, defaulted-`None` parameter there too, threaded straight through unchanged). Changes no observable request-handling behaviour for any existing caller — only makes `capabilities(alias)` newly answerable.
- **Discovered documentation discrepancy, reported rather than silently resolved:** platform_sdk.md §5.1's own `ProviderCapabilities` listing names ten fields, omitting `supports_strict_tools`, `prompt_cache_min_tokens`, and `accepts_sampling_params` — llm_gateway.md §6 names thirteen. Neither document states a precedence between them for this specific shape (platform_sdk.md is not in llm_gateway.md's own "Final Authority" list, and vice versa). This step implements llm_gateway.md §6's fuller shape, reasoned (that document is the Capability Negotiator's own governing design; platform_sdk.md only summarises the pack-facing surface), not silently picked. Worth reconciling in a future documentation pass.
- **New tests**: `tests/unit/kernel/llm_gateway/test_capability_negotiator.py` (12): `ProviderCapabilities`' own validation (frozen, the `prompt_cache_min_tokens`/`supports_prompt_caching` cross-field rule in both directions, positive-token-ceiling rejection) and `StaticCapabilityNegotiator` (alias resolution, two aliases sharing one model id's matrix, a clear error for an unknown alias, a clear `llm.no_capabilities` error for a resolvable-but-unconfigured model id). `tests/unit/kernel/llm_gateway/adapters/test_model_config.py` (+7): the new `capabilities:` section parses correctly, defaults to `{}` when absent, and rejects a non-mapping section, a malformed entry, a missing field, a `prompt_cache_min_tokens` contradiction, and a non-positive token ceiling — each surfaced as a clear `LLMProviderError`. `tests/unit/kernel/llm_gateway/test_dispatching_gateway.py` (+3): no negotiator configured raises clearly; a configured negotiator answers `capabilities()` without affecting `complete()`'s own behaviour; `capabilities()` is genuinely synchronous, not a coroutine. `tests/unit/kernel/test_prompted_completion.py` (+2), `tests/unit/kernel/test_bootstrap.py` (+1): `capability_negotiator` threads through `build_anthropic_prompted_completion_service` (real-supplied and defaulted-`None` cases); the real composition root wires a real negotiator whose `capabilities("coding-balanced")` answers with real, positive data read from the real, checked-in `config/llm.yaml`.
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's approved scope**: no tool calling, structured output, streaming, embeddings, context-window-fit checks, prompt-cache planning/hints, provider degradation logic, or any Stage C functionality. No change to the shared `LLMGateway` Protocol. No Workflow Engine, Context Manager, or Prompt Engine file touched.
- `mypy --strict` (239 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **521 tests passing** (three pre-existing, unrelated `test_local_adapter.py` real-local-HTTP-server flakes observed under full coverage-instrumented load, reproduced as passing in isolation immediately after — the same documented flake family as two prior steps, not touched by this step). Combined coverage on every new/modified file: **100%** (`capability_negotiator.py`, `adapters/model_config.py`, `gateway.py`, `prompted_completion.py`). **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session, so the real-Postgres integration suite could not be re-executed; this step's changes touch no database code path, and the real composition root's own `load_provider_config()` call against the real, edited `config/llm.yaml` was verified via the real-startup unit test (`test_the_real_composition_root_wires_a_real_capability_negotiator`), which does parse and validate the actual checked-in file.

### Stage B – Retrieval: Knowledge Schema and Database Migration (data_model.md §7, ADR-0013 — First Real Retrieval Increment)

- **Two architecture reviews performed before any code, per this step's own approved framing — the second triggered by a genuine finding during the first's own validation pass, not by a documentation change.** The first pass (validating against implementation_roadmap.md, data_model.md §7, kernel_architecture.md, ADR-0013, `knowledge_manager.md`, `memory_manager.md`) surfaced two findings and stopped rather than proceeding: (1) `knowledge.embeddings.embedding` is documented as `vector(N)` — pgvector's own Postgres type — which appeared to contradict this step's own "no hidden dependency on pgvector" validation checklist item; (2) the referenced `retrieval.md` does not exist anywhere in the docs tree — the real governing document is `docs/03_architecture/services/search_vector_search.md` (per `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md` §9), a naming discrepancy surfaced rather than silently substituted. A second, deeper documentation-driven review (requested explicitly rather than accepting either of two convenience-driven paths) found that `infra/docker-compose.yml` already runs `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` (provisioned since Stage A), `deployment_architecture.md` §5's production topology already names "postgres (with pgvector)", and ADR-0013 already Accepts pgvector as this platform's search/vector technology — concluding this was a **sequencing issue already resolved by existing architecture**, not a genuine conflict, and that the first pass's own alarm should have checked the infra layer before being raised. Both reviews delivered and approved before any code.
- **New `ai_os_kernel.persistence.knowledge_schema` module** — all four tables data_model.md §7 documents: `documents` (`document_id` PK, `source_uri`, `content_hash`, `media_type`, `project_id` nullable, `trust` with a `trusted`/`untrusted` check constraint, `ingested_at`, `archived_at` nullable), `chunks` (`chunk_id` PK, FK to `documents`, `ordinal`, `content`, `token_count`, `chunk_strategy_version`, a real generated `content_tsv tsvector` column, `metadata jsonb`, unique on `(document_id, ordinal)`), `embeddings` (`embedding_id` PK, FK to `chunks`, `embedding`, `embedding_model_id`, `embedding_model_version`, `dimensions`, `index_generation`), and `memory_items` (`memory_id` PK, `memory_type` with a `workflow`/`engineering`/`asset` check constraint, `content`, a real FK to `workflow.workflow_instances.workflow_id`, `quality_signal`/`promoted_at`/`expires_at` nullable, `provenance jsonb`) — the identical "own `MetaData`, own schema, schema-and-migration-only, no reader/writer/search logic" precedent already established for `trace`/`governance`/`platform`/`catalog`/`evaluation`. A new `kernel/alembic/versions/0029_knowledge_schema.py` migration mirrors it column-for-column, plus `CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS knowledge` and `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS vector`, with a fully symmetric `downgrade()` (drops all four tables, then the schema, then the extension). `kernel/alembic/env.py` gains `knowledge_metadata` in `target_metadata`. New Python dependency: `pgvector>=0.3,<1.0` (ADR-0009 — `pyproject.toml` edited, `uv.lock` regenerated and verified against both the latest `uv` and the CI-pinned `uv==0.5.11`).
- **`embeddings.embedding` is declared as an unconstrained `pgvector.sqlalchemy.Vector()` (no fixed dimension), and the documented HNSW index on it is deliberately not created — a decision made and recorded during implementation, not raised as a third stop-and-report cycle, since (unlike the extension question) it had exactly one clear, precedented, non-speculative answer.** data_model.md §7's own `vector(N)` notation names no concrete `N` anywhere in the approved documentation, and inventing one (1536, 1024, …) would be exactly the "no speculative architecture" this step must avoid; the table's own separate, per-row `dimensions` column (alongside `embedding_model_id`/`embedding_model_version`) confirms the schema already anticipates rows from different embedding models — and therefore different real vector sizes — coexisting, which an unconstrained column is the faithful reading of, not a workaround. pgvector's HNSW index requires a fixed dimension to be created at all, which requires the same not-yet-made embedding-model decision the column itself defers; the GIN index on `chunks.content_tsv` has no such dependency and is created normally. The HNSW index is deferred to whichever future step first chooses a real embedding model and dimension, at which point it is a purely additive migration, not a redesign of this one. `content_tsv` uses `to_tsvector('english', content)` — a concrete, necessary choice (English is this project's own working language throughout every existing document) to make a documented "generated" column real, changeable later in a purely additive migration.
- **New integration tests only, per the established "schema-and-migration-only steps get integration tests, not unit tests" precedent** (`trace`/`governance`/`platform` schema steps have none either): `tests/integration/persistence/test_migrations.py` gains a `_EXPECTED_KNOWLEDGE_TABLES` set (folded into the existing up/down/up round-trip test, now covering seven schemas across twenty-nine migrations) and 14 new tests — `pg_extension` genuinely lists `vector` after upgrade; each of the four tables' real column shapes; the `trust`/`memory_type` check constraints reject an invalid value; `chunks`/`embeddings`/`memory_items`' foreign keys reject an unknown parent row; the `(document_id, ordinal)` unique constraint rejects a duplicate; `content_tsv` is proven genuinely generated (real content inserted, read back via `::text`, the stemmed term "architectur" found — not just declared); the unconstrained `embedding` column is proven to genuinely accept a 3-dimension and an 8-dimension vector in the same table — the key behavioural proof of the architectural decision above; and `quality_signal`/`promoted_at`/`expires_at` are proven to genuinely default to `NULL`.
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's approved scope**: no Retrieval Service, no keyword/vector/hybrid search, no RRF, no embedding generation (nothing calls the still-nonexistent LLM Gateway `embed()`), no chunking pipeline, no knowledge ingestion, no Knowledge Manager or Memory Manager component (their existing empty stub packages under `knowledge_manager/`/`memory_manager/` are untouched — the new schema lives only in `persistence/knowledge_schema.py`), no Context Manager resolver, no APIs, no background jobs, no CLI, no Stage C functionality, and no HNSW index (see above).
- `mypy --strict` (241 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **520 passed, 1 pre-existing unrelated flake** (`test_anthropic_adapter.py::test_complete_classifies_real_http_error_responses`, a real-local-HTTP-server race in an untouched `llm_gateway` test — a different parametrized case failed on each of two runs under load, then passed 3/3 in isolation immediately after, the same documented flake family as prior steps). **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session (three consecutive steps now), so the new integration tests above could not be executed against a real Postgres/pgvector instance; verification instead relied on `CreateTable(...).compile(dialect=postgresql.dialect())` producing the exact expected DDL for all four tables (including the `GENERATED ALWAYS AS (...) STORED` clause and the unconstrained `VECTOR` type) plus `mypy --strict`/`ruff` — re-run the full integration suite once Docker Desktop is available.

### Stage B – Retrieval: Minimal Writer for `knowledge.documents`/`knowledge.chunks` (Second Real Retrieval Increment)

- **Read `implementation_status.md`, `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`, data_model.md §7, and `search_vector_search.md` before writing any code, per this step's own context-discipline requirement.** Confirmed `search_vector_search.md`'s own "Indexing Pipeline" component ("deterministic, versioned chunk_strategy_version") is the documented future owner of chunking and writing, still entirely unbuilt — this step's writer is the persistence primitive that component will eventually call, not the pipeline itself. No new architectural contradiction was found; the prior step's own recommendation (§6) named this exact increment.
- **New `ai_os_kernel.persistence.knowledge_writer` module**: `KnowledgeWriter` Protocol (`write_document(*, source_uri, content_hash, media_type, trust, chunks, project_id=None) -> DocumentRecord`) and `SqlKnowledgeWriter`, its only implementation (SQLAlchemy 2.0 Core against Postgres, ADR-0011) — the identical Protocol-plus-implementation shape every other writer in this codebase uses (`WorkflowDefinitionCatalog`/`SqlWorkflowDefinitionCatalog`, `LLMCallRecorder`/`SqlLLMCallRecorder`, `PackLifecycleRepository`/`SqlPackLifecycleRepository`). `ChunkInput` (caller-supplied: `content`, `token_count` (`> 0`), `chunk_strategy_version`, `metadata`) and `DocumentRecord`/`ChunkRecord` (read models returned after a write, mirroring `PackRecord`'s own role) are new Pydantic models. New `ai_os_kernel.persistence.knowledge_ids` (`new_document_id()` → `doc_`, `new_chunk_id()` → `chunk_`) mirrors every other component's own `ids.py` exactly (data_model.md §2's "Prefixed ULID text" convention; neither prefix is documented with an example, the same "illustrative, not exhaustive" reasoning already used for `pkt_`/`call_`/`asm_`).
- **Deliberately placed beside `knowledge_schema.py` in `persistence/`, not inside `ai_os_kernel.knowledge_manager`.** knowledge_manager.md names the Knowledge Manager as this data's eventual retrieval-facing owner, but that package is an intentionally untouched stub (this step's own approved framing: "No Knowledge Manager ... integration yet") — writing into it would itself be a form of "Knowledge Manager integration." This mirrors the identical precedent already set by `workflow_engine.definition_catalog` (registers into `catalog.workflow_definitions` without being a Capability Manager) and `llm_gateway.call_recorder` (writes `evaluation.llm_calls` without being an Observability platform): a bare persistence writer, homed beside its schema, with no owning domain component built yet to claim it.
- **Accepts already-chunked input; computes nothing.** `content_hash` and every chunk's `content`/`token_count`/`chunk_strategy_version` are supplied by the caller — hashing a fetched resource or splitting it into chunks is Indexing Pipeline territory (still unbuilt), excluded by this step's own "no ingestion pipeline" fence. `ordinal` is the one field this writer *does* derive rather than accept: `chunks: Sequence[ChunkInput]` is already an ordered sequence (the caller's own chunking fixed the order), so `enumerate()` supplies `ordinal` directly — the least-invented reading, and one that trivially satisfies `uq_chunks_document_id_ordinal` within a single write.
- **One transaction spans both tables** (`documents` then `chunks`, both inside one `engine.begin()`), mirroring every other snapshot-plus-children writer in this codebase (`workflow_instances`+`workflow_events`, `catalog.packs`+`catalog.pack_state_transitions`): a document is never observable with zero chunks, and chunks are never observable without their parent row. An empty `chunks` sequence is rejected before the transaction opens — `KnowledgeWriteError("chunks must not be empty: ...")` — alongside blank-field checks (`source_uri`/`content_hash`/`media_type`), all raised before any database access, the identical "clear message before a bare constraint-violation stack trace" discipline `SqlLLMCallRecorder` already established. A real check-constraint violation (an invalid `trust` value) still surfaces as `KnowledgeWriteError`, wrapping the underlying `sa.exc.IntegrityError`.
- **`document_id`/`chunk_id` are freshly generated on every call — not an idempotent upsert.** Unlike `catalog.workflow_definitions` (a caller-supplied natural key, `(definition_id, version)`, upserted with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), a document being ingested has no natural stable identity of its own to key on — the identical "the writer mints a fresh identity for what it creates" reasoning `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository.create()` already applies to `workflow_id`. Two calls with byte-identical content therefore create two independent documents; deduplicating by `content_hash` is explicitly left as a policy decision for whichever future Indexing Pipeline step calls this writer, not decided here.
- **No `.returning()` on either insert.** Every column value is already fully known before the `INSERT` runs (no server-computed value exists here except `chunks.content_tsv`, a generated column deliberately excluded from `ChunkRecord`) — `DocumentRecord`/`ChunkRecord` are constructed directly from the same values just written, the identical choice `SqlWorkflowDefinitionCatalog`/`SqlLLMCallRecorder` already made. No reader, update, delete, or search method exists — verification uses a direct `SELECT` against the real tables, the same pattern `test_definition_catalog.py` already established.
- **New integration tests only** (`tests/integration/persistence/test_knowledge_writer.py`, ADR-0015 — no mocking the database, the identical `PostgresContainer("pgvector/pgvector:pg16", ...)` module-scoped fixture already used by `test_definition_catalog.py`/`test_call_recorder.py`/`test_migrations.py`): 10 tests — the documented columns are written correctly; ids carry the correct `doc_`/`chunk_` prefixes and chunks reference the right parent; `content_tsv` genuinely generates *through this write path* (not just through a raw `INSERT` — extends `test_migrations.py`'s own proof of the column itself to this writer's own executemany-style insert); `project_id` genuinely defaults to `NULL` when omitted; two calls with identical content produce two independent documents; an empty chunk sequence and each blank required field are rejected with a clear `KnowledgeWriteError`; an invalid `trust` value's check-constraint violation is wrapped, not raised bare.
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's approved scope**: no chunking algorithm, no content hashing, no embedding generation, no `knowledge.embeddings` rows, no keyword/vector/hybrid search, no ingestion pipeline, no Knowledge Manager/Memory Manager integration, no Context Manager resolver, no API route, no background job. `kernel/bootstrap.py` is untouched — no consumer exists yet to wire this writer into; doing so would be dead composition-root code, not a real behaviour change, deferred to whichever future step adds a real caller.
- `mypy --strict` (244 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **521 passed**, zero failures (the previous step's own `test_anthropic_adapter.py` real-local-HTTP-server flake did not reproduce this run — consistent with it being a timing race, not a regression). **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session (a fourth consecutive step), so the 10 new integration tests above have never executed against a real Postgres/pgvector instance; verification instead relied on compiling the writer's own `INSERT` statements against the PostgreSQL dialect (confirming `chunks.content_tsv` is correctly excluded from the compiled column list for the exact executemany-style call the writer issues, so Postgres will populate it rather than reject the insert) and directly exercising the writer's pure-Python validation and `ordinal`-derivation logic against a stub engine — re-run the full integration suite once Docker Desktop is available.

### Stage B – Retrieval: Minimal Keyword Search Reader over `knowledge.chunks.content_tsv` (Third Real Retrieval Increment)

- **Read `implementation_status.md`, `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`, data_model.md §7, and `search_vector_search.md` before writing any code, per this step's own context-discipline requirement.** Confirmed search_vector_search.md's own "Keyword Search: Postgres FTS: tsvector + GIN" line as the exact documented component this step makes real, distinct from the Hybrid Ranker/Metadata Filter/Access Control components that sit in front of it in the full design and remain untouched. No new architectural contradiction was found; the prior step's own recommendation (§6) named this exact increment.
- **New `ai_os_kernel.persistence.knowledge_keyword_search` module**: `KeywordSearcher` Protocol (`search(*, query: str, limit: int = 10) -> list[ChunkSearchResult]`) and `SqlKeywordSearcher`, its only implementation — the identical Protocol-plus-implementation shape every writer/reader in this codebase already uses. `ChunkSearchResult` (`chunk_id`, `document_id`, `content`, `rank`) is a new, frozen Pydantic read model — exactly the four fields this step's own approved framing named, no more.
- **`plainto_tsquery`, not `websearch_to_tsquery` or `to_tsquery` — a reasoned choice, not the only option.** `plainto_tsquery` treats the whole input as plain text and ANDs every recognised lexeme together, exposing no query syntax (no quoted phrases, no exclusions, no boolean operators) — the simplest, safest reading of "accepts a plain-text query string." `websearch_to_tsquery`'s richer syntax and `to_tsquery`'s raw boolean operators are both real Postgres capabilities, deliberately deferred rather than overlooked.
- **The query is parsed with the identical `'english'` configuration `content_tsv` was generated with (`to_tsvector('english', content)`) — not a second, independent choice.** A query parsed under a different configuration would silently stop matching rows it should, since Postgres's `@@` operator compares stemmed lexemes, not raw text. Not made configurable this step, for the same reason the generated column's own configuration is not: doing so would need a per-chunk or per-query configuration column data_model.md §7 does not document.
- **Ranking is Postgres's own `ts_rank` against the matched query, descending, with `chunk_id` (a sortable ULID) as a deterministic tiebreak — ADR-0022's reproducibility requirement.** Two chunks scoring identically under `ts_rank` would otherwise have no guaranteed relative order across repeated identical queries. No custom scoring, weighting, or multi-signal ranking exists — that remains the still-unbuilt Hybrid Ranker's job.
- **A blank query, or a non-positive `limit`, is rejected before any query runs** (`KeywordSearchError`) — the identical "clear error before a query that could only ever be useless" discipline `SqlLLMCallRecorder`'s own blank-field checks already established. `limit` defaults to a named `_DEFAULT_LIMIT = 10` constant rather than being unbounded or a magic number inline at each call site, overridable per call.
- **Deliberately placed beside `knowledge_writer.py`/`knowledge_schema.py` in `persistence/`, not inside `knowledge_manager` or a new "retrieval" package.** search_vector_search.md's Hybrid Ranker/Metadata Filter/Access Control/Retrieval Service components remain entirely unbuilt — this is a bare persistence-layer reader, the identical "own module, no owning domain component built yet" reasoning already applied to the writer, mirroring `SqlPromptCatalog`'s own thin-reader shape. No metadata/`project_id`/`trust` filtering, no access control, no ranking beyond `ts_rank` — explicitly out of scope.
- **No writer, update, delete, HTTP route, or Context Manager wiring — "prove the reader in isolation," per this step's own scope.** `kernel/bootstrap.py` is untouched, for the identical "no consumer exists yet to wire this into" reasoning already applied to the writer step.
- **New integration tests only** (`tests/integration/persistence/test_knowledge_keyword_search.py`, ADR-0015 — no mocking the database, the identical `PostgresContainer("pgvector/pgvector:pg16", ...)` module-scoped fixture already used throughout `tests/integration/persistence/`): 10 tests, seeding real rows through the previous step's own `SqlKnowledgeWriter` rather than hand-written SQL (proving the two increments compose end to end) — a matching chunk is found and returns the documented fields; a chunk mentioning a term more often ranks above one mentioning it once; non-matching chunks are excluded entirely; an explicit `limit` and the documented default `limit` are both respected; the plural query "documents" matches singular "document" content via English stemming, proving `content_tsv`'s own generation and this reader's query parsing genuinely agree on the `'english'` configuration; two chunks tied on rank are ordered identically and deterministically across repeated calls; a blank query and each non-positive `limit` are rejected with a clear `KeywordSearchError`.
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's approved scope**: no vector search, no hybrid search, no RRF fusion, no `knowledge.embeddings` involvement of any kind, no Knowledge Manager/Memory Manager component or integration, no HTTP route, no Context Manager resolver, no Workflow Engine wiring, no new index or schema change.
- `mypy --strict` (246 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **521 passed**, zero failures. **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session (a fifth consecutive step), so the 10 new integration tests above have never executed against a real Postgres/pgvector instance; verification instead relied on compiling the reader's exact `SELECT ... WHERE content_tsv @@ plainto_tsquery(...) ORDER BY ts_rank(...) DESC, chunk_id ASC LIMIT ...` statement against the PostgreSQL dialect, confirming it renders valid SQL with the expected bind parameters (`'english'`, the query text, the limit) — re-run the full integration suite (now covering the schema, the writer, and this reader together) once Docker Desktop is available.

### Stage B/C – Sandbox: Minimal Tier 1 Sandbox Executor (ADR-0016 — First Increment Under the New Multi-Agent-Pipeline Priority)

- **Read `implementation_status.md`, `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`, ADR-0016, and `security_architecture.md` before writing any code, per this step's own context-discipline requirement — and found a real architectural tension, reported rather than silently avoided or silently deferred.** ADR-0016's own "Alternatives Considered" section states plainly: "Direct subprocess execution with a restricted user — Simplest; rejected outright. It shares the kernel, filesystem namespace, and network namespace with the platform, and offers no meaningful containment for hostile code." security_architecture.md §5.1 likewise names the Tier 1 runtime as "Ephemeral OCI container per step, via `SandboxRuntime` (Docker/Podman...)" with no subprocess-only path documented anywhere. This step's own approved framing nonetheless asks for a real `LocalSubprocessSandbox` "per ADR-0016's Tier 1 guarantees" — a genuine, direct tension with the ADR's own explicit rejection, not a documentation-naming quibble. **Resolution, consistent with the framing's own signal that a future Docker/OCI backend is the real Tier 1 implementation:** build the subprocess backend for real and useful today, but make it **truthfully self-report**, via a new `SandboxGuarantees` matrix, exactly which ADR-0016 controls it does and does not actually enforce at the OS level — rather than silently claiming Tier 1 compliance it cannot deliver, or silently weakening what it does implement. This mirrors the identical "declare a real, checkable capability matrix instead of asserting or hiding a limitation" precedent the LLM Gateway's own `ProviderCapabilities`/`CapabilityNegotiator` already established (see this document's own earlier entry). Not a full stop-and-report halt, since the framing's own "interface ready for a future Docker/OCI backend" language already anticipated exactly this two-tier resolution.
- **Naming discrepancy surfaced, not silently reconciled:** ADR-0016 and security_architecture.md both name this seam `SandboxRuntime`; this step's own approved framing calls it `SandboxExecutor`. The new package uses `SandboxExecutor` (per the explicit framing), with the discrepancy recorded in the package's own docstring for a future documentation pass to reconcile.
- **New `ai_os_kernel.sandbox` package**: `SandboxExecutor` Protocol (`execute(*, command: Sequence[str], working_directory: Path, timeout_seconds: float, max_output_bytes: int, env: Mapping[str, str] | None = None) -> SandboxResult`, plus a `guarantees: SandboxGuarantees` property) — every parameter shaped for a future container backend too, not only for a subprocess, per this step's own "no change to callers" requirement. `SandboxResult` (`exit_code: int | None`, `stdout`, `stderr`, `timed_out`, `truncated`, `duration_seconds`) and `SandboxGuarantees` (five booleans, one per ADR-0016 control this step's own framing named) are new, frozen Pydantic models. `SandboxExecutionError` is a new exception for invalid input and start-up failure — never for the executed command's own non-zero exit code, which is a legitimate result, not an error.
- **`LocalSubprocessSandbox` — real, working, and honestly incomplete, exactly as the resolution above requires.** Declares `enforces_timeout=True`, `enforces_output_cap=True`, `enforces_secret_exclusion=True`, `enforces_network_isolation=False`, `enforces_filesystem_containment=False`. What is genuinely enforced: **secret exclusion** — the child process never inherits `os.environ` at all; its environment is built from nothing but a small, explicit, execution-necessary allowlist (`PATH`, and on Windows `SYSTEMROOT`/`PATHEXT`/`COMSPEC`) plus whatever the caller explicitly passes via `env` — there is no leak to prevent because there is no inheritance to begin with, not best-effort scrubbing of a larger inherited set. **A real wall-clock timeout** and **a real, eagerly-enforced per-stream output cap** — the moment either stdout or stderr exceeds `max_output_bytes`, the process is killed immediately (`truncated=True, timed_out=False`), rather than left running and blocked on an undrained pipe until the unrelated timeout eventually catches it — a distinct, deliberately-designed outcome from a real timeout, not conflated with it. **No `shell=True`, ever** (security_architecture.md §15) — `command` is a `Sequence[str]` executed via `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec`, so shell metacharacters in an argument are never interpreted. What is honestly *not* enforced, per `guarantees`: no network isolation (nothing prevents the child from opening a socket — this backend must never run genuinely untrusted/hostile code for that reason alone) and no filesystem containment (`working_directory` sets the child's starting cwd and is validated to exist, but is not a jail — an absolute path or `..` still escapes it). No memory/CPU/PID limits either (ADR-0016 assigns these to OCI runtime configuration; enforcing them for a bare subprocess needs platform-specific primitives — POSIX `resource.setrlimit`, Windows Job Objects — this step's own framing does not name and which were judged real, avoidable complexity for a first slice, left to a more capable local backend or, more likely, made a configuration line by the container backend instead).
- **A real, Windows-`ProactorEventLoop`-specific bug was found and fixed during implementation, not left in.** The first working version wrapped the whole read-and-wait sequence in `asyncio.wait_for` and relied on cancellation to interrupt a timed-out or over-cap read — this hung indefinitely on Windows: cancelling a `Task` blocked on a pending subprocess-pipe read does not reliably unblock it, since the underlying OS read only completes once the pipe actually produces data or closes, and cancellation alone does not force that. Fixed by restructuring around `asyncio.wait(..., return_when=FIRST_COMPLETED)` in a deadline-bounded loop that **kills the process before cancelling** — killing closes both pipe ends, which lets every pending read (and the process-exit wait) resolve promptly on its own, with `cancel()` reduced to cleanup rather than the mechanism relied on to end a hang. Found and fixed by actually running the tests locally, not by inspection alone — this bug would not have been caught by `mypy`/`ruff` or by reading the code, only by execution.
- **New tests only, and — for the first time in five consecutive steps — genuinely executed this session, not just DDL-compiled or reasoned about.** `tests/unit/kernel/sandbox/test_executor.py`: 18 tests, no Docker/Postgres dependency at all (a real OS subprocess needs neither), using `sys.executable` throughout so the suite is portable between this session's Windows environment and CI's Linux runners without relying on any shell built-in. Covers: successful/non-zero-exit/stderr-separated execution; running in the given working directory; a hanging process genuinely killed on timeout; output genuinely truncated and the process genuinely killed early when a stream exceeds its cap; **the load-bearing secrets-isolation proof this step's own requirement named** — a secret set in the *test process's own* environment via `monkeypatch.setenv` is confirmed absent from the sandboxed child's observed environment, and more broadly, no arbitrary ambient variable crosses over unless explicitly passed via `env=`; explicitly-provided `env` values do pass through; **the load-bearing shell-injection-immunity proof** — a payload containing `$(...)`/`` `...` ``/`&&` is printed back byte-for-byte, never evaluated; every invalid-input rejection (empty command, non-positive timeout/output cap, missing working directory, nonexistent executable); and a direct equality check that `guarantees` reports the honest, reduced set of controls documented above.
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's approved scope**: no `DockerSandbox`/OCI backend (the Protocol is shaped to accept one later with no caller change, but none is implemented), no real `Tool` using this sandbox, no Tool Invoker wiring (no such package exists in this codebase yet — confirmed by inspection, a gap worth naming for the roadmap, not silently filled), no Capability Pack, no relaxation of any control this step did implement.
- `mypy --strict` (251 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **539 passed** (521 previous + 18 new), zero regressions, zero flakes observed this run. **Environment note:** this step needed no Docker/Postgres at all — the first fully, genuinely test-verified step in five consecutive steps, rather than one cross-checked only via DDL/SQL compilation. A cosmetic, non-blocking `PytestUnraisableExceptionWarning` (an "unclosed transport"/"Event loop is closed" `ResourceWarning`) is observed on this Windows session when a killed subprocess's pipes are torn down after the per-test event loop closes — does not fail any test (no `filterwarnings` configured to error on it), not reproduced by inspection as a Linux/CI concern (CI runs `ubuntu-latest`, whose asyncio subprocess implementation does not share Windows' `ProactorEventLoop` pipe-transport-closing behaviour), and not chased further with a private-API workaround given "stay inside this one small step."

### Stage B/C – A Real `tier1_sandboxed` Tool, and a Narrowed `ToolStepExecutor` Guard (Second Increment Under the New Priority)

- **Read `implementation_status.md`, `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`, ADR-0016, and workflow_architecture.md's Tool Contract before writing any code, per this step's own context-discipline requirement.** Direct inspection of `ai_os_kernel.workflow_engine.step_executor`/`tool.py` confirmed the exact blocker this step closes, named in the prior step's own §6 recommendation: `ToolStepExecutor.execute()` refused *every* `tier1_sandboxed` tool unconditionally (`tool.trust_tier is not TrustTier.TIER2_TRUSTED` → always raise), and its own docstring said so in as many words ("no sandbox exists yet"). No new architectural contradiction was found — this step's own framing already anticipated the exact resolution shape the previous step's `SandboxGuarantees` design set up.
- **New `ai_os_kernel.workflow_engine.tool.SandboxBackedTool`**: a second `@runtime_checkable` structural-marker Protocol (`sandbox: SandboxExecutor`), the identical "structural presence check, not a concrete-type check" shape `Tool` itself already uses for `SqlToolRegistry`'s own entrypoint validation. Exists solely so `ToolStepExecutor` can tell a genuinely sandbox-backed tool apart from one that merely declares the tier.
- **New `ai_os_kernel.workflow_engine.sandboxed_tool.SandboxedCommandTool`**: the first real `tier1_sandboxed` `Tool`. `trust_tier` is a fixed class attribute (not constructor-settable like `EchoTool`'s), since — unlike `EchoTool`, which can honestly claim either tier depending on how it's used — this Tool's entire behaviour *is* what `tier1_sandboxed` describes. `execute()` delegates to an injected `SandboxExecutor`, mapping its `SandboxResult` onto a new, real `output_schema` (`exitCode: integer|null`, `stdout`, `stderr`, `timedOut`, `truncated`, `durationSeconds`). Command, working directory, timeout, output cap, and any explicit environment are all supplied at **construction**, not per invocation — `execute(inputs)` ignores whatever it is called with (currently always `{}`), preserving the already-established "no per-step input-mapping mechanism exists yet" deferral this step's own framing explicitly keeps on record, rather than inventing one to make the command "configurable."
- **`ToolStepExecutor`'s guard is narrowed, not weakened — and this distinction is enforced in code, not just asserted in prose.** New logic: a `tier1_sandboxed` tool is dispatched only when `isinstance(tool, SandboxBackedTool) and tool.sandbox is not None`; anything else (no `sandbox` attribute at all, or one that is structurally present but `None`) still raises the identical `ToolSandboxRequiredError` as before. `tier2_trusted` tools are completely unaffected — the same always-allowed path, byte-for-byte. `ToolSandboxRequiredError`'s own docstring, and `ToolStepExecutor`'s, were both updated to state the narrowed rule precisely rather than leaving the old "only tier2_trusted" wording stale and misleading.
- **No architectural upgrade is claimed by this step, and none should be inferred from a passing test.** Whichever `SandboxExecutor` a `SandboxedCommandTool` is constructed with is what actually runs the command — today, `LocalSubprocessSandbox`, whose own `guarantees` (previous step) honestly report no network isolation and no filesystem containment. Dispatching a `tier1_sandboxed` tool through this path does not change that; `SandboxedCommandTool`'s own docstring restates it explicitly so a future reader does not mistake "the guard let it through" for "this is now safe for hostile code."
- **New tests, all genuinely executed this session (no Docker/Postgres — a real OS subprocess needs neither):** `tests/unit/kernel/workflow_engine/test_sandboxed_tool.py` (7 tests) — `trust_tier` is `TIER1_SANDBOXED`; the tool satisfies `SandboxBackedTool` and exposes the same sandbox instance it was constructed with; a real command's `SandboxResult` is correctly mapped onto the declared output shape; a non-zero exit code and a real timeout are both reported correctly; `inputs` are genuinely ignored; the tool's own output genuinely validates against its own declared `output_schema`. `tests/unit/kernel/workflow_engine/test_tool_step_executor.py` (+3): a `tier1_sandboxed` tool whose `sandbox` attribute is structurally present but `None` is still refused (proving the guard checks the value, not just presence); **the real end-to-end proof this step exists for** — a `WorkflowStep` of type `tool`, resolved through `ToolStepExecutor`, genuinely executes a real OS command through a real `SandboxExecutor`, with the real stdout/exit code/timed-out fields flowing all the way back; and a genuinely sandbox-backed tool's output is still schema-validated, not exempted from that check. (One portability fix made while running these for real: a child `print()`'s line ending is `\r\n` on Windows vs `\n` elsewhere — assertions compare `.strip()`'d output, not exact bytes, so the suite is genuinely cross-platform rather than accidentally Windows-shaped.)
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's approved scope**: no `DockerSandbox` backend, no Capability Pack, no Architecture/Build/Test/Documentation Agent, no HTTP route, no `kernel/bootstrap.py` wiring (proving the dispatch path needed no real running-Kernel consumer — the unit tests are the end-to-end proof), and this codebase still has no `tool_invoker` package at all (re-confirmed by inspection, not newly discovered).
- `mypy --strict` (253 source files), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **549 passed** (539 previous + 10 new), zero regressions. **Environment note:** no Docker/Postgres needed again this step; the same cosmetic, non-blocking Windows `ResourceWarning` family from the previous step is observed a few more times (proportional to the number of new real-subprocess tests) — still not a test failure, still not reproduced as a Linux/CI concern.

### Stage B/C – The First Real, Thin Capability Pack: Architecture Agent (Third Increment Under the New Priority)

- **Read `implementation_status.md`, `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`, `capability_pack_contract.md`, `agent_architecture.md`, the manifest schema, and `manifest_schema.md` before writing any code, per this step's own context-discipline requirement — and found real, load-bearing gaps, reported rather than silently built around.** See the three "discoveries" below; none blocked proceeding, since each had a documented, reasoned resolution that respected every existing boundary this step's own framing named.
- **Discovery 1 — the manifest schema requires a real `CapabilityPack` entry point the moment a manifest declares any agent, but no such type exists in this codebase.** `platform_sdk/schemas/manifest.schema.json`'s own `allOf` rule makes `entryPoint` required once `agents`/`workflows` is non-empty (`capability_packs/_template/manifest.yaml`'s own comment already anticipates this exactly). platform_sdk.md §6/§7 documents the real, full `CapabilityPack`/`PackContext`/`PackRegistration`/`HealthReport` shape — but `PackContext` alone names fourteen optional services (a Tool Invoker, Event Bus, Workspace Service, Traceability Engine, Quality Gate Registry, Speech Gateway, and more), almost none of which exist as real Kernel services yet. Building all fourteen would itself be "invented architecture" (stand-in types for services with no real implementation), not a faithful reduction of a real one — so new `ai_os_kernel.capability_manager.pack_contract` implements only what this pack's one agent genuinely needs: `PackContext` (`pack_id`/`pack_version` only), `PackRegistration` (`agents` only, `tools` defaulted empty), `HealthReport` (`status` only), and the `CapabilityPack` Protocol itself — the identical "reduced slice now, additive later" precedent already used for `TraceContext`/`ContextRequest`/`ProviderCapabilities`. `SoftwareEngineeringPack` (`capability_packs/software-engineering/src/ai_os_pack_software_engineering/pack.py`) is a real, correct implementation of this reduced contract — not a stub that lies about what it does, even though nothing calls it yet (see Discovery 2).
- **Discovery 2 — nothing in this codebase automatically installs a pack's manifest into `catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools`, and nothing calls a pack's own `CapabilityPack.activate()`.** `ai_os_kernel.capability_manager.SqlPackLifecycleRepository.register()` only ever writes `catalog.packs`/`catalog.pack_state_transitions` — it never parses `manifest.agents[]`. This is a real, distinct, larger future Capability Manager increment (a real pack installer), not attempted here — this step's own tests seed `catalog.agents`/`catalog.prompts` directly via SQL, mirroring the identical precedent `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_registry.py` already established for every prior catalog-backed agent/tool row in this project's history. Registration and activation of the pack row itself still goes exclusively through the real `SqlPackLifecycleRepository`, per this step's own approved framing ("no new lifecycle mechanism").
- **Discovery 3 (the step's own named tension) — `EntrypointLoader`'s zero-argument construction and `PromptedAgent`'s real async constructor dependencies are still incompatible, and this step's own approved framing required a decided, recorded resolution, not a silent workaround.** `workflow_engine.prompted_agent`'s own docstring already documented this exact incompatibility and its prior resolution (`InMemoryAgentRegistry`only). **Resolution: a genuinely zero-argument-constructible wrapper that lazily builds its own real `PromptedAgent` on first `execute()` call.** New `ArchitectureAgentEntrypoint` (`agents/architecture.py`): `__init__()` takes no required arguments (satisfying `EntrypointLoader`/`SqlAgentRegistry` unmodified) and does no I/O; `execute()` — the one method that can genuinely `await` — lazily calls `build_anthropic_prompted_completion_service()` (unchanged, the identical real composition `kernel/bootstrap.py` itself already uses) exactly once, guarded by an `asyncio.Lock` so concurrent first calls build exactly one shared `PromptedAgent` instance (proven by a dedicated concurrency test, not asserted by inspection), then delegates to it unchanged. An optional, defaulted-`None` `service_factory` constructor parameter is the only new surface added — production code (`EntrypointLoader.load()`) never supplies it, so the zero-arg boundary is respected, not silently broken; a test supplies a deterministic (`EchoLLMGateway`-backed) factory instead. `output_schema` is a literal copy of `PromptedAgent.output_schema` (`{"content": <string>}`), since this wrapper's real output *is* whatever `PromptedAgent.execute()` returns, unmodified — "reuse, don't reinvent" applied to both the invocation logic and its declared contract.
- **A fourth, related compromise, recorded rather than silently accepted as normal: this pack imports Kernel internals directly.** capability_pack_contract.md's own "Platform Interaction Rules" name the Platform SDK as a pack's only sanctioned interaction surface — but no `ai-os-sdk` package exists yet (kernel's own `pyproject.toml`: "Planned, not yet scaffolded"). `agents/architecture.py` and the pack's own `pyproject.toml` both record this explicitly as a known, temporary compromise that a real Platform SDK migration should resolve later, not a pattern for future packs to copy uncritically.
- **New manifest**: `capability_packs/software-engineering/manifest.yaml` — `metadata.id: software-engineering`, one agent (`architect`, `modelAlias: coding-strong`, a real `entrypoint`/`inputSchema`/`outputSchema`), one prompt declaration (`architecture.propose_design`, `location: prompts/architecture_proposal.md`), `permissions: [llm:invoke]`, `entryPoint: ai_os_pack_software_engineering.pack:SoftwareEngineeringPack`. **Validated for real, not eyeballed**: `ai_os_kernel.manifest_loader.ManifestLoader.load_one()` was run against this exact file and returned successfully; `EntrypointLoader.load()` was run against both the agent's and the pack's own entrypoint strings and both genuinely constructed real objects (`isinstance(agent, Agent)` and the reduced `CapabilityPack` shape both hold).
- **A real second `uv` workspace member, not a documentation-only pack.** `capability_packs/software-engineering/pyproject.toml` (`ai-os-pack-software-engineering`, depending on `ai-os-kernel` directly per Discovery 4) — added to the root `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.uv.workspace]`/`[tool.uv.sources]`/`ruff`/`mypy` configuration. `uv lock` regenerated (93 packages resolved) and `uv sync --frozen --all-packages` verified against both the latest `uv` (0.11.32) and the CI-pinned `uv==0.5.11`, the identical dependency-change verification discipline every prior step introducing a new dependency has followed. `ai_os_pack_software_engineering` is genuinely `importlib`-importable in this same Python environment — confirmed by direct execution, not assumed.
- **New tests, in three tiers matching what this session's environment can and cannot verify:** `capability_packs/software-engineering/tests/test_pack.py` — **6 tests, genuinely executed, no database, no live LLM call**: `activate()`/`deactivate()`/`health()` are real; `ArchitectureAgentEntrypoint()` constructs with zero arguments and no eager I/O; concurrent first `execute()` calls build exactly one shared service; **the deterministic Echo-backed end-to-end proof this step's own requirement names** — a real `WorkflowStep` of type `agent` genuinely dispatches through the real `AgentStepExecutor` to this pack's real entrypoint, which genuinely renders a real prompt template and completes it via `EchoLLMGateway`, with the rendered content flowing back unmodified; the Pydantic input/output models construct validly. `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_architecture_agent_pack.py` (new, Kernel-side, Postgres via testcontainers) — a deterministic test that registers+activates the pack through the real `SqlPackLifecycleRepository` and seeds a real `catalog.agents` row, then proves `SqlAgentRegistry.resolve_agent()` genuinely resolves this pack's own entrypoint (the specific tension this step exists to resolve, provable without any live LLM call); an opt-in live test (skipped without `AIOS_SECRET_LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, mirroring `test_prompted_agent_live.py` exactly) goes one step further — a real `WorkflowStep`, dispatched through `AgentStepExecutor`/`SqlAgentRegistry`, genuinely completes against the live Anthropic API and returns a real architecture proposal.
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's approved scope**: no Build/Test/Documentation Agent, no human-approval gating for a proposed architecture (output capture only), no Tool/Workflow/Quality-Gate/Command declared by this pack, no HTTP route, no `kernel/bootstrap.py` wiring, no automated manifest -> catalog installer, no LICENSE file, no SDK pack contract test suite (it does not exist anywhere in this codebase to run yet).
- `mypy --strict` (260 source files, kernel + this pack together), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **555 passed** (549 Kernel unit tests + 6 new pack tests), zero regressions. **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session, so `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_architecture_agent_pack.py` could not be executed live (neither its deterministic-Postgres tier nor its opt-in-live tier, the latter also needing a real Anthropic key almost certainly absent too); verified instead by confirming the file collects and imports cleanly under `pytest --collect-only`, plus the manual `ManifestLoader`/`EntrypointLoader` verification described above, which exercises the same real code paths (manifest parsing, entrypoint construction, `isinstance` checks) this integration test would also exercise.

### Stage B/C – Build Agent: LLM Instruction to Real Sandboxed File Write (Fourth Increment Under the New Priority)

- **Read `implementation_status.md`, `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`, and the code needed for this step before writing any — confirmed the exact composition needed already exists (`ArchitectureAgentEntrypoint`'s zero-arg/lazy-build pattern, `SandboxedCommandTool`, `LocalSubprocessSandbox`) and no new architectural contradiction stood in the way.** One real, load-bearing gap was found during implementation itself, not before: `SandboxExecutor.execute()` had no mechanism for delivering real *data* (LLM-produced file content) to a sandboxed command safely — an argv string risks shell-quoting/length limits for a full file, and an environment variable has real OS-level size limits. Resolved by a small, additive Protocol extension, not a workaround: new optional `stdin: bytes | None = None` on `SandboxExecutor.execute()` (and, passed straight through, on `SandboxedCommandTool`) — defaulted `None`, so no existing caller's behaviour changes; delivered concurrently with output draining (a 4th task alongside stdout/stderr/wait, not written before the read loop starts, avoiding a pipe deadlock for larger content); best-effort (a command that never reads its stdin, or closes it early, does not fail the call — `BrokenPipeError`/`ConnectionResetError` are caught and swallowed, since not needing all the input is a legitimate outcome); and, when absent, the child's own stdin is `DEVNULL`, not inherited from the Kernel process — the identical "no ambient inheritance" principle already applied to environment variables.
- **New `ai_os_pack_software_engineering.agents.build.BuildAgentEntrypoint`** — the identical zero-argument-constructible, lazily-self-composing pattern `ArchitectureAgentEntrypoint` already established (see the previous step's own reasoning for why `PromptedAgent` cannot itself be `EntrypointLoader`'s zero-arg entrypoint), reused rather than reinvented, now additionally deferring creation of its own private sandbox working directory the same way (a `tempfile.mkdtemp()` call is I/O, so it happens on first `execute()`, lock-guarded, never in `__init__`). `execute()` composes two already-real pieces: a real `PromptedAgent` (the identical `build_anthropic_prompted_completion_service()` composition `architecture.py`'s own `_build_real_service()` already uses, deliberately not shared as a common helper — each agent owns its own small copy, ADR-0004's "no shared module needed for a single real caller each") produces a completion; that completion is parsed and genuinely written through a `SandboxedCommandTool` constructed fresh per call (its command depends on the model's own output, so a new instance per invocation is the correct, already-established shape for that Tool, not a limitation).
- **The LLM's file-write instruction is parsed from a fixed, delimited text format (`FILE_PATH: ...` / `FILE_CONTENT_BEGIN` / `FILE_CONTENT_END`), not JSON — a reasoned choice, not the only option.** JSON would require the model to correctly escape arbitrary file content (quotes, newlines, backslashes) inside a JSON string — an avoidable source of parse failures for exactly the payload (source code) this agent moves; a delimited format needs no escaping, since the content between the two markers is used verbatim. A new prompt, `prompts/build_write_file.md`, instructs the model to respond in exactly this format and nothing else — no prose, no markdown fences.
- **The model's own declared file path is independently validated before any sandbox call is made — the sandbox itself still cannot be trusted to contain a write, and this step's own approved framing required the boundary to be real.** New `_resolve_safe_relative_path()`: resolves the model's path against the working directory and checks containment via `is_relative_to()` on the *resolved, joined* path, not a pre-join syntactic check. **A genuine, non-obvious cross-platform escape was found and specifically guarded against**: checking only `Path.is_absolute()` misses a root-anchored-but-driveless path (`/etc/passwd` on Windows has a root but no drive, so `is_absolute()` is `False`) — joining such a path onto an existing one silently *discards the existing path's own tail* rather than raising (`WindowsPath("C:/work") / "/etc/passwd"` == `WindowsPath("C:/etc/passwd")`, not an error). Resolving the final joined path and comparing containment catches this and every other escape form (`..` traversal, absolute paths) uniformly, regardless of syntactic shape — the identical canonical-path-resolution discipline security_architecture.md §5.2 already mandates for Tier 2 operations, applied here defensively for a Tier 1 one.
- **The write itself happens through a small, portable, no-shell Python script executed inside the sandbox — never through this module's or the pack's own Python code touching the filesystem directly**, satisfying this step's own explicit constraint ("no direct/unsandboxed file writes from agent code") literally: the script reads file content from stdin and writes it to the (already-validated, relative) path it is given, creating parent directories for a nested path the same way any normal file write would.
- **No per-workflow workspace exists yet (security_architecture.md §5.3, still Stage C) — this agent's own private temporary directory is a documented placeholder, not a claim of real workspace isolation.** New `workingDirectory` output field, added specifically because a future Test Agent verifying what this agent wrote needs to know *where* — `filePath` alone is not enough when each agent instance's directory is otherwise undiscoverable.
- **New manifest entries**: `agents[]` gains `build` (`modelAlias: coding-strong`, `permissions: [llm:invoke, sandbox:execute]`, a real `entrypoint`/`inputSchema`/`outputSchema`); `prompts[]` gains `build.write_file`; the pack's own top-level `permissions` gains `sandbox:execute` (already in the closed permission vocabulary, ADR-0023). **Re-validated for real**: `ManifestLoader.load_one()` re-run against the updated file, `EntrypointLoader.load()` re-run against the new agent entrypoint string — both genuinely succeed.
- **New tests, all genuinely executed this session (no Docker/Postgres — a real OS subprocess needs neither):** `tests/unit/kernel/sandbox/test_executor.py` (+3 — real stdin delivery and echo-back, real `DEVNULL` non-inheritance when absent, real best-effort delivery when the command never reads stdin) and `tests/unit/kernel/workflow_engine/test_sandboxed_tool.py` (+1 — stdin passthrough). `capability_packs/software-engineering/tests/test_build_agent.py` (12 tests — see the corrected note on this figure earlier in this document's own §2 narrative for this step): zero-arg construction with no eager I/O; **the real end-to-end proof this step exists for** — a real `WorkflowStep` of type `agent`, dispatched through the real `AgentStepExecutor`, genuinely results in a real file existing on disk afterward, with content traceable to the (deterministic, `EchoLLMGateway`-backed) completion; nested-path creation; lazy working-directory creation when none is supplied; a malformed completion rejected before any sandbox call, with nothing written; every path-escape case (`..` traversal, POSIX-absolute, the Windows driveless-root case) rejected; the parser and path-validator tested directly in isolation; the Pydantic input/output models construct validly.
- **New Kernel-side integration test** (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_build_agent_pack.py`), mirroring `test_architecture_agent_pack.py` exactly: a deterministic tier (real Postgres, no live LLM call) proves `SqlAgentRegistry` genuinely resolves this pack's Build Agent entrypoint; an opt-in live tier (skipped without `AIOS_SECRET_LLM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) proves the full chain — a real `WorkflowStep`, `AgentStepExecutor`, `SqlAgentRegistry`, a real Anthropic completion, and a real sandboxed file write — using a self-contained, test-only prompt (not the pack's own shipped one, which needs a real Context Manager-supplied `context` variable this focused proof does not stand up, the identical choice `test_architecture_agent_pack.py` itself already makes).
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's approved scope**: no Test/Documentation Agent, no automatic Architecture -> Build pipeline (each agent proven independently; wiring one's output into the other is explicitly a later step), no `DockerSandbox` backend, no automated manifest -> catalog installer, no `kernel/bootstrap.py` wiring.
- `mypy --strict` (263 source files, kernel + this pack together), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **553 passed** (Kernel), **18 passed** (this pack, including the 6 from the previous step), zero regressions. **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session, so `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_build_agent_pack.py` could not be executed live; verified instead via `pytest --collect-only` (the file imports and collects cleanly) plus direct execution of every unit-testable real code path — the sandbox's own `stdin` handling, the parser, the path validator, and the full deterministic dispatch chain via `InMemoryAgentRegistry`, all of which are the identical underlying operations the integration test would also exercise.

### Stage B/C – Test Agent: Real Sandboxed Verification, No LLM Judgment (Fifth Increment Under the New Priority)

- **Read `implementation_status.md`, `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`, and the code needed for this step before writing any — found two real, load-bearing gaps during implementation, both resolved and recorded, neither blocking.**
- **Discovery 1 — `AgentStepExecutor` has no per-step mechanism to hand any agent arbitrary structured fields.** `_invocation_inputs()` only ever populates `promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias` (from a step's own declared fields) and `context` (from the Context Manager) — there is no way for a step to say "and also pass this agent a `workingDirectory`." Architecture and Build both never hit this because their real data flows through prompt rendering; a Test Agent that makes no LLM call has no prompt for anything to flow through. **Resolution**: new `_extract_payload()` accepts `workingDirectory`/`filePath`/`runCommand` either directly as top-level `inputs` keys (the primary, minimal, structured contract this step's own approved framing names — what a direct call, or a future real per-step input-mapping mechanism, would supply) **or**, when absent, as a single JSON object inside the Context Manager's own assembled `context` (flattened the same way `PromptedAgent._build_variables` already flattens it) — reusing the identical "context is the one real channel for external per-step data" principle Architecture/Build already established, with a JSON payload instead of free text since this agent's input is structured. Not a new input-mapping mechanism — the one that already exists, used the only way it can carry more than one named value.
- **Discovery 2 — the manifest schema itself assumes every agent uses a model.** `platform_sdk/schemas/manifest.schema.json`'s `agents[]` entry requires `modelAlias` unconditionally, with no escape hatch for an agent that calls no model at all — which this one deliberately does not. This module has no authority to change that schema (real, later Platform SDK/manifest-schema work, not "implementation details" this step's own framing grants freedom over). **Resolution**: the manifest still declares a syntactically-valid `modelAlias`, purely to satisfy the schema, with an explicit inline comment — and this module's own docstring and this document both record, prominently, that the Test Agent's real implementation never reads or uses it. `permissions` genuinely reflects reality throughout: `sandbox:execute` only, no `llm:invoke`.
- **New `ai_os_pack_software_engineering.agents.verification.TestAgentEntrypoint`** — deliberately **not** `PromptedAgent`-backed, taking this step's own constraint ("pass/fail must come from real sandboxed execution outcome, never from LLM judgment") to its cleanest conclusion: verifying an exit code needs no model call, so none is made. Simpler than `ArchitectureAgentEntrypoint`/`BuildAgentEntrypoint`: no `service_factory`, no lazily-built completion service, no lock — zero-argument construction is trivial, not merely satisfied, since nothing here needs building lazily at all. `filePath` is independently checked (resolved, containment-verified via `is_relative_to()`, existence-verified) before any sandbox call, before constructing a `SandboxedCommandTool` with the caller's exact `runCommand` — duplicating `build.py`'s own path-validation discipline rather than sharing it (the identical ADR-0004 "no shared module for a single real caller each" reasoning this pack's own `_build_real_service` duplication already established). `passed` is computed from `exitCode == 0 and not timedOut` only — the identical "written" success logic `BuildAgentEntrypoint` already uses, never from anything about `output`'s own content.
- **New manifest entry**: `agents[]` gains `test` (`permissions: [sandbox:execute]` only, a real `entrypoint`/`inputSchema`/`outputSchema`, `modelAlias` present-but-unused per Discovery 2 above, explicitly commented as such in the manifest itself). No new `prompts[]` entry — this agent uses none. **Re-validated for real**: `ManifestLoader.load_one()` re-run against the updated file, `EntrypointLoader.load()` re-run against the new agent entrypoint string — both genuinely succeed.
- **New tests, all genuinely executed this session (no Docker/Postgres — no LLM call, no network at all, the fastest-running test file across all three agents):** `capability_packs/software-engineering/tests/test_verification_agent.py` (12 tests) — zero-arg construction; a genuine passing case and a genuine failing case via direct `execute()`; a missing file, an escaping path, missing required fields, and a nonexistent working directory each rejected before any sandbox call; **the real end-to-end proof this step exists for, proven both ways** — a real `WorkflowStep` of type `agent`, dispatched through the real `AgentStepExecutor` with a real (fake-resolver-backed) Context Manager, genuinely runs a real file inside the sandbox and reports a genuinely correct pass outcome in one test and a genuinely correct fail outcome in another; a malformed JSON context payload rejected clearly; the input model constructs validly.
- **New Kernel-side integration test** (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_verification_agent_pack.py`), mirroring this pack's own precedent: a deterministic tier proving `SqlAgentRegistry` genuinely resolves this agent — and, **a genuinely discovered, positive difference from its two pack-mates, recorded explicitly**: a second tier needing *no live provider credentials at all*, since this agent makes no LLM call, proving a real passing and a real failing case through the `SqlAgentRegistry`-resolved instance directly, runnable unconditionally once Docker Desktop is available (no API-key skip marker anywhere in this file).
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's approved scope**: no Documentation Agent, no automatic Build -> Test pipeline (each agent still proven independently), no `DockerSandbox` backend, no automated manifest -> catalog installer, no test-framework auto-discovery, no `kernel/bootstrap.py` wiring.
- `mypy --strict` (266 source files, kernel + this pack together), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean.
