Unit suite: **571 passed** (Kernel, unchanged), **30 passed** (this pack, 18 previous + 12 new), zero regressions. **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session, so `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_verification_agent_pack.py` could not be executed live; verified instead via `pytest --collect-only` plus direct execution of every unit-testable real code path (both dispatch outcomes via `AgentStepExecutor`, the payload extractor, the path validator) — this step's own tests needed no Docker/live-key workaround to be *meaningfully* verified, only to be run through the real Postgres-backed `SqlAgentRegistry` specifically. A cosmetic, harmless `PytestCollectionWarning` is emitted whenever this agent's `Test`-prefixed classes are imported into a `test_*.py` file — each is skipped automatically (all declare `__init__`), nothing is ever mis-collected; not renamed, to preserve the `Architecture`/`Build`/`Test` naming symmetry this pack's own manifest and code deliberately share.

### Stage B/C – Documentation Agent: LLM-Recorded Build+Test Artifact, Sixth and Final Named Role for This Phase (Sixth Increment Under the New Priority)

- **Read `implementation_status.md`, `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`, and the code needed for this step before writing any — this step reused two already-established resolutions rather than discovering new gaps.** Both the "`PromptedAgent`-backed, lazy-build, zero-arg" pattern (Architecture/Build) and the "`context`-as-JSON dual-path structured input" pattern (Test) were combined, not extended with anything genuinely new to the codebase.
- **New `ai_os_pack_software_engineering.agents.documentation.DocumentationAgentEntrypoint`** — `PromptedAgent`-backed like Architecture/Build, since this role genuinely needs a model call (unlike Test, which needs none): describing what was built and why is exactly the free-text synthesis an LLM is the right tool for. Unlike Build, this agent's completion needs **no parsing at all** — the write target is never the model's to choose; it is derived deterministically as `"<filePath>.md"` from the Build Agent's own `filePath`, so the model's entire completion is used verbatim as the documentation content. Unlike Build, this agent creates no private working directory of its own — like Test, it reuses the caller-supplied `workingDirectory` directly, so the resulting `<filePath>.md` sits beside the file it describes.
- **A second real use of the Test Agent's own `context`-as-JSON dual-path resolution, extended to also feed a `PromptedAgent`'s own `variables` — not a new mechanism.** This agent's real input is six structured fields (a Build result's `workingDirectory`/`filePath`/`instruction` plus a Test outcome's `passed`/`exitCode`/`output`) — the identical `AgentStepExecutor`-has-no-per-step-structured-input gap `verification.py`'s own `_extract_payload()` already resolved, reused here verbatim for six fields instead of three. Once recovered, the fields are placed into a `variables` dict this module builds and forwards, alongside the caller's own `promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias`, into the internal `PromptedAgent.execute()` call — `PromptedAgent._build_variables`'s own already-documented `inputs.get("variables")` direct passthrough is the seam used, not a new one; the original `context` key is popped from the forwarded inputs first, since it has already been consumed, avoiding a redundant re-flattening `PromptedAgent` would otherwise perform into an unused `context` prompt variable.
- **The write path is exactly Build's own, reused, not reinvented**: content delivered to the identical portable, no-shell write script via the Sandbox Executor's own `stdin` parameter, through a real `SandboxedCommandTool` — per this step's own explicit constraint ("reuse SandboxedCommandTool/stdin delivery exactly as the Build Agent already established, do not invent a second file-write path"). `filePath` is independently checked twice before any sandbox *or* LLM call: `verification.py`'s own `_resolve_existing_file` (does the Build Agent's file genuinely still exist?) and `build.py`'s own `_resolve_safe_relative_path` (does the derived `.md` target stay contained?) — both duplicated, not imported, the same ADR-0004 reasoning this pack's every agent module already applies. **A real ordering bug was caught by this step's own test suite and corrected**: validation was initially placed after the lazy `PromptedAgent`/database-engine construction, which made malformed-input tests fail on a missing `AIOS_DATABASE_URL` instead of the intended validation error; moving the six-field extraction and both path checks before `_ensure_ready()` fixed this and, as a genuine side effect, means a real model call is never spent on an input this agent cannot safely act on.
- **New manifest entry**: `agents[]` gains `documentation` (`permissions: [llm:invoke, sandbox:execute]`, a real `entrypoint`/`inputSchema`/`outputSchema`); `prompts[]` gains `documentation.record_artifact` (`prompts/documentation_record_artifact.md`, instructing the model to respond with the Markdown document and nothing else — the identical "exactly this format, nothing else" discipline Build's own prompt already establishes, without delimiters, since there is nothing left for anything downstream to parse back out). **Re-validated for real**: `ManifestLoader.load_one()` re-run against the updated file — confirms all four agent ids (`architect`, `build`, `test`, `documentation`) — `EntrypointLoader.load()` re-run against the new agent entrypoint string — both genuinely succeed.
- **New tests, all genuinely executed this session (no Docker/Postgres for the deterministic tier):** `capability_packs/software-engineering/tests/test_documentation_agent.py` (12 tests) — zero-arg construction; **the real end-to-end proof this step exists for, for *both* Test outcomes** — a real `WorkflowStep` of type `agent`, dispatched through the real `AgentStepExecutor` with a real (fake-resolver-backed) Context Manager, genuinely writes a real Markdown file recording a genuinely passing case in one test and a genuinely failing case in another, with the written file's own content asserted equal to the agent's own returned `content`; a direct-`inputs` call proving the dual-path extraction's other branch, without any Context Manager involved at all; a missing file, a nonexistent working directory, missing required fields, and a malformed JSON context payload each rejected clearly before any LLM call; both duplicated path-safety functions' own unit tests; the Pydantic input model constructs validly.
- **New Kernel-side integration test** (`tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_documentation_agent_pack.py`), mirroring this pack's own precedent for its deterministic tier (`SqlAgentRegistry` genuinely resolves this agent) — and, **a genuinely discovered, positive difference from Architecture's/Build's own live tests, recorded explicitly**: this agent's opt-in live tier uses this pack's own real shipped prompt (`prompts/documentation_record_artifact.md`) directly, not a self-contained test-only substitute, because this agent's structured input is supplied via direct `inputs` keys (`_extract_payload`'s own primary path), never requiring the Context Manager round trip Build's/Architecture's own `{{context}}`-based prompts need for their own live tests.
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's own approved scope**: no automatic Architecture -> Build -> Test -> Documentation pipeline (each of the four agents remains independently proven; chaining is explicitly the next step, per the product owner's own framing), no Docker backend, no automated manifest -> catalog installer, no new persistence/templating system — the documentation artifact is a plain Markdown file written through the sandbox, exactly as this step's own approved framing names, not a `knowledge.documents` row or any other new persistence target.
- `mypy --strict` (269 source files, kernel + this pack together), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **553 passed** (Kernel), **42 passed** (this pack, 30 previous + 12 new), **595 passed** combined, zero regressions from this step's own changes (one unrelated, pre-existing, timing-sensitive test in `test_local_adapter.py` flaked once under full-suite load and passed cleanly in isolation — not caused by this step). **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session, so `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_documentation_agent_pack.py` could not be executed live; verified instead via `pytest --collect-only` (2 tests collect cleanly) plus direct execution of every unit-testable real code path (both dispatch outcomes via `AgentStepExecutor`, the payload extractor, both path validators).

### Stage B/C – Delivery Pipeline: Architecture → Build → Test → Documentation Chained Into One Real Workflow (Seventh Increment Under the New Priority — First Complete Product Loop)

- **Read `implementation_status.md`, `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`, `workflow_architecture.md`'s Step Contract section, and the code needed for this step before writing any.** The Step Contract section's own words shaped this step's design directly: its five invocation fields are "never a computed value, a cross-step reference, a template, or conditional logic" — read as ruling out a new `WorkflowStep` field for a cross-step reference, not as ruling out chaining itself (that document's own Context Management section separately, explicitly names "Previous workflow state" as a sanctioned context source, without specifying how it reaches an agent — this step is that "how").
- **New `ai_os_kernel.context_manager.resolvers.WorkflowStepOutputResolver`** — the one genuinely missing piece this step's own approved framing named. A second real resolver for the same "Workflow State" source `context_manager.md` §3 already documents (sibling to `WorkflowStateResolver`, not a new `SourceType`; §5's own `required_context_types` example list already names `previous_outputs` explicitly). Configured with a flat, statically-declared `step_sources: Mapping[str, str | Sequence[str]]` (`{consuming_step_id: source_step_id(s)}` — "this step's input includes the named output of step X," the smallest data structure that says exactly that), reading a named prior step's real, persisted output (`workflow_steps.outputs`, via `WorkflowInstanceRepository.list_steps()`, picking the highest `attempt` for a given step name). Two small, optional per-step knobs, both still flat and statically declared: `field_selectors` (extract one named field verbatim, for a consumer wanting free text fed into a prompt's own `{{context}}` variable) and `output_transforms` (a real, narrow Python callable applied to the merged output before encoding — used exactly once by this pipeline's own composition, never declared inline in any workflow definition file). Returns no items — not an error — for a step absent from `step_sources`, or when a referenced source step has not (yet) produced a real output: the identical "cannot be checked, not an error" shape `WorkflowStateResolver` already established.
- **New `ai_os_pack_software_engineering.pipeline` module and `workflows/delivery_pipeline.yaml`** — the first genuine consumer of the already-real, previously-unused `ai_os_kernel.workflow_engine.loader.WorkflowDefinitionLoader` in this codebase's own history (every prior `WorkflowDefinition` in this project, including `kernel/bootstrap.py`'s own demo, was constructed directly in Python, for the documented reason that no real pack directory existed yet to load one from — one now does). The YAML declares four `agent` steps in order (`architecture`, `build`, `test`, `documentation`), each naming a real, pack-qualified `agentId` (`software-engineering/architect`, etc.). `pipeline.py` owns this pipeline's own configuration of the generic resolver above: `build` reads `architecture`'s own `content` field as free text; `test` reads `build`'s own whole output, enriched with the one deliberate exception — `runCommand = [sys.executable, filePath]`, since the Build Agent's own already-shipped contract has no reason to decide how its own file is run; `documentation` reads **both** `build`'s and `test`'s own outputs merged (`build` first, `test` second — `test`'s own `exitCode` therefore wins over `build`'s own same-named field, a declared merge order, never a computed value), which already contains every field Documentation's own contract needs, with no transform required. `build_pipeline_trigger()` mirrors `kernel/bootstrap.py`'s own `_build_workflow_trigger` shape exactly (create → start → run-to-completion via the existing `WorkflowAdvanceRunner`) — composition only, no new Workflow Engine capability — and lives in the pack, not the Kernel, since every step here needs the pack's own `SqlAgentRegistry`-resolvable agents.
- **A genuine, discovered bug — found by actually running the four-step chain end to end, not by inspection.** `WorkflowStateResolver` has no per-step concept of its own: it unconditionally contributes the workflow instance's own top-level `inputs` to *every* agent step's assembled context — correct, existing, unchanged behaviour, since every prior real caller of it had only ever had one agent step to worry about. Included naively alongside the new resolver in one shared `DefaultContextManager`, this corrupted the clean single-JSON/single-text payload the `build`/`test`/`documentation` steps each expect: `DefaultContextManager.assemble()` concatenates every resolver's own items, and `PromptedAgent`'s/this pack's own `_extract_payload()`'s own `"\n\n".join(...)` flattening then produced two JSON objects (or two texts) joined by a blank line — a `json.JSONDecodeError` for `test`, and an invalid, multi-line Python string literal for `build`'s own generated file. **Resolution**: `_StepScopedResolver`, a small, pipeline-owned wrapper (in `pipeline.py`, not the Kernel) restricting `WorkflowStateResolver`'s own contribution to the `architecture` step alone — the one step that genuinely needs it (no prior step to read from) — with zero changes to `WorkflowStateResolver` itself.
- **Docker Desktop was unavailable yet again this session — both tiers of the real integration test need a real Postgres container, a genuinely discovered fact about this specific test.** Every prior single-agent test in this pack could stay deterministic without one (`InMemoryAgentRegistry`, `AgentStepExecutor.execute()` called directly, no persistence at all); this test's own job is to prove a step's real, *persisted* output reaches the next step's real input, which only exists once `SqlWorkflowInstanceRepository.advance_workflow()` has actually written it — there is no way to prove that hand-off without the real, Postgres-backed instance/step machinery underneath, regardless of whether the agents themselves make a real LLM call. **In its place, a manual, ad-hoc end-to-end trace was run this session** against a fake, in-memory `WorkflowInstanceRepository` stand-in exposing only `get_instance`/`list_steps`, driving the real `AgentStepExecutor`, the real `WorkflowStepOutputResolver`, and all four real, completely unmodified pack agents through the same code paths production would use. This is what caught the bug above (on the first run) and, after the fix, confirmed for real: a design proposal's own real content reaching Build's own real prompt (read back from Build's own persisted `instruction` field, never hand-copied by the trace itself); a real, valid file genuinely written; a real Test Agent genuinely running it and reporting a real `passed: true, exitCode: 0`; and a real Documentation Agent genuinely recording that real outcome to a real Markdown file on disk. Not a substitute for the real Postgres-backed test (unexecuted this session — the single highest-priority re-verification item once Docker Desktop is available), but materially stronger than `--collect-only` alone.
- **New tests**: `tests/unit/kernel/context_manager/test_resolvers.py` (+8) — `WorkflowStepOutputResolver`'s own whole-object JSON encoding, single-field extraction, multi-source merge with declared collision order (later step wins), transform application, three distinct no-items cases (absent from `step_sources`, source step not yet completed, only one of several sources ready), and highest-`attempt` selection. `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_delivery_pipeline.py` (2, collect cleanly, unexecuted this session — see above) — a deterministic full-chain proof (`InMemoryAgentRegistry`, all four real agents, `EchoLLMGateway`-backed completion services for the three `PromptedAgent`-backed ones, no pack registration needed at all since `pack_id` is a plain, unenforced string column) and an opt-in live one (the real `SqlAgentRegistry`, seeded `catalog.agents` rows for all four agents). **A genuinely discovered, positive difference for the live tier**: unlike every prior single-agent live test in this pack (each needed a self-contained, test-only substitute prompt specifically because their own `{{context}}` placeholder needed a real Context Manager/workflow-instance round trip no single-agent test stood up), this test's own live tier seeds and uses all three real, shipped prompts directly — this pipeline finally *is* that round trip, for real, end to end.
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's own approved scope**: no human-approval gating between steps, no `DockerSandbox` backend, no automated manifest → catalog installer, no new HTTP route (the `build_pipeline_trigger()` closure is callable but unrouted, exactly like `kernel/bootstrap.py`'s own demo trigger before its own later step added one), no second pipeline or general-purpose chaining mechanism beyond this one, fixed, four-step definition — the manifest's new `workflows[]` entry (`se.delivery_pipeline`) is schema-valid and re-validated, but nothing in this codebase automatically installs a manifest-declared workflow into `catalog.workflow_definitions` either (the identical, already-recorded "no automated installer" gap every catalog-backed entity in this codebase shares).
- `mypy --strict` (273 source files, kernel + this pack together), `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` all clean. Unit suite: **603 passed** (553 Kernel + 8 new resolver tests + 42 pre-existing pack tests), zero regressions. **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session — see this section's own bug/manual-trace bullets above for how this step's real end-to-end proof was verified in its place; re-running `tests/integration/workflow_engine/test_delivery_pipeline.py` (both tiers) is the single highest-priority re-verification item once Docker Desktop is available again.

### Stage B/C – DockerSandbox: A Real ADR-0016 Tier 1 Sandbox Backend (Eighth Increment Under the New Priority)

- **Read `implementation_status.md`, `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`, ADR-0016, and `security_architecture.md` §5.1 before writing any code — both documents converged on an identical, specific control list, used directly as this step's own checklist**: network disabled by default, read-only root with one writable working-copy mount plus `tmpfs` scratch, non-root `--user`, `--cap-drop=ALL`/`--security-opt=no-new-privileges`, default seccomp retained (never `--privileged`/`seccomp=unconfined`), memory/CPU/PID/wall-clock limits from configuration, no Docker socket or extra host mounts, no secrets, and ephemeral per-step lifetime.
- **New `ai_os_kernel.sandbox.docker_executor.DockerSandbox`** — implements the existing `SandboxExecutor` Protocol unchanged (`guarantees` property, `execute()` with the identical signature `LocalSubprocessSandbox` already has); no caller anywhere in this codebase needed to change. Every construction parameter (`image`, `mem_limit`, `nano_cpus`, `pids_limit`, `user`, `tmpfs_size`) is a named, documented, overridable default — and, per this step's own explicit constraint, there is no parameter that could weaken isolation (no network-isolation opt-out, no extra-mount parameter, no way to request `privileged` or `seccomp=unconfined`).
- **Every Tier 1 control genuinely requested from the Docker Engine, not merely intended**: `network_mode="none"`; `read_only=True` plus exactly one `volumes={...}` entry (the caller's own `working_directory`, resolved, bind-mounted read-write at `/workspace`) plus one `tmpfs={"/tmp": "size=64m"}` scratch mount; `user="65534:65534"` (nobody:nogroup) by default; `cap_drop=["ALL"]`; `security_opt=["no-new-privileges"]`; `mem_limit`/`nano_cpus`/`pids_limit` passed straight through to the container's own cgroup limits. `SandboxGuarantees` for this backend: all five fields `True` — verified directly against `LocalSubprocessSandbox`'s own honest 3/5 in a dedicated test.
- **Timeout and output-cap enforcement, honestly documented as mechanically different from `LocalSubprocessSandbox`'s own live, eager-kill-on-cap-exceeded draining loop, while preserving an identical outcome guarantee.** `container.wait(timeout=...)` is raced against the deadline; a `requests.exceptions.ReadTimeout` triggers an explicit `container.kill()` and `timed_out=True`, with no partial output preserved (the identical "simple, easy to reason about" choice `LocalSubprocessSandbox`'s own docstring already commits to). When the command finishes in time, its full stdout/stderr are fetched from the Docker Engine's own log buffer and truncated client-side to `max_output_bytes` per stream — a container is not killed the instant a cap is exceeded the way a bare subprocess is here, but the caller-visible contract is identical: never more than `max_output_bytes` per stream, ever, and the wall-clock timeout remains a hard backstop regardless.
- **"Docker not available" is a clear, typed `DockerSandboxUnavailableError`, confirmed for real against this session's own genuinely-unavailable daemon.** `DockerSandbox()` construction does zero I/O; the real `docker.DockerClient` is built and `ping()`-checked, lock-guarded, only on the first `execute()` call — any `docker.errors.DockerException`/connection failure at that point is caught and re-raised with a clear message, never left as a raw third-party exception.
- **A genuine, discovered, load-bearing limitation, explicitly out of this step's own scope to fix — recorded, not silently patched.** This pack's own Build/Test/Documentation agents construct `command` using `sys.executable` — a host-specific absolute interpreter path with no reason to exist inside this backend's own minimal container image. Running those exact, already-shipped commands against `DockerSandbox` unmodified would fail outright. This step's own approved framing explicitly forbids touching agent/pack code to fix this; it is recorded here and in §5/§6 as the necessary prerequisite before `DockerSandbox` can become any agent's own default.
- **New dependency**: `docker>=7.1,<8.0` added to `kernel/pyproject.toml`'s own `dependencies` (previously only an effective transitive dependency via `testcontainers`, a dev-only one) — `uv.lock` regenerated, `uv sync --frozen --all-packages` re-run. One new, narrowly-scoped mypy override (`module = "docker.*"`, `ignore_missing_imports = true`) records that `docker` ships no types and no stub package exists.
- **A deliberate, recorded first departure from this project's own "real fakes, never mocks" testing convention.** `tests/unit/kernel/sandbox/test_docker_executor.py` (13 tests) uses `unittest.mock` — the stdlib, no new dependency — because `docker`'s own client/container classes are third-party and cannot be meaningfully re-implemented as an in-process fake the way every other Protocol in this codebase already is. Scoped narrowly to proving this backend's own Python code asks the Docker Engine for the right thing (every Tier 1 control individually asserted against the real `containers.create()` call's own kwargs, timeout-triggers-kill, output-cap truncation, container-always-removed, lazy client construction, stdin-failure-never-raises) — never that Docker itself enforces anything, which only a real daemon can prove.
- **New Kernel-side integration test** (`tests/integration/sandbox/test_docker_sandbox_live.py`, 7 tests) — the real, unmocked proof: a real container's own socket-connect attempt genuinely fails (network isolation); a real write inside the mounted working directory genuinely succeeds and is genuinely visible on the host afterward, while a real write anywhere else on the container's own root genuinely fails with "Read-only file system" (filesystem containment); a real non-root UID; a real timeout-triggered kill; genuine container removal after every call (verified against the real Docker Engine's own container list, not merely trusted); and real stdin delivery over `docker`'s own raw `attach_socket` API — the single most platform/version-sensitive mechanism in this backend. Gated by a `docker_available` fixture that pings the daemon and skips cleanly, with a clear reason, on failure — the identical opt-in-live shape (ADR-0015) this project's own LLM-key-gated tests already use.
- **Docker Desktop was unavailable yet again this session — all 7 live tests collect and skip cleanly; none could be executed for real.** A genuine, positive fact recorded rather than left implicit: GitHub Actions' own standard runners ship Docker pre-installed and running, and `ci.yml`'s own `integration` job already runs `pytest tests/unit tests/integration -v` — unlike every prior Docker-gated test in this project's history (which needed testcontainers' own separate Docker dependency to run Postgres, and were *also* blocked in CI-equivalent local runs only because this specific development machine lacks Docker Desktop), this suite should genuinely execute, unskipped, the next time CI runs.
- **Still deliberately narrow, exactly per this step's own approved scope**: no agent/pack/Workflow Engine code changed at all; `LocalSubprocessSandbox` untouched and still the default everywhere it already was; `DockerSandbox` is not wired in as any agent's own default (a small, separate decision, explicitly deferred here — see the `sys.executable`-portability limitation above); no network-isolation escape hatch or per-tool override mechanism of any kind — one consistent Tier 1 posture, no configurable weakening; the default image (`python:3.12-slim`) is tag-pinned, not digest-pinned (security_architecture.md §10's own aspirational target) — recorded as a real, later hardening step, the identical "documented hardening path, not a redesign" treatment ADR-0016 itself already gives gVisor.
- `mypy --strict` (276 source files) clean, `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean. Unit suite: **616 passed** (603 previous + 13 new), zero regressions. **Environment note:** Docker Desktop was unavailable again this session — the 7-test live suite collects and skips cleanly; re-running it, and the still-unexecuted `test_delivery_pipeline.py` from the previous step, are jointly the single highest-priority re-verification item once Docker Desktop is available.

### Stage B/C – Documentation Reconciliation, Fixture Fix, and Real DockerSandbox Verification (Health-Audit Follow-Up — Not a New Increment)

- **Read `implementation_status.md`, `DOCUMENTATION_INDEX.md`, `agents.md`, `workflows.md`, and every doc the health audit named before changing any of them.** This step's own approved framing was explicit that it is a reconciliation step, not development — no new features, and "zero behaviour change" for every rename, proven rather than assumed.
- **Agent/workflow naming fork resolved.** `architect` → `architecture`, `test` → `qa-test`, renamed in `manifest.yaml`, `pack.py`, `delivery_pipeline.yaml` (`agents[]` and every step's own `agentId` — **not** the workflow-local `steps[].id`, a deliberately different, unrenamed namespace), and every pack-level/Kernel-level test's own agent-id constant. `documentation` already matched `agents.md`'s own catalog exactly — confirmed, not changed. `build` had no genuine fit among the other 14 documented ids; `agents.md`'s own Agent Catalog gained a new, honestly-scoped entry for it instead of a force-fit or a silent, undocumented id. `se.delivery_pipeline` was checked directly against `workflows.md` §4's own `se.implement_task` and found to genuinely diverge in shape, purpose, and participating agents — kept as its own id, given its own real section in `workflows.md` rather than forced into that shape. Both docs gained a "Currently Implemented Subset" section naming exactly which agents/workflows are real today (4 of 16; 1 of 7) so a fresh reader is never misled. **Verified zero behaviour change twice**: the full existing test suite (616 tests) passes with only id-literal changes, and the manual end-to-end pipeline trace was re-run with the new ids, producing byte-for-byte identical output to the pre-rename run.
- **Every doc-drift gap the audit named is now closed**: `context_manager.md` §4 and `workflow_architecture.md`'s Context Management section record `WorkflowStepOutputResolver`; `capability_pack_contract.md` carries a dated, explicit exception note for the real, live direct-Kernel-import compromise; `manifest_schema.md` (new validation rule 11, renumbering the rest) and `platform_sdk/schemas/manifest.schema.json` itself (a real, tested `if`/`then`: `modelAlias` required only when `permissions` contains `llm:invoke`) were both fixed — the QA/Test Agent's manifest entry no longer carries the now-unnecessary decoy value; `llm_gateway.md` §15's dead `platform_sdk/contracts/llm.py` reference now points at the real files; ADR-0016 gained an additive "Implementation naming note" (not a rewrite — this codebase's ADR process has no in-place-amend path for an Accepted decision) cross-referenced from `security_architecture.md` §5.1, recording `SandboxRuntime`/`SandboxExecutor` as the same seam under two names.
- **New shared test helper, `tests/integration/_postgres_fixture.py`** (`postgres_container()`), replacing the identical, independently-repeated `PostgresContainer(...)` construction line across all ~30 of this tree's own `database_url` fixtures — the one real, current, thirty-times-repeated case ADR-0004 itself says justifies a shared helper. A genuinely unreachable daemon now raises a clean `pytest.skip()` with a clear reason. **Verified both directions directly**: `DOCKER_HOST` forced to a nonexistent pipe produced a clean, fast (~2 second), clearly-reasoned skip (16/16, no raw exception); the real suite then ran successfully with Docker genuinely available (below).
- **Docker Desktop became available partway through this step — the first time in this project's history — and the resulting real run found and fixed two genuine bugs in `DockerSandbox`.** (1) `containers.create()` does not implicitly pull a missing image the way `docker run` does — fixed by pulling explicitly and retrying once on `ImageNotFound`. (2) A real Windows/npipe-specific bug: every stdin-delivery attempt hung until the wall-clock timeout, traced by direct, live debugging to `NpipeSocket.shutdown()` being implemented as a full `close()` rather than a half-close, combined with this backend's own `detach=True` leaving the container's `StdinOnce` config field unset — the daemon had no configured signal to treat the closed attach socket as stdin EOF. Fixed by using `detach=False` (verified: previously-hanging `cat` now exits the instant its stdin closes), which has no other effect on this class's own control flow. A regression-locking assertion (`kwargs["detach"] is False`) was added to the existing mocked unit test.
- **With both fixed, all 7 real, unmocked `DockerSandbox` live guarantee tests pass against a genuine daemon** — network isolation, filesystem containment (both directions), non-root identity, timeout-kill, guaranteed removal, and real stdin delivery, none of which had ever been confirmed against a real daemon before this step. **A full real run of the entire integration suite reports 218 passed, 10 skipped (correctly, no live API key), 0 failed** — the single largest verification-debt closure in this project's history, executed in one sitting rather than accumulated further. Every container this session's own runs created was confirmed cleanly removed afterward.
- **Still deliberately a reconciliation/verification step, not new development**: no new agent, workflow, or Kernel capability was built; the only non-documentation code changes are the id renames, the schema conditional fix, the shared Postgres fixture helper, and the two real `DockerSandbox` bug fixes the newly-available daemon surfaced. `DockerSandbox` is still not wired in as any agent's own default — the `sys.executable`-portability gap recorded in the previous step's own §5 is unchanged and remains the prerequisite.
- `mypy --strict` (277 source files) clean, `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean. Unit suite: **616 passed**, zero regressions. **Integration suite, genuinely executed: 218 passed, 10 skipped, 0 failed.** One unrelated, pre-existing, timing-sensitive flake (`test_multi_provider_routing.py`) under full-suite load, passed cleanly in isolation — not caused by this step.

## 4. What Remains in Stage A / Before Stage B's Workflow Engine Can Execute

Cross-checked against `implementation_roadmap.md` §3. Not yet done:

| Gap | Roadmap reference | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Secrets resolution: file backend, Vault, cloud, Access Broker, TTL cache/rotation, and wiring into any consumer** | ADR-0024 | `SecretProvider`/`EnvSecretProvider` (env backend only) exist, this step — see §3. Configuration Manager's layer 7 (secrets) still does not resolve references; nothing calls `EnvSecretProvider` yet. |
| **OpenTelemetry: OTLP export, more metrics, Compose observability profile** | ADR-0017 | Real span creation and one real counter (`aios.http.requests`) exist (console exporters only, see §3). No OTLP exporter, no Collector, no Compose profile (Prometheus/Tempo/Loki/Grafana), no propagation across process/sandbox boundaries, and only one metric exists — none of the workflow/LLM/gate/sandbox metrics `observability_stack.md` §3.1 lists (those subsystems don't exist yet either). |
| **Real external tool execution and sandboxing — a real `tier1_sandboxed` Tool genuinely dispatches, and a real ADR-0016 Tier 1 sandbox backend now exists, but nothing yet uses it in place of `LocalSubprocessSandbox`, and no real running-Kernel consumer calls a tool step through either** | Stage B/C, ADR-0016 | `EchoTool` remains in-process and trivial; there is still no Tool Invoker, and no real workflow (via `kernel/bootstrap.py`/HTTP) that declares a tool step naming a `SandboxedCommandTool`. `ai_os_kernel.workflow_engine.sandboxed_tool.SandboxedCommandTool` genuinely executes a command through an injected `SandboxExecutor`, and `ToolStepExecutor` genuinely dispatches to it — proven end to end. Two real `SandboxExecutor` backends now exist (see §3): `LocalSubprocessSandbox` (`SandboxGuarantees` 3/5 — honestly no network/filesystem containment; still every agent's own default) and the new `ai_os_kernel.sandbox.docker_executor.DockerSandbox` (`SandboxGuarantees` 5/5 — network disabled, filesystem contained, non-root, real resource limits, genuinely satisfying ADR-0016's Tier 1 bar). Neither `SandboxedCommandTool` nor any pack agent has been switched to use `DockerSandbox` yet — a genuine, recorded blocker (§5) prevents it as-is: the pack's own agents construct commands using `sys.executable`, a host-specific path that does not exist inside `DockerSandbox`'s own container image. The command a `SandboxedCommandTool` runs is still fixed at construction time, not driven by any per-step input. This pack's own Architecture Agent does not declare or use `SandboxedCommandTool` directly at all (the Build/Test/Documentation agents compose it internally, by construction, not as a manifest-declared Tool of their own). |
| **The first real, thin Capability Pack now has all four of its currently-planned agents — Architecture (design only), Build (genuinely writes a file through the sandbox), Test (genuinely runs a file through the sandbox, no LLM call), and Documentation (genuinely calls an LLM to record a Build+Test result, writing a Markdown file through the same sandbox) — and now a real, declared `workflows[]` entry genuinely chaining all four into one pipeline; still no automated installer, no Tool/Quality-Gate/Command declared, no human-approval gating** | Stage B/C, capability_pack_contract.md, platform_sdk.md §6/§7, ADR-0016 | `capability_packs/software-engineering` (see §3): a schema-valid `manifest.yaml`, a real `SoftwareEngineeringPack` `CapabilityPack` implementation (a new, deliberately reduced `ai_os_kernel.capability_manager.pack_contract`), `ArchitectureAgentEntrypoint` (design proposal only, no code), `BuildAgentEntrypoint`, `TestAgentEntrypoint`, and now `DocumentationAgentEntrypoint` — all four zero-argument-constructible and genuinely resolvable through `SqlAgentRegistry`; Architecture/Build/Documentation lazily self-compose a real `PromptedAgent` on first use, while Test makes no LLM call at all (see §3) — the pipeline's only agent not `PromptedAgent`-backed. The Build Agent composes a real `SandboxedCommandTool` to genuinely write an LLM-produced file to disk, through a small, portable, no-shell script fed via a `SandboxExecutor`/`SandboxedCommandTool` `stdin` parameter; the Test Agent composes the same `SandboxedCommandTool` to genuinely run an already-written file's own command and report its real exit code; the Documentation Agent reuses the identical write script/`stdin` path to genuinely write a real Markdown record of a Build+Test result, with no completion-parsing needed at all (its target path is derived deterministically from the Build Agent's own `filePath`, never chosen by the model) — all three sandbox uses proven end to end by real, non-mocked tests. Registered/activated through the real `SqlPackLifecycleRepository`; `catalog.agents`/`catalog.prompts` rows are still seeded directly by tests, since **no automated manifest -> catalog installer exists**, and **nothing in this codebase calls `CapabilityPack.activate()`** — both real, discovered, recorded gaps, not simulated, unchanged since prior steps. This pack imports Kernel internals directly (no Platform SDK package exists yet) — a documented, temporary compromise. `AgentStepExecutor` genuinely has no per-step mechanism for arbitrary structured fields beyond `context` — a real, discovered gap the Test Agent's own `_extract_payload()` works around by reusing the Context Manager's `context` channel with a JSON payload, and the Documentation Agent's own second, real use of that same resolution (six fields instead of three, now also feeding a `PromptedAgent`'s `variables`) confirms it generalizes rather than being a one-off; the manifest schema's own unconditional `modelAlias` requirement, genuinely unused by the Test Agent, is the same kind of recorded-not-hidden mismatch. **A real, declared `workflows[]` entry now exists** (`se.delivery_pipeline`, `workflows/delivery_pipeline.yaml`) — the pack's own `ai_os_pack_software_engineering.pipeline` module chains all four agents through a new, generic, Kernel-level `WorkflowStepOutputResolver` (see §3), the first genuine consumer of the previously-real-but-unused `WorkflowDefinitionLoader`. No Tool/Quality-Gate/Command is *declared* by this pack; no human-approval gating for anything any agent produces; no automated manifest -> catalog installer for agents, prompts, *or* workflows. |
| **LLM Gateway has a Protocol seam, one Observability writer, two real provider adapters, a real composition path, a real caller of it, a genuinely multi-provider Router, all three of the Retry & Fallback Manager's named pieces, a real Error Taxonomy driving all three of them, two independent, real Policy & Budget Enforcer ceilings (per-alias and per-workflow) plus request metadata (`TraceContext`), and now a real Capability Negotiator matrix lookup — but most of its documented internal subsystems remain unbuilt, and only one provider is registered in the checked-in production config** | llm_gateway.md §3/§4/§6/§9/§10, platform_sdk.md §4.1/§5.1 | `LLMGateway`/`EchoLLMGateway`, `SqlLLMCallRecorder`, `AnthropicAdapter`, `LocalAdapter` (both classifying every failure via `error_taxonomy`, extracting a real `Retry-After` header when present), `build_anthropic_prompted_completion_service()` (accepts `additional_gateways`, `circuit_breaker`, `backoff_policy`, `budget_enforcer`, `workflow_budget_enforcer`, `capability_negotiator`), `PromptedAgent`, `Router`/`StaticRouter` (per-alias `RoutingDecision`, carrying a real, optional `fallback` chain), `DispatchingLLMGateway` (checks an optional alias-scoped `BudgetEnforcer`, then an optional workflow-scoped one (populated only when `LLMRequest.metadata.workflow_id` is set) — both reusing each adapter's own honest `usage.cost_usd`, neither ever triggering a fallback since both ceilings are scoped to something other than the provider — then an optional `CircuitBreaker`, then retries a candidate per an optional `BackoffPolicy` only when `retriable`, honouring a real `retry_after_seconds`, before walking `fallback` on any other still-failing `LLMProviderError`; now also exposes a separate, synchronous `capabilities(alias)` method, unconsulted by `complete()` itself), `CircuitBreaker`/`InMemoryCircuitBreaker`, `BackoffPolicy`, `error_taxonomy` (`ErrorCategory`, `classify_http_status()`, `parse_retry_after_seconds()`, now also `NO_CAPABILITIES`), `BudgetEnforcer`/`PerScopeBudgetEnforcer` (renamed from `PerAliasBudgetEnforcer` once a second, workflow-scoped use existed — see §3), `TraceContext` (`llm_gateway/models.py` — a deliberately minimal slice of platform_sdk.md §4.1's canonical shape, carrying only `workflow_id`/`step_id`; see §3), and new `CapabilityNegotiator`/`StaticCapabilityNegotiator`/`ProviderCapabilities` (`llm_gateway/capability_negotiator.py` — llm_gateway.md §6's full matrix, config-driven, keyed by model id; see §3) exist. Still not built: provider health beyond the breaker's own binary signal, experiment pinning, allowed-model policy (`SecurityError` — this project's `ErrorCategory` deliberately excludes `security`), per-principal rate limiting, context-window-fit pre-checks (now possible in principle — the matrix and `max_input_tokens` exist — but still needs `count_tokens()`, unbuilt), per-step/per-experiment budgets (only per-alias and per-workflow cost exist now), the four capability *degradation rules* themselves (use/emulate/fail/never-emulate — the matrix exists, nothing consumes it), tool calling, structured output, streaming, embeddings, prompt caching, `agent_id`/`experiment_id`/`run_id`/`trace_id`/`span_id` on `TraceContext` (the last two have real values available via OTel/ADR-0017 already, but wiring them in is a distinct later step, see §3), and the platform-wide `AiOsError` exception hierarchy error_handling_retry.md §8 describes (deliberately not built here — see §3 for why) — the `Retry-After` HTTP-date form is not parsed (numeric-seconds form only), and both budget ceilings are hardcoded constants, not persisted or configurable. `config/llm.yaml`'s checked-in `local_provider` section is commented out by default — no local model server is assumed running — so `kernel/bootstrap.py` registers only `anthropic` today even though the Router/dispatcher/`LocalAdapter`/circuit breaker/backoff policy/both budget enforcers are all real; genuine two-real-provider dispatch is proven by a dedicated local-HTTP-server test (`test_multi_provider_routing.py`), not (yet) by the real running Kernel. The one real, live-by-default fallback (`coding-strong`, anthropic-to-anthropic) is same-provider, not cross-provider — the cross-provider example is commented out for the identical reason `local_provider` is. `Tool.execute()` is still always called with `{}`, unconnected to the Gateway (tools have no analogous invocation-field passthrough — the Step Contract gives tool steps none). A real prompt can now genuinely be rendered, routed (through a genuinely multi-provider-capable Router and dispatcher that enforces two independent real budgets, retries only when appropriate, circuit-breaks only on real provider-health signals, and falls back on failure), completed by a real model, and recorded, through the real `AgentStepExecutor`/`AgentRegistry` path, and `kernel/bootstrap.py` genuinely does this at real app startup — every piece above is constructed there, reachable via `app.state.trigger_prompted_agent_workflow`, and also reachable through a real, authenticated `POST /api/v1/workflows` route (see §3 of earlier steps). |
| **Prompt Engine has a Protocol seam plus two implementations, but a real Prompt Registry, version resolution, and four other subsystems are unbuilt** | prompt_engine.md §5, Stage B | `PromptEngine`/`InMemoryPromptEngine`/`SqlPromptCatalog` exist (see §3 — the latter reads `catalog.prompts` by `(prompt_id, version)`, now the table's own composite primary key, see §3's most recent entry; both implementations share one `render_template()` routine), but version resolution, role/alias-based Prompt Resolver, prompt composition/inheritance, the cache-boundary split (ADR-0025), and an Observability writer are not. `PromptedCompletionService` (see §3) now calls `render()` from a standalone composition and feeds the result into the LLM Gateway. |
| **Context Manager now has a real Size & Token Budget Enforcer, its original one real resolver, and now a second real resolver reading a named prior step's own output — the "step-output-to-next-step-input" seam a real, four-step pipeline now genuinely uses; implementation_roadmap.md §3's own three-part Stage B deliverable ("deterministic assembly, budget enforcement, and trust tagging") is now fully met, though breadth (still two of six documented sources) is not** | context_manager.md, agent_architecture.md's Invocation Lifecycle, implementation_roadmap.md §3, workflow_architecture.md's Context Management section, see §3 | New `ai_os_kernel.context_manager` package: `ContextRequest`/`AssembledContext`/`ContextItem`/`SourceRef`/`SourceType` (a reduced slice of context_manager.md §5/§6), `ContextSourceResolver`/`WorkflowStateResolver` (reads a workflow instance's own declared `inputs`, also standing in for context_manager.md §3's "User-provided inputs") and now `WorkflowStepOutputResolver` (see §3 — a second resolver for the same "Workflow State" source, reading a *named prior step's* own persisted output via `WorkflowInstanceRepository.list_steps()`, configured with a flat `step_sources` mapping plus two small optional per-step knobs, `field_selectors`/`output_transforms`), and `ContextManager`/`DefaultContextManager` (queries every configured resolver, concatenates their items, then admits items by descending `relevance_score`). `AgentStepExecutor` optionally assembles context and adds it to an agent's `inputs["context"]`; `PromptedAgent` flattens it into one `context` prompt template variable; this pack's own `_extract_payload()` convention (Test/Documentation) parses it as JSON instead. `kernel/bootstrap.py` still wires only `WorkflowStateResolver` into its own demo workflow; `ai_os_pack_software_engineering.pipeline` is the first real caller combining both resolvers (via a small, pipeline-owned `_StepScopedResolver` wrapper — see §3 for the real, discovered overlap bug this combination required fixing). Still not built: Knowledge Manager/Memory Manager/AI Context Pack resolvers, a Runtime Configuration resolver, a Context Filter/Ranker as a first-class component, `required_context_types`, and a Context Audit Logger persistence writer (see §5). |
| **Multi-instance worker process framework** | Stage B/C | `WorkflowAdvanceRunner.run_to_completion()` loops `run_once()` to completion for **one** instance with a bounded iteration count and a structured, non-raising result; `WorkflowLeaseReaper.reap_once()` proactively reclaims expired leases in one bounded pass. Still missing: scheduling/polling across *many* instances (including how often to call the reaper), and any decision to call `WorkflowLeaseService.renew()` mid-step for a long-running step. |
| **Agent/tool invocation fields are documented, validated, recorded, dispatched by id, genuinely loaded from a real catalog-declared `entrypoint`, and now gated on a pack lifecycle with a real writer — but still no pack discovery, upgrades, health monitoring, or permissions** | workflow_architecture.md, kernel_architecture.md (Capability Manager), capability_manager.md, see §3 | `AgentRegistry`/`ToolRegistry` now have two implementations each: `InMemoryAgentRegistry`/`InMemoryToolRegistry` (a caller-supplied mapping, always `EchoAgent`/`EchoTool`, no pack to check) and `SqlAgentRegistry`/`SqlToolRegistry` (confirms the id is a real `catalog.agents`/`catalog.tools` row, confirms its declared `pack_id` names an `activated` `catalog.packs` row — `PackState`/`PackNotActivatedError` — then uses `EntrypointLoader` to actually import and construct that row's declared `entrypoint`, validating the result against the `Agent`/`Tool` Protocol and, for tools, checking `trust_tier` agreement). A real catalog row can now resolve to a genuinely different, real implementation, not always `EchoAgent`/`EchoTool`, and only if its pack is actually activated — and a pack can now genuinely *become* activated through `ai_os_kernel.capability_manager.SqlPackLifecycleRepository` (register/install, activate, deactivate, each recording a transition in `catalog.pack_state_transitions`, see §3), not only via a test hand-writing a row with raw SQL. Still missing: pack discovery (nothing scans a filesystem or registers what the Manifest Loader finds — `register()` must still be called directly), an upgrade path (registration is one-time; a new version of a known pack is explicitly rejected, not merged), health monitoring, permissions enforcement, sandboxing, and any network/marketplace/remote-download mechanism — an activated pack owning the declared id is still the only additional thing checked at resolution time. The Workflow Engine's step-execution machinery is constructed in `kernel/bootstrap.py` itself, with five authenticated HTTP routes reaching it (see §3 from prior steps); the pack lifecycle writer is now constructed there too (`app.state.pack_lifecycle_repository`, see §3), but it has no HTTP surface at all yet — no `/api/v1/packs/...` route. `SqlAgentRegistry`/`SqlToolRegistry` themselves are still not constructed in `kernel/bootstrap.py` (the demo agent is registered through `InMemoryAgentRegistry`, not the catalog-backed registry) — still only exercised by tests. `promptId`/`promptVersion`/`modelAlias` are genuinely consumed — `AgentStepExecutor` forwards them from a step's declaration into `inputs`, and `PromptedAgent` (real-composition-root-registered) reads them and calls the real Prompt Engine/LLM Gateway composition — but only for whichever agent the registry resolves; `SqlAgentRegistry`'s real entrypoint loading and `PromptedAgent`'s real constructor dependencies remain mutually exclusive by design, so this path today only works through `InMemoryAgentRegistry`. The rest of the Capability Manager (discovery, upgrades, permissions, sandboxing, health, and any HTTP surface) remains Stage C, a distinct, later, larger step. Decision branching and sub-workflow linkage remain genuinely undocumented — out of scope for the Step Contract, which only covers agent/tool invocation. |
| **Agent/Tool `input_schema` / real per-step inputs** | agent_architecture.md | Deliberately not implemented for either contract. The Context Manager now has a first real slice (see §3, and the row above) and does supply a real `AssembledContext` to `PromptedAgent`'s `inputs`, but this is context assembly, not per-step *schema-declared* input validation — no `input_schema` exists on either contract for anything to validate against yet. |
| **Application DB role with revoked UPDATE/DELETE on `workflow_events`** | data_model.md §4.2 | Deferred — see §3. Needs a real role/auth story, likely alongside secrets/OIDC work. |
| **Security Manager is a minimal slice, not the full design** | security_manager.md, authentication_authorization.md, ADR-0023 | `Principal`/`SecurityContext`/`permissions_for_roles()`/`JWTBearerTokenVerifier`/`require_permission()` exist (see §3), enforcing bearer-token authentication and four permissions (`workflow:start`, `workflow:read`, `pack:read`, `pack:manage`) across nine routes. Not yet built: full OIDC (JWKS, issuer/audience validation against a live identity provider), service-account API keys as a distinct mechanism from user bearer tokens, the full ADR-0023 monotonic-narrowing chain (principal ∩ workflow ∩ agent ∩ tool declared permissions — needs manifest-declared permissions, Capability Manager territory), the SDK's full closed permission vocabulary (only four of its many strings are modelled), role assignment/administration, a `governance.audit_log` writer for authentication/authorization events (logged via structlog only), rate limiting, and RFC 9457 `application/problem+json` error bodies (400/401/403/404/409/422/503 responses are FastAPI's default `{"detail": ...}` shape today). |
| **Almost no API surface** | — | Nine real, authenticated routes now exist (see §3): five workflow routes (`POST`/`GET /api/v1/workflows`, `GET /api/v1/workflows/{id}`/`.../steps`/`.../events`) and four pack routes (`POST /api/v1/packs`, `.../activate`, `.../deactivate`, `GET /api/v1/packs/{id}`) — `kernel/bootstrap.py`'s `app.state.trigger_prompted_agent_workflow`, `app.state.workflow_instance_repository`, and `app.state.pack_lifecycle_repository` are all reachable by an actual HTTP client, fronted by the minimal Security Manager slice. But the workflow write route is deliberately narrower than api_architecture.md §6.1's full contract (no `definition_id` selection, no `Idempotency-Key`, `200` instead of `202`), the workflow list route has no filtering, the `/steps`/`.../events` routes are not paginated at all, `POST /api/v1/packs` is not in §6.5's documented list at all (a reasoned addition, see §3), pack activate/deactivate return `200` not §6.5's documented `202`, there is no `GET /api/v1/packs` (list), and every other endpoint §6 documents (approvals, experiments, gates, usage, agents, config, traceability, `/stream`) still does not exist. The Security Manager itself is also minimal (see the row above) — so most future routes still need real design work, not just wiring. |
| **`approvals` has no writer, reader, or execution path** | data_model.md §4.5, human_approval_points.md | Table and migration exist (schema only). Nothing creates a `pending` row when a `human_approval` step is reached, nothing lets a human decide one, and `HumanApprovalPoint`-type steps still complete as a same-as-everything-else `NoOpStepExecutor` no-op. |

Nothing computes `row_hash`/`prev_hash`/a digest or writes a row; no component that would (Security Manager, Workflow Engine's Human Approval Point path, `secrets_manager`, Configuration Manager, ...) is built out far enough to yet; no scheduled chain-verification job exists (no worker process framework). |
| **`platform.event_outbox`/`idempotency_keys` have no writer or reader** | data_model.md §10, ADR-0012 | Both tables and migration exist (schema only). No Event Bus (in-process pub/sub or outbox relay) exists to write/dispatch outbox rows; no HTTP route exists yet that would need idempotent-replay. |
| **`trace.artifacts`/`trace.links` have no writer, reader, or impact-analysis query** | data_model.md §8 | Both tables and migration exist (schema only). No Traceability Engine exists yet to write a link or run the recursive-CTE impact analysis §8 describes. |
| **`knowledge.documents`/`chunks` now have a real minimal writer and a real minimal keyword-search reader; `embeddings`/`memory_items` still have none; no vector/hybrid search exists for any of the four** | data_model.md §7, ADR-0013, search_vector_search.md | All four tables and migration exist (see §3). `ai_os_kernel.persistence.knowledge_writer` (`KnowledgeWriter`/`SqlKnowledgeWriter`) persists a real document and its already-chunked content in one transaction, and `ai_os_kernel.persistence.knowledge_keyword_search` (`KeywordSearcher`/`SqlKeywordSearcher`, see §3) can now rank real chunks for a plain-text query via `plainto_tsquery`/`ts_rank` against the existing `content_tsv` GIN index — but the writer accepts already-chunked, already-hashed input only (nothing computes a content hash or performs chunking), and the reader is keyword-only with no metadata/`trust`/`project_id` filtering and no access control. No Retrieval Service, no vector or hybrid search, no RRF fusion, no chunking pipeline, no ingestion path (nothing fetches a `source_uri` or calls the writer for real yet), no `knowledge.embeddings` writer, no Knowledge Manager or Memory Manager (existing empty stub packages, deliberately untouched), no Context Manager resolver reading from any of these tables, no HTTP route exposing either the writer or the reader. `embeddings.embedding` is deliberately unconstrained (no fixed dimension) and its documented HNSW index is deliberately deferred, both pending a real embedding-model decision this codebase has not made (see §3/§5) — additive once that decision is real, not a redesign. |
| **`workflow_definitions`/`prompts`/`tools`/`agents`' own `pack_id` columns remain un-FK'd to `packs.pack_id`** | data_model.md §5 | Technically unblocked since `catalog.packs` was built, but out of scope for every approved step so far — a distinct, later step, not attempted here. |
| **`platform.schema_metadata`** | data_model.md §10 | Not created. §10 gives it no column list — only "Alembic revision plus `index_generation` counters for retrieval," and the plural "counters" is ambiguous about shape.
